# Computed route (Spec 48)

Measures a rendered design draft and writes block settings through the framework database, one surface at a time. **Solve** takes an existing layout tree, compares it with the draft through the parity walker, turns each difference into a setting write, rebuilds and repeats (at most three rounds). **Fill** fills a skeleton tree from the measured draft. Governing spec: `.claude/specs/48-CLONING-SYSTEM.md`.

**Never touches:** `plugins/sgs-blocks/scripts/` (nothing is imported from it), the framework DB's contents (opened read-only, never written), the canary's homepage, posts page or motion-QA fixtures, and any site's active snapshot pointer (R-47-1, R-47-2, R-47-11); the active header, footer and drawer pointers are set only by `site-build.mjs` and `site-run.mjs` on a client's own site (R-48-2). `lint.mjs` enforces the index below and the import ban.

**External imports, read-only:** `node:sqlite`; `plugins/sgs-blocks/src/blocks/*/block.json` (attribute schemas); `scripts/parity/lib/*.mjs`; `scripts/wp-build-page.js` and `scripts/parity/draft-live-walk.mjs`, run as commands.

**Tests:** `node --test "scripts/computed-route/tests/*.test.mjs"` (Node 24 runs a glob, not a bare folder). Each file names the rule it proves and has one case marked MUST FAIL.

## Files

| File | Job |
|---|---|
| `README.md` | This index. |
| `package.json` | The route's own third-party packages at exact versions (FR-48-75): `playwright`, `ajv`, `ajv-formats`, `saxes`, `@babel/parser`, `html-tag-names`, `html-element-attributes`, `mdn-data`, `@mdn/browser-compat-data`, `oembed-providers` and `aria-query` (the web platform's lists for the capability inventory's draft side), and `css-tree` (the CSS parser intake's usage read uses). Route code imports them from `scripts/computed-route/node_modules` only (`npm ci` here installs them). |
| `package-lock.json` | The lock file for `package.json`. |
| `delivery-map.mjs` | `node scripts/computed-route/delivery-map.mjs <client> --check` checks `sites/<client>/build/qa/delivery-map.json` (`lib/delivery-map.mjs::mapProblems`); `--status` prints the open keys per unit and the done and accepted counts. A done animation key needs live evidence (`lib/delivery-map.mjs::animationEvidence`), tree evidence counting only on the block that renders the record's own draft element (`lib/delivery-bind.mjs::boundNodes`): the effect and entrance or hover-state layers in the client's built trees (`sites/<client>/build/trees/**`), or a walker effect row (`kind: 'effect'`, live side read) in a report given with `--walk <report.json>`; a done key with neither is a problem. A done `state:<surface>:<name>` key also needs its commit to have changed that surface's build files (`lib/delivery-state-files.mjs::stateCommitEvidence`). |
| `capabilities.mjs` | Clone coverage plan Phase 1: `--write` regenerates `data/capabilities.json` (DB sections need the framework DB; without it they keep the committed entries), `--check` fails when it is out of date (skips without the DB), `--seed` adds an `unassigned` gap stub for every inventory ID with no coverage entry (never changes an existing one), `--gaps` prints the gap list as Markdown, `--like <id>` lists the covered entries solved the way <id> would be (same write reference, or same id prefix when it has none), `--drafts` lists the gaps named in `sites/*/build/intake/review.md` by wave with the draft folders that hit them, `--backfill-tests` sets `test` from the test file a covered note names and writes the rest to the baseline, `--gate` prints every coverage problem and exits 1 on any. |
| `state-map.mjs` | FR-48-13: prints a surface's generated state map and its diff against `state-map-reasons.json`; `--check` exits 1 when an `sc-if` is neither reached nor listed, or the diff holds a row (only the client's own conflict entries are judged, and a reasons file with an entry naming no client is refused). `--client`, `--surface`, `--records`, `--surfaces`, `--intake`, `--json`. `--check-walkers <client>` (`lib/walker-check.mjs`) exits 1 when a walker file a surface names does not exist or does not walk every state the surface maps; a state the generated config lists as `unwalked` is printed with its reason. |
| `head-check.mjs` | FR-48-84: `<client> [--repo <root>] [--url <page address> [--site-title <title>]]` reads every entry draft's head through the census (replaying intake's recorded network file) and checks the fonts the design system's `tokens/typography.css` declares (else the head's font loads) against `sites/<client>/theme-snapshot.json` (`lib/head-check.mjs::fontCheck`) and, given a page address, the live page's head (`headCheck`); prints each problem and every head item the route does not clone. Exit 0 pass, 1 problems, 2 usage. |
| `media-upload.mjs` | FR-48-58: `<client> --site <site> [--apply] [--repo <root>]` uploads every image under `sites/<client>/handoff/assets/` and `sites/<client>/assets/` that the site's media library does not hold live, through the WordPress REST media endpoint with the site's application password, and writes `sites/<client>/build/media-map.json`; without `--apply` it lists what it would upload. An SVG the upload sanitiser changed is printed for review. Exit 0 done, 1 failure, 2 usage. It also uploads the stock previews the draft paints (`lib/media-stock.mjs`, `stock: true`) and writes `sites/<client>/build/stock-review.md`, which also lists the photo IDs the draft holds but paints nowhere (never uploaded); a stock image that did not download exits 1 after the map is saved. A secrets file whose `WP_URL_*` is not https (plain http only to localhost) is refused. |
| `shop-parts.mjs` | FR-48-60, FR-48-61, FR-48-62: `<client> [--write]` reads the client's draft folder (`sites/<client>/handoff/surfaces.json` and the `.dc.html` files) and maps each part of its buy box, filter and basket drawer to its framework home (the `sgs-pdp-buybox` template part, `sgs/buybox` and `sgs/cart` settings, the filter blocks `archive-product.html` composes) or to a missing-features row, by what each part is in the draft's code (`lib/shop-parts.mjs::RULES`); a part only words name is a reported gap row. Prints the record, and `--write` saves `sites/<client>/build/shop-parts.json`. Every client draft with a `surfaces.json` is read as a fixture: a vocabulary entry that names a setting the framework DB lacks, a block its file lacks, a duplicate id, an unknown rule, or that matches no unit of any draft is refused. Exit 0 done, 1 refused, 2 usage. |
| `shop-fixtures.mjs` | Spec 48 section 16 "Minimum fixture products": `<client> --site <site> [--apply] [--repo <root>]` plans, then with `--apply` creates, a simple and a variable WooCommerce product (price, stock, image) through the REST API with the site's application password (a local mirror, which keeps none, has the same requests dispatched in-process through wp-cli), each found again on a rerun by its SKU and `_sgs_fixture` meta key, and reads each back. The variable product's Size is the global `pa_size` attribute (a product-local one is converted, its sizes kept) and each size term gets `_sgs_variesby_value` through wp-cli, only where it has none, so SGS's product preflight no longer reports `no_variesby`. The default is a dry run (it also lists each term's variesBy state); `--apply` is refused outside `mamas-test`, `eye-care-clone-test`, `local-mamas` and `local-eye-care`; a site without WooCommerce, or a site URL that is not https (plain http only to localhost), is refused; a media-map image id is checked on the site (`GET wp/v2/media/<id>`) before a product uses it. Exit 0 done, 1 failure, 2 usage. |
| `content-coverage.mjs` | The content coverage check: `[--cache <slot-map folder>] [--json]`. Every content setting of an SGS block (the framework DB's content-bearing roles, read-only) has one way a re-clone fills it from the draft (`measured`: a slot-map text, link or presence reading; `list`: the list writer; `role-writer`: the skeleton, media, icon or form writer; `no-element`: the DB says the page prints nothing for it) or a `content-setting:<block>.<setting>` gap row in `data/clone-coverage.json`. Each claim comes from the writer's own selection function. Prints every setting with no route and why each route missed it, every stale gap row (a writer now fills the setting) and every gap row naming a setting the DB no longer holds, then a per-route count; `--json` prints the same as JSON. Exit 0 none, 1 any, 2 no DB or cache folder. The cache is gitignored: pass `--cache` the folder of the checkout that calibrated. |
| `content-sync.mjs` | `<client> --from <mirror> --to <site> [--only a,b] [--apply]`: copies the built surfaces (`surfaces.json` `target.postId` posts and `target.template` copies) from a client's local mirror to the test site it mirrors (`mirrorOf`), by post id, over `wp eval-file` (SSH for a hosted site, no browser); refuses a source that is not that site's mirror, a post missing on either side or of another type on each (the ids diverged); `--apply` writes only what differs, then reads the target again and fails unless every one matches. The flow (Bean 2026-10-26, Hostinger's edge 403s after editor-load bursts): site-build on the test site, refresh the mirror from it (`scripts/local-wp/refresh-from-remote.sh`), manifest and `site-run.mjs` on the mirror, then this. |
| `manifest.mjs` | `<client> --site <site> [--apply] [--only <key,key,...>] [--repo <root>] [--db <file>]` (`--only` limits the run to those generated keys and an unknown key lists the valid ones; `--db` points at another framework database for the state vocabulary, read-only; keys in `surfaces.json` that the page map no longer has print as `stale` and are kept; a hand-set `draftUrl` stops `draftDir` and `draftIndex` being added; `handoff/MANIFEST.json` may name several `entry_drafts` (at least one), each surface's `draftIndex` being the one `lib/manifest-build.mjs::draftFor` picks; the generated `walker` and `walkerFull` are `qa/parity/<key>.fill-base.mjs` and `qa/parity/<key>.fill.mjs`, the two files Fill writes; none for the cart and checkout page entries, which the site run never walks, and a re-apply drops those fields there) writes `sites/<client>/build/surfaces.json` from the confirmed page map, the handoff surface records, intake and the calibration target. Without `--apply` it prints the entries and every row it could not settle and writes nothing. Exit 0 done, 1 a header, footer, drawer, mega menu, modal, form, post or page row has no unique handoff record, 2 usage; a row of a post type with no record kind is listed as `states-unmatched` and does not change the exit code. Each printed row names its target and the handoff record it matched. A form row matches the `form` record on its page (`lib/manifest-build.mjs::matchRecord`: the row's `route` against the record's `page_key`, else its slug without `form-` against the `page_key` of a form inline in the entry draft or the record's `dc` name without `-form`, never the title) and its entry carries `draftRoute`, the page's route the draft is read on. The page map's `post` rows are surfaces too: each matches its page record by route and is marked `contentOnly` (only its `post_content` is cloned), and the first post row also gives one `single` surface, `{ template: 'single' }`, the site's own copy of the theme's single-post template (`fromPost` names the post it is shaped from; a page-map row keyed `single` is a hard error). A `shop`, `product` or `product_cat` row is a surface whose `target` is `{ template: <the template its tree builds> }`, with or without an id, and its handoff record is its route's page record (`lib/manifest-build.mjs::KINDS`). |
| `site-build.mjs` | FR-48-55: `<client> --site <site> [--apply] [--repo <root>]` builds every row of the confirmed `sites/<client>/build/page-map.json` on the site once: each post through `scripts/wp-build-page.js` (its stage 3 tree at `sites/<client>/build/trees/<postType>/<slug>.json`, else a one-block placeholder), then its parent and page template, each mega-menu panel's section page (the page row on its menu slot, Spec 36 FR-36-5), the classic menus with mega-menu items and their theme locations, the front and posts page, `/%postname%/` permalinks and the active header, drawer and footer (`wp sgs <area> set-active`). Without `--apply` it prints the plan and a before-and-after table of every option it would change; with `--apply` it runs the posts, menus, layout and site phases with a fresh site read before each, writes each new id into its row at once, and fails if a final read still plans anything. It changes only what it made: a stored id must name a post carrying that row's key, a reused menu must carry the site build's mark, an item no row asks for is reported and left, and an unmarked post already holding a row's type and slug stops the run (as do a stored id the site no longer holds and a shop row), all before any write. Exit 0 done, 1 failure or stop, 2 usage. A shop client's rows (FR-48-55 parts 1 and 6, FR-48-56): a `shop` row is a `page` (its id is `woocommerce_shop_page_id`) whose tree file `trees/shop/<slug>.json` builds the site's own `archive-product` template, a `product` row builds `single-product` and a `product_cat` row `archive-product`, each with `wp-build-page.js --template` (a row's own `template`, else its type's default; a template two rows name is built from the one with a tree file, else the first; the first `post` row also names the shared `single` template, built from its `trees/post/<slug>.json` through `wp-build-page.js --template single` like a shop template) and makes no post of its own, so its menu entries add no item and its id stays empty; a template is built only from a real tree file (a placeholder leaves the theme's own default and says so), its tree hash is kept in the `sgs_site_build_templates` option and its saved content's hash in `sgs_site_build_template_content`, a copy is rebuilt only when its tree changed and stops the run when the saved copy differs from what the build saved, and a saved copy the build did not make stops the run; a page's saved content hash is kept in post meta `_sgs_site_build_content` and a rebuild of an edited page stops the run, while a page's title, slug and parent are set once at creation and a person's change is reported, never undone. The four `woocommerce_*_page_id` options take the shop row, the page row whose template is `cart`, the one whose template is `checkout`, and the page row slugged `my-account` or `account`, in the option table; a page WooCommerce's installer made and a page option names is taken over and marked only while its content is still WooCommerce's default, any other holder of a row's slug still stops the run, two rows that could take one role stop the run, and a replaced default page is noted. A page map holds one header, footer and nav drawer row (FR-48-30), so two rows of one layout kind stop the run naming both, and each active pointer goes to its row's post whichever post it names now, a hand-made layout included (Bean 2026-10-27: the clone owns every site-wide setting it builds), the plan line showing `active <area>: <old> -> <new>`. Two rows making one WordPress type and slug stop the run. The site the page map's ids were written for is recorded in `sites/<client>/build/page-map.site`; a run against another site that shares the client plans without those ids and its write-back replaces them. The front page is the page row with route, else slug, `home`. Tests: `tests/site-build-shop.test.mjs`, `tests/site-build-review.test.mjs` (the layout refusal and the pointer move: `tests/fixtures/intake-pagemap/site-build-mutations.json`, 5 of 5 mutations red). | `<client> --promote-options <Fill run folder or site-options.json>` merges a Fill run's site options into `sites/<client>/build/site-options.json`; the site phase then sets them (`lib/site-build-options.mjs`). `<client> --site <site> --flow-links [--apply]` runs only the product flow links (`lib/site-build-flow-links.mjs`, Spec 43 FR-43-25), which a whole build also runs last: every published product a `live` page-map product row with `opensFlow` covers gets that flow's slug as `_sgs_choice_flow` (one `wp eval-file` read and, with `--apply`, one write with read-back); a product linking another flow and the calibration reference product (`_sgs_cr_fixture`) are noted and left; a flow the site lacks, a flow slug no row holds or two live rows opening different flows stop it before any write; it never writes the page map. `<client> --site <site> --catalogue [--apply]` runs only the draft catalogue (FR-48-92, `lib/site-build-catalogue.mjs`): every product of `sites/<client>/build/catalogue.json` planned by SKU, draft id or slug (create, complete, exists, or left when it is a fixture or calibration product), made or completed with `--apply` (a new product published last, an existing one only filled in), read back to a plan of 0, and its product id and permalink recorded in `catalogue.json`'s `sites.<site>`; each facet (a filter the draft's shop offers) is planned as a global attribute `pa_<slug>` to create or complete with its terms and swatches, each product lacking its terms is completed with a filter-only product attribute, and each attribute id is recorded in `sites.<site>.attributes` (`lib/site-build-catalogue-facets.mjs`); the page map is neither read nor written. A shop client's whole build also switches WooCommerce's coming-soon mode off: a site reading `woocommerce_coming_soon` = `yes` (a new WooCommerce install's default, which serves every visitor a holding page) is planned to `no` in the option table and written in the site phase (`woocommerce_store_pages_only` is never written); `<client> --site <site> --store-visible [--apply]` runs only that switch (`lib/site-build-store-visible.mjs`): one read, with `--apply` one write and a read-back that must say `no`, nothing for a non-shop client or a site already visible, and the page map is read only to tell a shop client, never written. `<client> --site <site> --menus [--apply]` runs only the menus and their mega bindings on a site that is already built (`lib/site-build-menus-step.mjs`): each mega panel's section page, the menu, item and theme-location steps, and the removal of an item of a site-build menu that opens a page now serving as a mega item's section page on that menu; it makes no post and never writes the page map (a menu entry naming a post with no id on the site stops it), and `--apply` reads the site again, fails unless nothing is left to do, and records the menu ids in `menu-map.json`.
| `site-run.mjs` | Phase 2 site run (exports `main(argv)`): `<client> --site <target> [--only a,b] [--stages inventory,propose,fill,build,walk] [--jobs 3] [--widths 1440,768,375] [--live-base <url>] [--with-content]` (`--live-base` names the live site address in place of the surface's env file; `--with-content` reads the `contentOnly` post surfaces, which are skipped without it) runs every `surfaces.json` entry through the skeleton inventory (a surface the intake record marks state-driven (`root.viaState`) takes the inventory intake saved with the state open, rewritten into the served key space (`lib/skeleton-saved-inventory.mjs::servedInventoryOf`); a form surface takes the same saved inventory through `lib/skeleton-saved-inventory.mjs::savedInventoryOf`; any other surface is read live at the tag of the record's root element, at its route; the route and live page come from the exported `surfaceReach`: the entry's `draftRoute` (a form's page, a post's route) first, a form's live address the page row carrying that route, a post surface and the `single` template surface (`fromPost`) the post row's permalink), `skeleton.mjs::proposeSurface`, `fill.mjs` (`--draft-url` the served draft and route; a state that never rendered fails the stage as an opener failure, `--live-url` the page's permalink), the `wp-build-page.js` dry run then build (one at a time; `--template single` for the template surface, `--post-id` for the rest; a built header, footer or drawer surface, one whose `target.postId` is a page-map layout row, then becomes its area's active layout through `wp sgs <area> set-active`, read back and logged as `active <area>: <old> -> <new>`, whichever post the pointer named, `lib/site-run-layout.mjs`; a run whose `--only` leaves it out changes no pointer) and the parity walker (`--headless --no-review`, `SGS_DRAFT_URL` the served route), copying Fill's walker configs into `build/qa/parity/`. Serves the handoff folder once (`lib/draft.mjs::serveDraft`); a failed stage is recorded and the run goes on. Writes `build/qa/site-run/<stamp>/status.json` and one log per surface and stage. `surfaceReach` takes `routing` (intake.json `routing`): a state-page draft's page, form or post surface is reached at its state fragment (`#@state/page=<route>`, `lib/state-route.mjs`), which the inventory read, Fill's `--draft-url` and the walker's `SGS_DRAFT_URL` all set on the loaded draft, in place of a hash a state-page draft ignores; hash and files drafts keep their hash. `liveAddress(row, rows, shown)` returns the live address of a row (a post's permalink, or where WordPress shows a `template` row's template), or null; `needsTestOrder(surfaces, keys)` tells whether the run reads the order confirmation, which shows only at an order's received page; `orderAddress(reply)` returns the path and query of the received page a test-order reply names, or null; `runKeys(surfaces, only)` returns the surfaces a run covers: the named ones plus each template copy a named page sits in and, with the page copy, the copies built from its shell; `copyTrees({ surfaces, keys, filled, buildDir })` returns `{ slug: { tree, shell } }` for every template surface in `keys` from the pass's Filled trees. |
| `data/capabilities.json` | The capability inventory: sections of stable IDs, each with its status (`ok` or `absent`) and, where its data package says so, `deprecated: true`. Framework: `block-setting`, `fx`, `motion-preset` from the DB; `sgs-support`, `extension`, `template`, `part`, `pattern`, `post-type`, `plugin`, `snapshot-key` from the code. Draft: `html`, `html-attr`, `input-type`, `svg`, `embed`, `css-at`, `css-media-feature`, `css-group`, `css-property` (vendor and legacy properties mdn-data's groups do not hold), `css-pseudo-class`, `css-pseudo-element`, `css-function`, `css-unit`, `aria-role`, `aria-attr` from the web platform packages, `construct` from the construct registry. Generated; never hand-edited. |
| `data/shop-part-vocab.json` | FR-48-60 to FR-48-62: the shop-part vocabulary: how a draft's buy box, filter and basket drawer units map to template parts, WooCommerce filter blocks, block settings (resolved against the framework DB, never named as attributes in code) or a missing-features row. |
| `data/photo-to-come.png` | FR-48-92: the "photo to come" image (1200 x 1200 PNG) the catalogue step gives a product that would otherwise have no image, the same for every client (`lib/site-build-catalogue.mjs::PHOTO_TO_COME_FILE`; kept in git by its own `.gitignore` exception). |
| `data/clone-coverage.json` | The clone coverage map: per capability ID its `recognise`, `write` and `check` references and, once covered, the `test` that proves it (a repo path to an existing test file with an optional `#name`; nothing under `sites/`) (`construct:<kind>` or a repo path with `#symbol`), a gap's `wave`, or Bean's signed `excluded`. |
| `data/coverage-test-baseline.json` | The covered entries that name no test yet: a sorted id list that may only shrink (the gate refuses a covered entry with no test that is not listed, and a listed id that is now tested or no longer covered). |
| `data/item-roles.json`, `data/item-roles.schema.json` | FR-48-82: the roles a list item's key or a draft item field can play (image, icon, organisation, role, date, quote, number, author, title, description, link), each with the name words that signal it, in priority order. A word may sit under two roles (`name`: author, title; `text`: quote, description); `lib/fill-list-content.mjs::matchFields` resolves it by the block's item keys. `loadItemRoles` validates the file against the schema and refuses a role named twice. |
| `data/mutation-list-stale-baseline.json` | The mutation-list entries already stale when the static guard was built: `{ "<list>": [ find, ... ] }`, a list that may only shrink (`tests/mutation-lists.test.mjs` refuses a stale entry it does not name and a named entry that is no longer stale). Each entry is a mutation `tools/mutate.mjs` skips, so the test it names is unproven until its find is re-aimed at the current code and the line removed (`node tools/check-mutation-lists.mjs --write-baseline` after a sweep). |
| `lib/delivery-map.mjs` | A client's delivery map (a delivery plan's tracking file): `mapPath`, `expectedKeys` (every coverage id the intake review names, active animation record and surface state), `planUnits` (the plan's work-table unit ids), `duplicateKeys`, `commitExists`, `PRIOR` and `mapProblems` (missing, duplicated or unknown keys, units the plan lacks, done commits that do not exist, marks that disagree with the coverage map, done animation keys with no evidence), `treeFacts`, `clientTrees`, `animationNeeds` (what a record needs on the page, from `lib/motion-behaviours.mjs::recogniseBehaviour` and `lib/motion-layers.mjs::classify`), `animationEvidence` and `walkerEffectRows`. `stateEvidence(key, reports)` gives `{ ok, why }` for a `state:<surface>:<name>` done mark: a walker run of that state exists in the reports given and no open row is left in any run of it. Tree evidence for an animation record is bound to the block that renders the record's own draft element (`lib/delivery-bind.mjs`), and a done state key's commit must touch the surface's files (`lib/delivery-state-files.mjs`). |
| `lib/coverage-register.mjs` | The coverage map as a register of solved gaps: `likeEntries` (worked examples), `reviewCoverageIds`, `readReviews` and `draftGaps` (open gaps per draft), `backfillTests` (the one-off test backfill). |
| `data/clone-coverage.schema.json` | The coverage map's schema. |
| `lib/content-coverage.mjs` | The routes behind `content-coverage.mjs`: which writer claims each content setting, the emit_shape reading of no-element, and the stale and unknown gap row checks. |
| `lib/capabilities.mjs` | Builds the capability inventory from its sources, never a hand-written list (FP-1). |
| `lib/clone-coverage.mjs` | Loads the coverage map and gates it against the inventory; `loadBaseline` reads the test baseline (`BASELINE_FILE`) and `testProblem` checks a `test` reference. |
| `tools/presentational-hints.mjs` | Derives `data/presentational-hints.json` from a saved copy of the WHATWG HTML Rendering section: every attribute the browser turns into CSS (its presentational hints and its style sheet's attribute rules), with the properties; `node scripts/computed-route/tools/presentational-hints.mjs <rendering.html> [--write]`. |
| `data/census-classes.json` | The ordered rules (pattern, class, owning Spec 48 item) that name the cause of every Fill UNMAPPED and already-held row; read by `lib/fill-census.mjs`. |
| `data/effect-layers.json` | The layer shape each SGS pointer effect paints, as the parity walker names effect layers (`sheen` a band, `ripple` a circle, `curtain` a cover); `lib/delivery-map.mjs` reads it to tell whether a walker report shows a record's effect playing or missing on the live page. |
| `data/census-mechanisms.json` | The walker and Fill vocabulary `lib/census-mechanism.mjs` reads a row's signature from: the interaction kinds, the element-less key kinds, Fill's state wording, the inventory-ID pattern, the Fill side names, the walker-only and Fill-wrote reason classes, the transform longhands the browser composes into one matrix, the `unregistered:` block-family prefix and the `(no block read)` family, and the pattern that reads a state-setting `uncalibrated` reason's block, settings and state (`lib/census-gap-class.mjs::calibrationEvidence`). |
| `data/presentational-hints.json` | `{ source, read, entries: [ { attribute, element, css } ] }`: what each presentational attribute renders as (`td` `align` → `text-align`). The inventory lists these attributes, intake's usage read counts their CSS, and the coverage map covers them by the properties Fill reads instead of excluding them as deprecated. Generated; never hand-edited. |
| `tests/presentational-hints.test.mjs` | The extractor over a spec fixture in the spec's own markup (each form a hint is written in), and the committed data against the inventory and mdn-data; MUST FAIL: a condition attribute, a quoted value, a quirks note or a heading is never a hint, and no attribute the spec renders is excluded as dead. |
| `lib/intake-usage.mjs` | Clone coverage step 1.6: what a draft uses, as capability IDs. The file read (the method) reads the census's stamped template, `<style>` bodies, linked stylesheets, the CSS, markup and elements its logic and helmet scripts write (syntax trees), and the SVG and CSS assets its files name; the runtime read (the check) reads what the running page shows that the draft owns, and every capability only it showed is reported. |
| `tests/intake-usage.test.mjs` | Step 1.6's usage read: each mapper (CSS, elements, the runtime's encodings, code strings and calls, bound values, assets), the runtime check in a real browser (an @import sheet, a script-set inline style, a shadow root, the runtime shell and markers excluded), and the done-when row for each listed capability; MUST FAIL: prose never read as CSS, an unknown property kept as itself, an asset the draft never names not read, the runtime's own shell and markers not counted. |
| `tests/capabilities.test.mjs` | Phase 1 steps 1.1 and 1.2: sections derived from a fixture DB and the code; MUST FAIL: a planted block setting and a planted draft tag each become a new entry. |
| `tests/delivery-map.test.mjs` | The delivery map check: the expected keys (each review coverage id once, active animation records only, every surface state), a positive control, and MUST FAIL cases for a missing, invented, dead-code or repeated key, a unit the plan lacks, a done commit that does not exist, an empty accepted reason, a value both done and accepted or of the wrong shape, coverage marks that disagree with the coverage map (an accepted id must be excluded there); the duplicate scan ignores names inside values; plan units come from the work table only; repeated state names get their own keys (15 of 15 mutations killed with `tools/mutate.mjs`); done animation keys need evidence: an element lacking the effect layer fails and the same with the layer passes, a walker effect row with its live side read evidences, a reduced-motion rule is native, an entrance or hover-state record needs its layer, an unnamed loop needs a walker row (`tests/fixtures/delivery-map-evidence/mutations.json`, 13 of 13 killed). |
| `tests/census-all.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/census-all/mutations.json`, `tests/fixtures/census-all/root/sites/alpha/build/qa/parity/out/header/report.json`, `tests/fixtures/census-all/root/sites/alpha/build/qa/fill/header/2026-10-23T04-45-48/unmapped.json`, `tests/fixtures/census-all/root/sites/alpha/build/qa/fill/header/2026-10-23T04-45-48/fill-report.json`, `tests/fixtures/census-all/root/sites/alpha/build/qa/fill/header/2026-10-23T04-45-48/filled.tree.json` | The mechanism census: a real header walk and Fill run (client name removed) classify with 0 unclassified and group by the known mechanisms; MUST FAIL: a row with no signature (unnamed Fill reason, joined or not, empty key) and `--check` exiting 1, a slug the DB lacks as its own `unregistered:` family and a slugless row as `(no block read)`, a state-setting `uncalibrated` reason read in its state, a nested pair's repeated row counted once, two clients' identical-mechanism rows in one group; run-state collapse, sides, written rows, property families and tier patterns (18 of 18 mutations killed with `tools/mutate.mjs`; part and effect rows group by the property family, pointer state and missing side, never the client's part label). |
| `tests/content-coverage.test.mjs`, `tests/content-coverage.mutations.json` | The content coverage check: a negative control (every setting routed), a positive case for each route, MUST FAIL cases for a setting with no route (uncalibrated, a second text setting, a misclassified-looking role), a stale gap row, a gap row for a setting the DB lacks or that is not content, a malformed ID and a gap row that claims a writer; each writer's real output held inside what the check claims for it. The mutation list changes the check, the claims and the register rules one at a time. |
| `tests/content-coverage-writers.test.mjs`, `tests/content-coverage-writers.mutations.json` | The reverse of the content coverage drift tests: every setting the form, icon and skeleton writers' claim lists name (`FORM_FIELD_SETTINGS`, `FORM_BLOCK_SETTINGS`, `ICON_SETTINGS`, `skeletonSettings`) is really written by the writer for some realistic input, so a claim the writer does not honour cannot make the check say covered. The mutation list deletes one write at a time. |
| `tests/clone-coverage.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/declared-writes/mutations.json` | The gate over the committed inventory and map; MUST FAIL controls 1 to 4: a planted setting, a planted tag, a missing writer or reader, an unsigned exclusion, a wave-less gap, an unknown ID; control 2b: an ID a module declares in `COVERAGE_WRITES` whose entry is a gap, missing or names another writer, and every declared ID is one the inventory holds; control 2c: an entry whose write names a lib module that does not declare it; and a gap carrying a Phase 0 marker, which the schema refuses. `mutations.json` is the mutation list for `tools/mutate.mjs` (all red). |
| `tests/intake-coverage-rows.test.mjs` | `lib/intake-missing.mjs` rule 3: one seen-but-unsupported row per uncovered capability; MUST FAIL: an uncovered element is never dropped. |
| `tests/fixtures/framework-db.mjs` | A temp framework DB built from `plugins/sgs-blocks/scripts/dbschema/schema.sql` for tests that cannot use the real one. |
| `tests/fixtures/motion-loop-css/records.json`, `tests/fixtures/motion-loop-css/A-02.expected.css` | Two minimal endless-loop inventory records, and the box content proven against the draft's own Web Animations call for Mama's A-02 (`.claude/qa/control-gaps/sparkle/compare-twinkle.mjs`). |
| `lib/motion-validate.mjs` | Spec 38A FR-38A-5 and FR-38A-2: the cloning writer's gate for an `sgsMotion` document, run before any write: the shared schema check (`lib/motion-schema.mjs`), then the custom-target rules mirroring `Sgs_Motion_Custom_Target` (a review row on every `custom:<n>` target; one selector that stays inside the block root). |
| `tests/motion-validate.test.mjs` | Spec 38A FR-38A-5 and FR-38A-2: a custom target with its review row and scoped selectors (a leading child combinator included) pass; refused: a custom target with no or a blank review row, a planted unknown key, an out-of-range value, selectors that could leave the block root or break the style block, and any character outside the allow-list (it fails closed). |
| `tests/wp-build-page-inserter-defaults.test.mjs` | Spec 38A (Bean, 2026-10-15): a block's default entrance lives in the inserter-default channels, never block.json, so `scripts/wp-build-page.js` builds a no-motion tree with no `sgsMotion` while the inserter's default variation in the same editor carries the entrance (the control). Live: needs `.claude/secrets/sandybrown.env` and the canary's serial-queue slot; skipped without them. |
| `tests/fixtures/bg-zoom-helper.php.txt` | A background-zoom helper's PHP source, kept as a fixture since M6.6-G1 deleted the real helper, so `tests/triage.test.mjs` (R1) has a real helper whose emitted selector it reads. |
| `lint.mjs` | The route's gate: README index, plugins/sgs-blocks/scripts import ban, `plugins/sgs-blocks/node_modules` ban (FR-48-75), no core `style` or `native_wp` writes in a `--tree`, no style values in a `--skeleton`, every printed post owned by a surface in a `--surfaces` manifest, every divergence-ledger entry beside that manifest citing register items its register holds (`--register`, else the `register` of `qa/ledger.config.json` beside the ledger; B4), client names. |
| `calibrate.mjs` | Calibration command: refuses on a deploy mismatch, resolves each fixture's site references from the target's `refs` and checks each referenced object exists on the site (a block with an unsupplied or missing reference is reported and not built), builds each block's markers on the calibration page (a marker with `base` beside a baseline instance carrying those attributes, in every chunk), reads them at 375/768/1440 (hover under a real mouse and focus by keyboard on the styled element, its panel opened when hidden; a shrunk marker with its ancestor class; scrolled markers with the window scrolled, against a scrolled default), writes two files, each only when due (`cache/<block>.json`, the site-independent slot map: kept when its key is current or another site measured it, unless `--recalibrate`; `cache/<site>/<block>.paint.json`, that site's default paint and each paint state's (`paintStates`, one default instance per state read for its paint only): kept only when its own file carries the current `paintKey`, which `paintKeyOf` builds from the build's md5, the snapshot's and, where the block has them, its paint states; a state that names `props` keeps only those properties through `statePaint`, and a declared state the run did not measure, `missingPaintStates`, leaves the paint unwritten and the block in error, so the next run measures it again), empties the page. |
| `solve.mjs` | Solve command: refs, then up to three build, walk and write rounds, a final build and walk, classification and the solve report. Each `surfaces.json` entry must carry `states` (walker state → setting state; unmapped states are reported, never written) and may carry `walkStates` (passed to the walker as `--states`) and `provides` (the linked blocks and template parts whose post it is, `"<block>:<value>"`). A linked placeholder is never written. |
| `fill.mjs` | Fill command (FR-47-4): a skeleton tree plus a draft (a hosted url or a local folder) to a tree resolved root-down in one pass, before anything is built. Needs `--client`, `--surface`, `--skeleton`, `--live-url` and one of `--draft-url` / `--draft-dir`; `--serve <folder>` serves a draft until Ctrl-C. Reads `surfaces.json`, the snapshot and the ledger; writes only into `--out`: `filled.tree.json`, `fill-report.md`/`.json`, `unmapped.json`, `handover.json`, `breakpoints.json`, `opener.json` (the state a drawer or panel surface was opened with, the widths it rendered at and any state refused: lib/fill-open.mjs), the generated walker config and (when the draft's focus, press or link-state looks live in the theme) `theme-snapshot.patch.json`, plus `<build>/<surface>.origin.json` (cr-ref to `tpl` key and fingerprint) when the skeleton names its draft elements by `tpl` finder. The draft url Fill reads is `lib/fill-open.mjs::readUrlOf`: a surface of a state-page export is read at its route's state fragment (with or without an opener), every other draft at the url it was given. |
| `form-submit-check.mjs` | FR-48-57 / FR-48-83 §10.1: `<client> --site <target> --form <slug> [--repo <root>]`. Loads the page carrying a built `sgs_form` post on a test site with Playwright, fills every required field with a valid test value (walking the steps), submits, and checks the success state against `successMessage` / `successRedirect`, one stored row in `sgs_form_submissions`, and the owner email's original recipient in the FluentSMTP log against `Sgs_Mailer::owner_recipient`; then an empty required field must show its `msgRequired` (or an error line) and store and send nothing. Reads the site over SSH `wp` (host and port from the target's `ssh_host` / `ssh_port` in `deploy_common.py::TARGETS`, else its shared `SSH_USER_HOST` and `SSH_FALLBACK` port; `BatchMode=yes`, `ConnectTimeout=20`); a `--form` that sanitising would change or empty exits 2; a placeholder post with no fields is reported as a Phase 2 dependency, exit 0. |
| `site-check.mjs` | Spec 48 §3.7: `<client> --site <site> [--only pages,menu,mega,drawer,modals,forms,flows,woocommerce] [--out <dir>] [--with-cart] [--repo <root>]`. The functional check of a clone, in one headless Chromium on page views only: every page-map page (permalink from the public REST API; a product or category row through one Store API example) loads 200 with a non-empty `main`; every header link reaches a page-map page, matched by the row's own permalink whatever the page redirects to (an empty basket's checkout loads the cart), and no checked page links to a `.dc.html` draft file, and every page-map header row has a link (a page-map `sgs_mega_menu` row, which has no address, has the header mega trigger whose panel id ends `-mega-<post id>`, found by the mega check's own `markMegaInPage`); each `[data-sgs-mega-trigger]` opens its panel (hover, then focus and ArrowDown, then a click) and closes; the burger opens the drawer at 375 and 768 and its close button, Escape or moving away closes it; every `sgs/modal` and `sgs/choice-flow` the pages render opens and closes (a flow advances a step and Back returns), and a page-map `sgs_choice_flow` or `sgs_form` no page renders fails; every form submits once with a test entry marked "SGS site-check test" in every free-text field, the honeypot left empty (`lib/form-submit-page.mjs`: the form's own Next, a visually hidden tile group ticked through its labels, a step its Next never leaves stopped as stuck with the step and the controls it cannot fill named; the success verdict of `lib/form-submit-run.mjs`; exactly one stored row counted read-only through `lib/calibration-refs.mjs::wpCommand`, or, for a form whose wrapper carries `data-store-submissions="false"`, the success state and a 200 from the submit endpoint with nothing stored; a visible Next or Continue button that is not the form's own control named in the detail; each form's screenshots named by its formId); the client's flows run through `scripts/parity/flows/run-all.mjs`'s `FLOWS` and `lib/flow.mjs::execute` (n-a for a client they do not belong to); cart, checkout and my-account show WooCommerce's working block or form (an empty basket's checkout is n-a unless `--with-cart` adds one line through the Store API). Nothing is forced open, so a trigger whose handler never runs fails. Before the checks the home page is loaded as a visitor: when WooCommerce's coming-soon holding page stands in place of the site (`lib/site-check-coming-soon.mjs`: the `woocommerce/coming-soon` block's root directly under `.wp-site-blocks` or the body), the run reports one failure, "the site is in WooCommerce coming-soon mode: visitors see a holding page (woocommerce_coming_soon=yes)", with the fix command `site-build.mjs <client> --site <site> --store-visible --apply`, and every other check it runs is n-a with that reason. Writes `sites/<client>/build/qa/site-check/<stamp>/result.json`, `result.md` and the screenshots. Exit 0 all pass or n-a, 1 a check failed, 2 usage or the site unreachable. |
| `sweep.mjs` | Sweep command: `--surfaces <surfaces.json> [--date YYYY-MM-DD] [--out <file>]`; reads each surface's newest `solve-report.json` and writes `<build>/qa/sweep/<date>/sweep.json`: one row per distinct open issue across the site `{ surface, ref, pair, path, block, property, widths, state, kind, class, reason, values, report }` (`pair` names a block-less hand-pair row; `values` holds each distinct draft and live pair, at most 6), totals per surface and class, and the surfaces with no report. |
| `register-sweep.mjs` | Register sweep command (A4): `bundle --register <md> --sweep <json> --pairs <qa/pairs dir> --out <folder>` writes one input file per A4 group (items, the sweep rows and measured refs of their surfaces, the status rules; agent brief `sites/eye-care-ward-end/build/qa/sweep/2026-10-05/a4/agent-brief.md`) and exits 1 on an item in no group; `merge … --verdicts <json> … --out <file>` checks the agents' verdicts and writes a copy of the register with a Sweep column (never in place). |
| `triage.mjs` | Triage command (B1): `--client <slug> --surface <s> [--report <solve-report.json>] [--out <file>]`; reads the surface's newest (or given) Solve report and its final walk (highest `round-N/report.json`), every surface tree, the DB read-only, calibration, the extension roster, block sources and every PHP file under the plugin's `includes/`; writes `<build>/qa/triage/<surface>.json` (a candidate class W, F, T or U with evidence per distinct issue) and prints counts by class. |
| `gate-chain.mjs` | Gate chain command (FR-48-64): `<solve-report.json> [--client <slug>] [--probes <module.mjs>] [--out <file>] [--gaps-out <file>] [--debt-out <file>]`; runs G0 to G7 (`lib/gate-chain.mjs`) on every distinct open issue of a Solve report, prints each row's first failing gate, class and evidence, and writes `gate-chain.json` beside the report and, with `--client`, `sites/<client>/build/qa/gaps.json` (`lib/gaps-file.mjs`, one row per issue from `lib/gap-input.mjs::gapInput`; `--gaps-out` overrides the path) and `sites/<client>/build/qa/instrument-debt.json` (FR-48-67, `lib/instrument-debt.mjs`: every I-class row grouped by owner plus the known items; `--debt-out` overrides the path). Every gate's evidence is structured, a command and its output line or a file and a key (`lib/gate-evidence.mjs`), never free text. The block of a row comes from `--client`'s surface tree; the live gates G5 to G7 run `lib/gate-probes.mjs` by default (the surface's live origin, a fresh walk of the draft served from its `draftDir`, the block renderer behind the surface's admin login), or a `--probes` module (default export `{ measure(row, setting), probe(row, kind), recheck(row) }`) in its place. `defaultProbes({ s, client, db, surface })` builds the default; its walk is lint-gated (`lib/walk-lint-gate.mjs`): a walker config that fails `lib/walker-lint.mjs` is never walked, and every row a walk would judge (G6's flow probe, G7) is W-walker with the lint fault as its structured evidence. Exports `finalWalk(dir)` (the highest round-N/report.json beside a report) and `buildContext({...})` (the gate inputs read from the report, tree, DB, cache and handoff files; G2's declared value is read at the row's own state and width and its FR-48-34 defect joined through the row's draft identity, `lib/draft-identity.mjs`; `draftIdOf(row)` gives that identity, or null). Reads the DB read-only. |
| `pairs.mjs` | Block pairing command: pairs every block of a surface with its draft element through the walker's word matcher, re-checks each kept finder at 375 and 768, and writes `<surface>.full.mjs` (the hand config plus one pair per block) and `qa/pairs/<surface>.json` (kept pairs and every left-out block with its reason). A panel surface pairs with its walker state open on both sides (`--state <name> --width <w> [--recheck <widths>|none]`), recorded in the report. A surface's `walkerFull` in `surfaces.json` makes Solve walk it. With `<surface>.origin.json` (`origin.mjs`), each block in it is paired by exact draft identity (a `tpl` finder) and the word pairing is cross-checked against it (`identityPass`): a replaced generated pair is recorded in `replaced`, a hand pair that disagrees in `mispaired`. |
| `origin.mjs` | Origin command (route-accuracy R4): `--client <slug> --surface <s> [--skeleton f] [--tree f]`; aligns the skeleton writer's skeleton with the surface's committed tree by block names, level by level, and writes `<build>/<surface>.origin.json` (cr-ref to tpl key and fingerprint); prints every block left without an identity and why. |
| `trial.mjs` | Try-before-write replay (route-accuracy R6): `--client <slug> --surface <s> --run <solve run dir> [--round 1] [--site <calibration target>] [--widths 375,768,1440] [--out <dir>]`; tries each write of one round of a stored Solve run through `lib/trial-run.mjs::openTrial` (the step Solve itself runs before each rebuild) and writes `trial.json`, `trial.md` and `writes-kept.json` (every write but the rejected ones, in solve-report form, for `answer-sheet.mjs --solve`). Changes nothing on any site. |
| `skeleton.mjs` | Skeleton writer command (Spec 48 §3.4): `inventory|propose|review|finalise|decide --client <slug> --surface <s>` into `sites/<client>/build/skeleton/`. `inventory --root <selector>` reads the hosted draft (`surfaces.json[surface].draftUrl`) at 1440, 375 and 768 and writes `<s>.inventory.json` and `shots/`; `propose [--site-name <text>]` writes `<s>.skeleton.json` (a Fill skeleton whose every draft link is a `tpl` finder) and `<s>.proposal.json` (per element: candidates, evidence, confidence, low = below 0.7); `review` writes `<s>.review.html`; `finalise` writes `<s>.finalise-request.json` for the low rows and `finalise --apply <picks.json>` merges the picks and re-proposes; `decide --key <k> (--block <slug> [--attrs <json>] | --remove)` and `decide --site-name <text>` record into `decisions.json`. Never calls a model, builds a page or writes outside that folder. |
| `census.mjs` | Fill census command: `<run dir> [--json] [--check]` prints each class of the run unmapped and already-held rows with count, blocks, properties and owner; `--check` exits 1 when a row matches no class (a new gap kind), when the run dropped a declared value (`lib/census-conservation.mjs::conservationRun`: a property the draft declares that ends neither written, held nor unwritten) and when the run has no `reads.json` (save one with `census-reads.mjs`) or one no longer bound to the run (status `unbound`: another run's stamp, a skeleton or draft changed since it was read, a hash the run records otherwise, a node the run's tree lacks; `SGS_REPO_ROOT` names the repository the inputs are hashed under). It lists every conserved read only a widthless held row answers. Not part of `capabilities.mjs --gate`, which checks the inventory and coverage, not a run. |
| `census-reads.mjs` | Read-set command: `<run dir> [--allow-external]` reads the draft again with the surface's skeleton and draft and writes `reads.json` beside the run (`lib/census-reads.mjs::readSetFile`), bound to the run folder's name and the sha256 of the skeleton and draft files it read (`inputHashes`), the file `census.mjs --check` reads for conservation. A draft that loads its runtime from unpkg needs `--allow-external`. |
| `census-drops.mjs` | Conservation report command: `<report file> <date> <run dir>...` writes the dropped declared values of Fill runs as a Markdown report (`lib/census-drops-report.mjs::dropsReport`), the list Track R works from. |
| `framework-backlog.mjs` | Framework backlog merge command (FR-48-68): `[--sites <dir>] [--out <file>] [--on <YYYY-MM-DD>]` merges the F-class rows of every client's `sites/<client>/build/qa/gaps.json` into `.claude/reports/framework-backlog.json` (`lib/framework-backlog.mjs::mergeBacklog`), one row per block, attribute and property listing every client that shows the gap. A closed row stays closed, a re-merge keeps `firstSeen` and never duplicates, and a row that is not a well-formed F-class row is refused and counted. |
| `backlog-recheck.mjs` | Framework backlog re-check command (FR-48-68): `[--dry-run] [--probes <module.mjs>] [--backlog <file>]`; after a framework change it re-runs G3 to G6 for every open backlog row against each entry's client site (`lib/backlog-recheck.mjs::recheckBacklog`) and moves a row whose gap no longer holds to closed with the commit (`git rev-parse HEAD`) and the gate evidence; a row whose re-check cannot run stays open with the reason. `--dry-run` writes nothing; `--probes` replaces the live probes for every client (default export `{ measure(row, setting), probe(row, kind) }`); without it each entry's surface runs `gate-chain.mjs::defaultProbes`. Reads the DB read-only. |
| `census-all.mjs` | The mechanism census across every surface and client (exports `clientRows(client, qa, lookup, rules)`, one client's census rows per surface; `renderTable({ groups, unclassified, surfaces, clients })`, the Markdown table; `censusAll({ root, clients, db? })`, the whole run): `[--clients a,b] [--root <repo root>] [--out <file>] [--json] [--check]` finds each surface's newest walker report (`<build>/qa/parity/out/<surface>/report.json`, else the final round of the newest Solve run) and newest Fill run (`<build>/qa/fill/<surface>/<stamp>/`: `unmapped.json`, `fill-report.json` `alreadyHeld`, `writes` and `confirm`, `filled.tree.json`), de-duplicates the walker rows, gives every row a mechanism signature (`lib/census-mechanism.mjs`) and exactly one FR-48-65 class (`lib/census-gap-class.mjs::classifyRows`) and prints and writes one table: signature, rows per client, example rows (client, surface, element, draft → live), the Fill reason, the class and the owning Spec 48 item from `data/census-classes.json`; `renderTable` takes the optional `classes` counts (`classCounts`), which sum to the row total the header states. `--out` writes Markdown, or JSON for a `.json` file; `--check` exits 1 when a row has no signature. Exit 2 on usage. Not part of `capabilities.mjs --gate`, which checks the inventory and coverage, not a run. |
| `check-refs.mjs` | Draft-link check: `--client <slug> --surface <s> [--skeleton <file>] [--draft-url <url> \| --draft-dir <folder> [--draft-index <file>]]` (with neither, the surface's `draftUrl`, else its `draftDir` and `draftIndex` served locally); opens the draft at 1440 (a state-driven surface opened first the way Fill opens it, `lib/fill-open.mjs::stateOpener` and `::openState`, the verdict printed), resolves every `draftRef` and `draftSlots` finder (a Site Info row's generated icons included) through the walker's `resolveFinder`, counts what the raw selector would match, prints resolved / unique / missing per node, and exits 1 on any target that is missing, ambiguous or outside its node. |
| `handoff-check.mjs` | Spec 48A handoff folder check: `<handoff folder> [--known <file>] [--schemas <dir>] [--json]` runs FR-48A-2 to FR-48A-8 and FR-48A-10 (exit 0 pass, warnings allowed; 1 a failure naming the file and rule; 2 usage). `--probe-runtime` renders `data/runtime-probe.dc.html` with the folder's `support.js` and prints the module difference from the nearest other known build; `--add-runtime [--source "<text>"]` records the build only when the probe passes. |
| `intake.mjs` | Stage 1 command (FR-48-17): `<client> [--record] [--widths 375,768,1440] [--surfaces a,b] [--repo <path>]`, `--palette-only <client> [--record] [--into-intake]` (the palette stage alone, written to `build/intake/palette.json` and with `--into-intake` into `intake.json` and `review.md`) and `--apply-review <client> [--section <row type>[,...]]` (marks only the named review sections) run intake on `sites/<client>/handoff/` (manifest re-check, replay file, census, routing, breakpoints, page map, tokens and palette, Site Info, flags, draft defects, skeletons, missing features, notices) and writes `sites/<client>/build/intake/intake.json` and `review.md` (carrying marks over from the previous intake, kept as `intake.prev.json`) and `sites/<client>/build/page-map.json`, each validated first; `--finalise <client>` writes `finalise-request.json` for the low-confidence and unresolved skeleton rows (an agent answers it with `prompts/skeleton-finaliser.md` into `finalise-picks.json`); `--apply-picks <client>` applies the picks and re-renders the review with no browser stage, writing nothing on an invalid pick; `--render <client>` re-renders `review.md` from `intake.json`; `--rerun-skeletons <client>` proposes the skeleton rows again offline from the inventories the last run saved (`build/intake/inventories/`) with today's standing rules, carries the earlier picks for rows still awaiting the finaliser and re-renders the review (no browser); `--facts <client>` writes only `build/intake/facts.json` (the template, logic and surfaces readers joined, FR-48-7) from the export, the replay file and the saved inventories (about 10 seconds for Indus, 30 for Mama's, against the full run's 97 minutes for Indus); the full run writes the same file from its `facts` stage after the skeletons; `--catalogue <client>` reads the draft catalogue offline (FR-48-92, `lib/intake-catalogue-run.mjs`) into `sites/<client>/build/catalogue.json` and its summary into `intake.json` and review.md's "Draft catalogue" section (the full run writes the same after the page map); `--page-map <client>` (a `product` row names the choice flow its page's button opens, `opensFlow`, `lib/intake-pagemap-flow-openers.mjs`) rebuilds only `sites/<client>/build/page-map.json` offline from the export (`lib/intake-pagemap-regen.mjs`: Bean's page-map review marks from `intake.json` re-applied by row id, the current map's ids carried by row key, validated before it is written, every added, removed or changed row printed; under a second per client); `--apply-review <client>` reads Bean's marks back, refusing any unmarked or off-list row, writes the confirmed page map, and saves his confirmed block choices to the client's skeleton decisions file; `--apply-review <client> --section <row type>[,...]` checks and writes the marks of the named sections only (not `page-map` or `skeleton-block`, whose confirmations need the whole apply), leaving every other row's review entry as it was and reading no other section of review.md. Every browser stage runs in a secret-free worker. Exit 0 pass, 1 failure named, 2 usage. |
| `freeze.mjs` | The cross-client guard for the skeleton rules. `snapshot <client> [--no-built]` proposes every surface offline (as `check-approved.mjs` does) and writes `sites/<client>/build/qa/freeze/<YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM>[-no-built]/proposals.json`: a header (client, commit, date, the flag, counts) and per surface each row's key, draft tag, proposed block or action, the rule and its confidence. `diff <client> <freeze dir>` proposes again with the current code and prints every changed, added and removed row with counts (it proposes as the freeze header says; a disagreeing `--no-built` is a usage error). `diff-all <stamp>` diffs every client whose freeze directory starts with the stamp. `diffProposals(before, after)` is the comparison. Exit 0 nothing changed, 1 anything did, 2 on usage or a missing file. Run `diff-all` before and after every skeleton rule commit; a changed row on a client that did not teach the rule goes to Bean. |
| `check-approved.mjs` | `<client> [--no-built]`: the skeleton standing rules measured against the client's approved review outcomes (`sites/<client>/build/intake/approved-decisions.json`), proposed offline from the saved inventories with the motion inventory and no finaliser picks; compares the block, the action family (remove, absorbed and unwrap are one) and Bean's link rulings ("not linked", "link source <x>"), against every copy of the approved template element (each copy that disagrees is listed with its own key); rows a built post settles and rows approved as a setting are counted apart; `--no-built` measures the rules alone. Exit 0 when nothing mismatches, 1 otherwise, 2 on usage. |
| `stage0.mjs` | Stage 0 command (FR-48-3, FR-48-16): `--client <slug> --site <mirror target> [--plan] [--recalibrate]`; runs the handoff check, derives the fixed block set (layout and content blocks plus the blocks the surface kinds call for), recalibrates each stale or missing slot map with `calibrate.mjs --slot-map-only` (no default paint), and writes `sites/<client>/build/intake/calibration-baseline.json` only when every block's slot map is current and the baseline validates. A block whose fixture names a reference the target does not supply or the site does not hold is `needs-refs` and blocks the run. `--plan` prints the set and what would run and touches nothing (it reads the site to check the references). Exit 0 only on a full pass. |
| `handoff-repackage.mjs` | Brings an export made before `MANIFEST.json` existed to the Spec 48A format (FR-48A-1): `<handoff folder> --project "<name>" --entry <draft> [--entry ...] --generated YYYY-MM-DD [--dry-run]`. Proves every file still matches its old `MANIFEST.md` size and SHA-256 and stops on any mismatch before writing; then removes `MANIFEST.md` and `handoff-schema/trace/`, copies missing master schema files, writes `MANIFEST.json` and runs the handoff check (exit 0 pass, 1 refused or check failure, 2 usage). |
| `trace-check.mjs` | `<handoff folder> [entry .dc.html] [--shift=7]` (FR-48A-7 cross-check): loads a single-file draft's entry page at 1440, scrolls it, records every Web Animations call, CSS animation start and CSS transition run with its `data-dc-tpl`, and matches each to an `animations.json` record; `--shift=7` is the negative control (a sound run drops to 0 matches). |
| `data/shared-state-elements.json` | Elements shared code prints inside many blocks, by the css_element name the framework DB gives their state rows (the shared wrapper's `overlay`, the link helper's `text-link` and `content-link`), each with its selector and the helper that prints it; `lib/calibrate-instances.mjs::stateTarget` looks for the block's own class or this shared selector. |
| `data/skeleton-decisions.json` | The skeleton writer's standing, client-free decisions: the column-label block, the list rule (minimum links, stacked), brief-only line patterns, Site Info text patterns (copyright, hours, address, attribution), the maps-link and logo-image patterns, and element decisions, and the header's three rows (`headerSlots`, taught by Indus's top bar, confirmed by Bean 2026-10-22). Every block named is checked against the framework database on load. |
| `data/calibration-baseline.schema.json` | Appendix C: the JSON Schema (draft 2020-12) of `calibration-baseline.json`, validated with the route's pinned Ajv. |
| `data/reading.schema.json`, `data/construct-registry.schema.json` | Appendix C: the JSON Schemas (draft 2020-12) of a reading record (FR-48-28) and of `data/construct-registry.json` (FR-48-24); `tests/intake-schemas.test.mjs` fails when either differs from the spec. |
| `lib/intake-spec-sync.mjs` | Spec 48 Appendix C against `data/*.schema.json`: parses each labelled JSON block, the intake-record key table and the construct-kind list from the spec, and reports every disagreement with a schema file. |
| `lib/intake-sandbox.mjs` | FR-48-23 / FR-48A-10: runs a verified export in a fresh Chromium context behind a refusing trap proxy (no bypass, loopback included): the draft server's exact origin is fetched by Node and fulfilled, fonts, stock images and the three pinned runtime scripts come from the export's replay HAR, every other request (another host or another loopback port) is aborted and listed with its draft ID, and every WebSocket is closed and listed. Re-checks `MANIFEST.json` (`schema_version` "1.0", every size and hash) first. `visit` opens a state-page route given as `<file>?#@state/page=<route>` by loading the entry and setting the route on the live logic (`lib/state-route.mjs::openStateRoute`); a route that is not reached throws. |
| `lib/browser-close.mjs` | Closing a Chromium browser without waiting on a page that cannot answer: the process id read while the browser answers, and the process killed when the graceful close has not finished within `CLOSE_GRACE_MS`. |
| `lib/intake-record.mjs` | FR-48-23: records the replay HAR without letting draft code reach the network: a closed-network discovery pass lists the recordable URLs, then Node fetches exactly those (browser User-Agent, SRI checked for the pinned scripts, `url()` references in recorded CSS followed) into `sites/<client>/build/intake/replay/<manifest hash>.har`. |
| `lib/intake-harness.mjs`, `lib/intake-harness-page.mjs` | FR-48-28 determinism harness: Playwright's paused clock at one instant (wrapped timers see the fake clock), a seeded `Math.random`, both storages cleared and seeded from the draft's own defaults, every font face `loaded` before a read; rest mode settles timeouts up to 10 s of virtual time, caps intervals at 3 ticks and frame loops at 60 frames, runs one scroll pass with 100ms at each step and holds infinite animations at phase 0; sample mode reads an animation at set times by seeking it or advancing the clock. A quiet stretch (no frame callback, idle callback, running animation or due timer before it ends) is crossed in one clock jump of whole 16ms steps with no frame wait, so every firing and reported time stays on the step grid and readings are byte-identical with it off (`INTAKE_SKIP_QUIET=0`). A timer still pending at the settle limit, or an uncaught draft error, fails the run naming it. `harness-page` is the in-page init script. `gotoDraft` applies a state-page route carried in its hash (`lib/state-route.mjs::applyStateRoute`) once the runtime has stamped the page. |
| `tests/fixtures/intake-harness-quiet/Home.dc.html`, `tests/fixtures/intake-harness-quiet/MANIFEST.json`, `tests/fixtures/intake-harness-quiet/support.js` | The quiet-step fixture export: a 500ms and a 9000ms timeout and a 6500ms interval writing the clock, a frame-loop counter, and a reveal below the fold that writes the clock and starts a transition and a 700ms timeout; no infinite animation. Not route code. |
| `lib/intake-reading.mjs` | FR-48-28 / Appendix C: one reading record per stamped element (draft ID, path, computed values for the walker's properties, inline declared values, border box, harness settings), sorted and written as deterministic JSON lines, validated against `data/reading.schema.json`; `readSurface` is one whole harness run. |
| `tests/fixtures/intake-harness/Home.dc.html`, `tests/fixtures/intake-harness/MANIFEST.json`, `tests/fixtures/intake-harness/support.js` | The harness fixture export: a clock value, a random value, a stored key read at boot, a 2000ms timeout, an interval counter, a frame-loop writer, a reveal below the fold, a late font, infinite CSS and Web Animations loops and a 600ms linear fade. Not route code. |
| `tools/mutate.mjs` | Mutation check for a MUST FAIL test: `<route dir> <mutations.json> <test file>`, mutations an array of `{ file, find, replace, why }` (`find` occurring once); the test must be green unmodified and red under each mutation (BROKEN when the mutated code no longer loads), the file backed up to `<file>.mutate-bak` and restored after every run and on exit. Exit 0 all killed, 1 one survived, broke or was SKIPPED (its `find` matches nothing or more than one place: named in the line and again at the end, so a stale find fails the run), 2 red baseline or a leftover backup. |
| `lib/mutation-lists.mjs`, `tools/check-mutation-lists.mjs`, `tests/mutation-lists.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/mutation-lists/mutations.json` | The static guard over every mutation list under `tests/` (a JSON array of `{ file, find, replace }`): `checkMutationLists` names each entry whose `find` matches no text, more than one place, or a missing target (line endings ignored, as `tools/mutate.mjs` reads it), so a list that went stale with the code fails the route suite instead of skipping silently. `compareBaseline` holds today's stale entries to `data/mutation-list-stale-baseline.json`. The command prints every stale entry as `known` or `NEW` and every fixed baseline entry, exit 1 on either; the test has negative controls (zero, two and missing-file matches; non-list JSON; a fresh and a fixed baseline entry). `jsonFiles(dir)` returns every JSON file under a folder, sorted; `isMutation(m)` tells whether one entry has string `file`, `find` and `replace`; `isMutationList(data)` tells whether parsed JSON is an array holding at least one such entry, so one malformed entry never hides its list (MUST FAIL, `tests/fixtures/mutation-lists/mutations.json` 2 of 2 killed); `listProblems(list, routeDir, read)` returns the problems of one list (`[{ index, file, why, count, malformed? }]` for each entry that is not a whole mutation, whose `find` does not occur exactly once in its target, or whose target is missing); `problemLine(x)` renders one problem as a line a person can act on. |

| `tools/cause-table.mjs`, `tests/cause-table.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/cause-table/census.json` | The cause-table dashboard feed: `<census.json> --out <dir> [--top 12]` reads the JSON of `census-all.mjs --json --out <file>.json` and writes `cause-classes.csv` (`class, meaning, owner`, one column per client of the census's own client list, `total`; one row per FR-48-65 class, meanings and owners from `data/census-classes.json` `classes`) and `cause-mechanisms.csv` (each class's 12 largest signatures: `class, block, property, state, tier, reason, owner_fr`, the client columns, `total`, `example` with client, surface, element and draft → live of the group's first example row). The census holds a group's rows per client and per class but not per client within a class, so a group spanning classes is split by class share (`apportion`: whole numbers, floored shares then the largest fractions, both the class and the client margins kept exactly); signatures rank by rows in the class, a tie by the signature. A construct the trace cannot decide is `unreached: null` with a `traceGap`, never a silent verdict. Each width-and-page unit runs in the browser-unit pool and records its changes as operations, applied after all units end in width-then-page order, so the result is the one-at-a-time run's.
