| `lib/shop-parts.mjs` | FR-48-60 to FR-48-62: classifies units into parts by `RULES` (structure first, then a label beside a value by position, then a vocabulary entry's word `hints` as a reported gap), gives each part `locators` (`locatorsOf`: the template key `<dc>/<data-dc-tpl>` of every element its units were found on, one per element in stamp order), builds the per-client record (`counts` with `hinted` and `unmatched`) and FR-48-34-shaped missing-features rows; `resolveVocab` refuses a stale vocabulary. Nothing a surface reads is dropped. |
| `lib/catalogue-source.mjs` | FR-48-92: the catalogue reader's sources (every reachable draft's logic and every local script a draft loads that is not a known runtime) and its static evaluation over their ASTs: `follow` (the nodes an expression stands for, across variables, object and class properties and a shared script's global) and `valueOf` (the plain value, `UNREAD` where it cannot be read). `runtimeHashes()` returns the SHA-256 of every known Claude Design runtime script (`data/known-runtimes.json`); `catalogueSources(folder)` returns the parsed sources of one export folder, `[{ file, idx, kind, text? }]` (a source that does not parse is left out); `sourceOfDraft(folder, file)` loads one draft on its own (tests); `propertyNodes(ctx, name, src, from)` returns the value nodes a property name can name outside its own expression (static or instance class properties and object-literal properties whose value is data, the asking source first); `arraysOf(ctx, src, node)` returns the array literal nodes an expression can be; `isRead(v)` tells whether an evaluated value was read (is not the unread marker). |
| `lib/catalogue-records.mjs` | FR-48-92: the product records (a looked-up list of named, priced records with a distinct key per record) and their fields: price and RRP (`priceFields`), image and gallery (`imageFields`, an export file or an address table), short description, filter facets. `lookups(ctx)` returns every list-of-records lookup in the sources; `descriptionField(ctx, records, taken)` returns the untaken text field the draft shows whose values average at least 30 characters, or null; `facetFields(ctx, array)` returns each facet field of a record list with the drafts whose filter reads it; `recordLists(ctx)` returns `{ lists, ambiguous }`: every looked-up list of object literals whose records each carry the key, a name and a price (a list with two price-like fields neither always the higher is ambiguous; one with no price is no catalogue); `moneyFields(ctx)` returns the field names read inside every currency-formatting call. |
| `lib/catalogue-options.mjs` | FR-48-92: a product's choices from its basket line (`basketLines`, `lineFor`, `lineKeys`) and each state key's options from its setter's `.map` list (`keyOptions`: choice, switch with its price factor, multi-choice, count, unread). `stateKey(ctx, src, n)` takes a member expression and returns the state key it reads (`S.size`, `this.state.size`), or null. |
| `lib/catalogue-read.mjs` | FR-48-92: `readCatalogue` assembles catalogue.json's shape (simple and variable products, pack-size axis, each attribute's Google variesBy axis (`variesByOf`), variations, categories, brand, product base) with a gap row for every choice WooCommerce core cannot hold and a `no-variesby` row for a variable product with no axis SGS's product preflight accepts; `productAddress`, `loadCatalogue`. Facets: `lib/catalogue-facets.mjs::readFacets` (FR-48-92). `MAX_VARIATIONS` (100) is the most variations one product holds (more is a `too-many-variations` gap); `SKU_PREFIX` (`sgs-draft-`) and `skuOf(draftId)` give the SKU a draft product takes; `PACK_SLUG` (`pack-size`) is the pack-size attribute's slug; `slugify(text)` returns a lower-case hyphenated slug (accents dropped, `&` read as "and"); `productBase(exp)` returns the product base the draft's own manifest declares for its single-product route, else `product`; `VARIESBY_WORDS` maps the words naming an axis to Google's closed variesBy values; `catalogueFile(repo, client)` returns the path of `sites/<client>/build/catalogue.json`. |
| `lib/intake-catalogue-run.mjs` | FR-48-92: `intake.mjs --catalogue`; `writeCatalogue` (validated against `data/catalogue.schema.json`, keeping `sites`, recording review drops), `catalogueSummary` for intake.json, `droppedIds`. `CATALOGUE_SCHEMA` is the path of `data/catalogue.schema.json`; `catalogueRecord(fresh, previous, dropped)` returns the file as written: the fresh reading, the dropped ids and the earlier file's `sites` section; `catalogueProblems(record, ajv)` returns the schema problems of a record. |
| `lib/site-build-catalogue.mjs`, `lib/site-build-catalogue-php.mjs` | FR-48-92: the catalogue step of the site build: the read and write PHP, `planCatalogue`, `runCatalogue` (dry run by default, read-back, `recordSite`), and the "photo to come" image (`PHOTO_TO_COME`, `data/photo-to-come.png`) for a product that would otherwise have none: one attachment per site, `_sgs_photo_to_come` on each product carrying it, `photoToCome` in its `sites.<site>` record, never over an image the product has; each attribute's variesBy written as `_sgs_variesby_value` on its terms that have none, a product with no axis (`heldDraft`) made and kept a draft; a stock-host image found by the media upload's file name (`lib/media-stock.mjs::stockFileName`) and any address image fetched by download, typed by its bytes. `catalogueStatePhp(products, images, catalogue)` returns the read's PHP: per product its id, status, SKU, price, image, gallery, categories, brand, attributes, variesBy, variations, permalink and facets, plus every image's attachment id; `catalogueApplyPhp(steps, images, uploads, productBase, catalogue)` returns the write's PHP, which makes the facets before the products, uploads or fetches each image and prints `{ done, issues }`. `GAP_PLUGINS` maps a gap kind to the plugin whose presence settles it (a recurring order for switch gaps, a gift card for open-amount gaps); `PHOTO_TO_COME_FILE` is the "photo to come" image's file (the tool's own); `catalogueImages(repo, catalogue)` returns the images the catalogue names, keyed by asset path or address, plus the photo-to-come image; `tallyCatalogue(plan)` counts the plan's actions by create, complete, exists and left; `readCatalogueState(runPhp, catalogue, images)` runs the read's PHP and returns its parsed answer. A changed product base (`woocommerce_permalinks`) is only saved by the write; `verifyProductBase(runPhp, base, draftIds)` then flushes the rewrite rules in a request of its own (`productBaseFlushPhp`: the product post type registers at `init` with the base the option held, so a flush in the saving request rebuilds the old rules) and proves it took: the stored rules hold the base, the first published catalogue product's address carries it and answers 200 over HTTP (a site that cannot call itself passes on the rules and the address); a failed proof is an issue that fails the apply. |
| `lib/shop-fixtures-variesby.mjs` | Spec 48 section 16: reads the fixture size terms' `_sgs_variesby_value` and writes the catalogue's axis to those with none through `wp eval-file -` (an existing value is never replaced; the apply reads again and refuses a term still without one). |
| `lib/shop-fixtures-wp.mjs` | Spec 48 section 16: a `fetch` stand-in that runs the fixtures' REST requests inside a local mirror through wp-cli (`rest_do_request`), which has no application password. |
| `lib/shop-fixtures.mjs` | Spec 48 section 16: the fixture product definitions (the variable one on the global `pa_size` attribute), the plan (create or exists, by SKU, and convert for a product-local choice), the image choice (the site's media-map entry once the site confirms the id, else a draft asset), the apply with read-back, and `requireSafeCreds` (https, or http to localhost). |
| `lib/catalogue-facets.mjs` | FR-48-92: `readFacets` turns each filter field other than price, brand and categories into a facet (label from the filter group drawing its state key, else the template text before its control; terms named and ordered by the code's own list, swatches from a keyed table; a value worked out by a local function evaluated per record; a value matching every choice carrying every term; a true/false field's term from its active-filter chip), a gap row for a range, a two-field condition or a value the filter offers no choice for; `settleFacets` against the variation attributes. `testOf(src, c, param)` takes one conjunct of a filter callback and returns what it tests: the fields read off the record, the derived ones (`{ field, fn, index }`), the state keys it compares with and the literal that matches every choice per field; `vocabularyFor(ctx, values, records)` returns the code's list naming a set of values (the one covering most of them, at least half; never the record list itself) as `{ entries, missing }`, or null; `groupLabel(ctx, key)` returns the label of the filter group drawing a state key; `templateLabel(ctx, key)` returns the text the draft template shows just before the control bound to a render value reading the key, or null; `chipLabel(ctx, key)` returns the text of the active-filter chip the key alone guards, or null. |
| `lib/site-build-catalogue-facets.mjs` | FR-48-92: the catalogue step's facets: `planFacets` (create, complete or exists per attribute; a reserved slug stops the run), `facetLack` per product, and the PHP that reads attributes and product terms and writes attributes, ordered terms, swatches it never overwrites and filter-only product attributes. `writtenFacets(catalogue)` returns the facets the step writes (those that are not variation axes); `facetSay(catalogue, p)` returns the plan line's facet part for a product to create; `tallyFacets(actions)` counts the facet actions by create, complete and exists; `facetStatePhp(catalogue)` returns the read's PHP (`$facets` and `$sgs_product_facets( $id )`); `facetApplyPhp(catalogue)` returns the write's PHP, which makes or completes each facet's attribute and terms and defines `$sgs_assign_facets( $id, $p, $ours )`. |
| `lib/site-build-catalogue-toggle.mjs` | FR-48-61: the catalogue step's on-off filter: a true-or-false facet is the theme's boolean toggle, so the step sets the theme mods `sgs_shop_filter_boolean_enabled`, `_source` (`attribute`), `_attribute` (the facet slug), `_term` (the slug WooCommerce gave its term) and `_label` (the draft's toggle text, else blank for the theme's "<term> only"); fills only what the site lacks, leaves a differing value with a note, gives the one toggle to the first boolean facet, and reads its write back (`runToggle`, called by `runCatalogue` in its dry run and after its apply). `TOGGLE_MODS` lists the five `sgs_shop_filter_boolean_*` theme mods; `toggleFacet(catalogue)` returns `{ facet, notes }`, the boolean facet the toggle shows (null when the catalogue has none); `toggleStatePhp(facet)` returns the read's PHP (the theme mods and the slug of the facet's term); `planToggle(catalogue, state)` returns `{ action, set, say, notes }` with action `none`, `exists`, `set` or `left`; `toggleApplyPhp(set)` returns the PHP setting the planned theme mods. |
| `lib/page-map-names.mjs` | FR-48-30 and FR-48-55: the page map's naming conventions both intake and the site build use: `MEGA_TITLE_SUFFIX`, `megaPanelTitle` (a mega-menu panel row's title from its menu item label) and `megaPanelLabel` (the label back from the title). |
| `lib/manifest-build.mjs` | The surfaces manifest builder (Spec 48 section 2): one `surfaces.json` entry per page-map row that has an id, with the walker states of its handoff record (cross-checked against intake, which holds no header, menu or footer records). A state's setting state is `scrolled`, `shrunk` or `current` only when the state's name says so, otherwise null; pointer states (hover, focus, active) are not walker states and are listed; an open-able surface (`OPENABLE`: mega menu, drawer, modal, accordion; Fill's state opener reads the same list) keeps a state named open or expanded and a state that sets a real state key to a set value (a mega menu's `{"mega":"sun"}`), a mega menu panel row (`<label> mega menu`) whose record holds several panels keeping only the state its label names (`panelStates`, so each panel surface opens its own tab) and linking to the intake skeleton whose `panel` its label names (`intakeSurfaceOf`; a kept `intakeSurface` naming a skeleton intake no longer holds gives way to the generated link), and drops one whose only keys are derived (not a state key of the draft's logic, read from `facts.json` `state`; a key the logic derives from the width; a `_` key) or unset, falling back to the key names `R`, `item` and `bp` with a `states-no-facts` line when the client has no `facts.json`; several `entry_drafts` are accepted and each surface takes the draft whose base name is its record's `dc` (else the first); a page whose route is a draft file (`Home.dc.html`, any query string ignored) matches the record whose `page_key` is the file's base name; an existing entry keeps every field it has. An entry links to its intake skeleton (`intakeSurface`) through the matched record's id, and its tree is `trees/<postType>/<slug>.json`. A form row matches the form record on its page, a post row its page record by route, and the first post row (`templateSource`) also makes the `single` template surface (`POST_TEMPLATE`). `templateSurfaces(rows)` returns the template copy surfaces (FR-48-56), one per template the site serves that is not a shop row's own: `{ slug, key, copy, row }` with `copy.from` `pages`, `fill` or `page`. |
| `lib/key-space.mjs` | The two key spaces of a draft's elements (Spec 48 §3.4): decision and motion keys written in intake's space (`<root component>/<n>#<copy>`) are matched against a served inventory's `Root/…` keys by one root-segment rewrite; an import chain's keys are never rewritten. `translateElement` and `translateInventory` resolve every served node to its fact-record key by the runtime's host and `data-dc-tpl` identity and its copy index (a `Root` host becomes the entry component; an element that cannot be translated is listed, never guessed); `lib/skeleton-record.mjs::recordView` takes the entry component and `skeleton.mjs propose` passes it, so the record's rules apply to a served inventory and the proposal carries `recordUntranslated`. |
| `lib/site-build-map.mjs` | FR-48-55: the page map as the site build reads and writes it: row keys, the build order (parents first, layout surfaces last), each row's tree (stage 3 file or placeholder; a drawer with no drawer menu binds the header menu) and its hash, and the id write-back that changes only `id` and refuses a map edited during the run; `readIdsSite`, `writeIdsSite` and `rowsForSite` keep which site the ids belong to. `isAccountRow(row)` tells whether a page-map row is WooCommerce's account page: an ordinary page slugged `my-account` or `account`. |
| `lib/site-build-menus.mjs` | FR-48-55 part 3: the menus the page map asks for, as ordered item lists (a mega row takes its slot from a page row, which becomes the panel's section page; a parent label no item carries is a custom `#` item) and the theme location each menu takes. An item's label is the draft's own link text for its route (`linkText`), stored as the item title when it differs from the page title; the page title stands in only when the draft gives no text. |
| `lib/site-build-menu-links.mjs` | FR-48-55 part 3, FR-48-92: a `product` row's menu entry (header, drawer, footer) is the draft's link to one product, so the site build makes it a custom link item: the draft's own link text, the product's permalink as `catalogue.json` `sites.<site>` records it (known once `--catalogue` has built the shop's products) and the draft href's own query (`?sub=1`). One resolver (`menuLinksFor`) for the site build's menu plan and the site check's header check; an entry with no recorded product, no draft item, or a category row is pending with its reason. |
| `lib/site-build-menus-step.mjs` | FR-48-55 part 3, `site-build.mjs --menus`: `supersededItems` (the items of a site-build menu a mega item took over: they open a page that is now the section page of a mega item of the same menu and that no row asks the menu for), `menusStep` (a whole plan's section-page, removal and menus-phase actions in run order, its theme-location options, and the posts they name that have no id on the site) and `runMenus` (prints the step; with `--apply` runs it and fails unless a fresh read plans none of it). Tests: `tests/site-build-menus-step.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/site-build-menus-step-mutations.json` 18 of 18 killed. |
| `lib/site-build-labels.mjs` | FR-48-55 part 3: the draft's navigation link text per surface kind and route: a looped `item.label` text hole's resolved logic values (`facts.json`) matched to the object literal holding that label beside an `href`; a label two routes claim carries no entry. A static link (`<a href="Gifts.dc.html">Gifts</a>`, no binding in its words or address) gives its own text for the route its href opens in each surface that holds it, beside the loop labels; two texts for one route in a surface are reported and carry no entry. |
| `lib/skeleton-header-slots.mjs` | Spec 48 §3.4 `rules.headerSlots`, the pure part: a header always holds exactly three `sgs/site-header-row` blocks (`rowSlot` top, middle, bottom); the main bar (the bar holding the navigation or the logo or home link) is the middle row, a strip above or below it is the top or bottom row. `besideStrips` walks the header root's siblings outward (an out-of-flow or non-rendering one is passed over, one holding another surface's root ends the walk), `insideStrips` finds a direct child of the header root that holds neither navigation nor logo beside one that does, and `slotPlan` gives each side its nearest strip as the row and the rest as extras. Universal signals only. |
| `lib/skeleton-footer-slots.mjs` | Spec 48 §3.4 `rules.footerSlots`: a footer always holds exactly three `sgs/site-footer-row` blocks (`rowSlot` `top`, `columns`, `bottom`); `footerPlan` (pure) finds the grid of link groups (the columns), the in-flow children stacked above (top) and below (bottom) it and the floating ones (bottom), and `buildFooterRows` builds the rows for `skeleton-propose.mjs`. |
| `lib/skeleton-carried.mjs` | FR-48-58: the ids a skeleton carries instead of hand-set ones: a logo block's `logoId` (the media-map attachment of the file the draft element draws, an `<img>` src or the file a script's source map names for the element's attribute value, chosen by the local sha256 against the map's source `sha256`), a navigation block's `ref` (the menu site-build recorded in `build/menu-map.json`) and a shop filter's `attributeId` (`attribute( slug )`: the facet's global attribute id in `build/catalogue.json` `sites.<site>.attributes`, the site's own record first, then the record of the test site a mirror copies; a site with no record is a skeleton problem naming the catalogue step to run). |
| `lib/skeleton-filter-rail.mjs` | FR-48-61: the shop's filter rail: on the shop template's surface, the draft's rail beside the product listing (the branch holding every placeable facet's label, found as the shown element outside the listing whose words are the label the catalogue read from the draft) becomes the theme's `#sgs-shop-filters` aside holding `woocommerce/product-filters` (the active filters and clear button, then per group an `sgs/heading` with `sgs-shop-filters__group-heading` read at the draft's label and its filter, in the draft's order): a facet's `product-filter-attribute` (chips, or a checkbox list when the group shows checkboxes; the open and swatch heading classes when the draft shows it open or the facet has swatches), and the rail's price, brand and category groups when the catalogue says what they filter; any other group (an on-off row, a variation axis) is a note. Rule data: `data/skeleton-decisions.json` `rules.filterRail`; placed by `lib/skeleton-rules.mjs` after the commerce region, with `lib/skeleton-commerce-region.mjs::commerceOf` giving the shop the catalogue's facets, brands and categories. `placeableFacets(facets)` returns the facets the rail places: those the catalogue step writes as filter attributes, not a variation axis and not an on-off switch; `filterRail({ m, region, filters, rule, money })` returns `{ key, attributes, inner, groups, notes, reason }`, or `{ note }` when the labels sit apart, or null when the surface has no rail to place. |
| `lib/skeleton-commerce-region.mjs` | FR-48-87: `rules.commerceRegion`, the part of a WooCommerce page surface the draft draws that WooCommerce's working piece draws, found by structure from the inventory and placed in the skeleton as that piece (the rest of the page stays the draft's own blocks). `filtersOf` reads a draft catalogue's facets, brands and categories. `COMMERCE_ROLES` maps a template (`archive-product`, `single-product`, `cart`, `checkout`) to its role; `commerceOf(entry, rows, catalogue)` returns a surface's `{ role, checkout, signIn?, filters? }` or null; `leadsTo(href, route)` tells whether an href leads to a route (a page file, `#/<route>`, `#<route>` or a path ending in it); `commerceRegion({ m, commerce, rule })` returns `{ key, role, block, attributes, inner, item, reason }` for the region (cart: the element holding the order-line loop and the checkout link; checkout: the one holding every payment field and the order lines, else (no card fields, or no order lines loop shown) the one holding the checkout's entry fields (`rules.commerceRegion.checkoutEntry`: enough shown fields, one an email, phone, name, address or card field) and the money-showing block beside it, the order summary; account: the one holding every password field, else the shown branch holding a link to the sign-in page; shop: the element whose children are the product loop's items), `{ note }` when two candidates leave the surface as it is, or null. Only shown elements count. |
| `lib/skeleton-product-region.mjs` | FR-48-60, FR-48-56: `productRegion`, the product page's hero on the single-product template's surface: the buy box region is the lowest element holding the add-to-basket control (found by the shop parts record's stamp, `lib/skeleton-shop-locators.mjs`) and the page's heading, and the hero is that region or, when a shown sibling of it holds the product images, its parent. The hero becomes an `sgs/container` holding a heading bound to the product title (`sgs-product/field`) and `sgs/buybox` (`rules.commerceRegion.roles.product`, the single-composite fallback until the Spec 27 parts split). Pure: the inventory model, the commerce facts and the rule in, the region out. |
| `lib/skeleton-shop-locators.mjs` | FR-48-60, FR-48-61: where the draft's shop parts sit, read from `shop-parts.json`: `tplOf` is the draft runtime stamp of a locator (`Product/108` is 108), `shopPartStamps` the stamps of the add-to-basket control (`add`), the sort control (`sort`) and the result count (`count`). Pure. |
| `lib/skeleton-shop-toolbar.mjs` | FR-48-61, FR-48-87: `shopToolbar`, the shop's sort control and result count (found by the shop parts record's stamps) become `woocommerce/catalog-sorting` and `woocommerce/product-results-count` in a toolbar row at the top of the product collection (`rules.shopToolbar`); the draft's own control, count and the wrappers holding only them (and a bare label) are absorbed into the collection, and a draft sort option none of WooCommerce's orders expresses (`rules.shopToolbar.sortOptions`) is returned as a missing feature, which `proposeSkeleton` stamps on the option's row (`missingFeature`, listed by `lib/intake-missing.mjs::skeletonMissing`). Category chips that narrow the listing and are labelled with the catalogue's categories are `woocommerce/product-filters` with a `product_cat` chips filter (`rules.categoryChips`, placed in `lib/skeleton-rules.mjs`). Pure. |
| `lib/skeleton-loop-blocks.mjs` | FR-48-83, Spec 27 FR-24-4, FR-48A-8: `loopBlocks`, a loop (`sc-for`) on any page is the working block its items say, read from the code and never from a heading: items that each show a currency amount and link to a product page (an `a` leading to the page map's single-product route, or a script link when the page map holds such a row) are the product collection the shop region uses with its own query (`inherit` off, one product per item, the draft's cards per row); a loop of links labelled with two or more catalogue brands is `sgs/brand-strip` reading the product brands; a loop whose items each show a rating (star glyphs or a score out of five) with quotes in at least half is the reviews block of the provider its links lead to (`rules.loopBlocks.reviews.providers`), or a note when no link names one. Not placed in an overlay or on a WooCommerce page's own commerce role. Pure: the inventory model, the page map rows, the catalogue's brands and the rules in, the regions out. |
| `lib/site-build-state.mjs` | FR-48-55: the site state the plan compares with, read in one `wp eval-file`: rows' posts (a mega panel's with its section page) and every post carrying a row key, the posts already holding an unbuilt row's type and slug, classic menus with their site-build mark and stored item titles, menu locations, page templates per post type, the front-page, posts-page and permalink options, the active-layout pointers with the row key each names, each post's and template's content hash, and WooCommerce's default cart, checkout and my-account content by role (`wooDefaults`). |
| `lib/site-run-layout.mjs` | FR-48-55: the active-layout pointer the site run sets after building a header, footer or drawer surface: `layoutAreaOf` (the area of a surface whose `target.postId` is a page-map layout row), `pointLayout` (reads the pointer, writes it through `wp sgs <area> set-active` when it differs, reads it back, refuses a non-layout area) and `pointAfterBuild` (both; null and no wp-cli call for any other surface). |
| `lib/site-build-plan.mjs` | FR-48-55: the pure planner: the page map against the state as phased actions (posts, menus, layout, site), the problems that stop a run, notes and the option before-and-after table; `tally` counts creates, changes and adoptions. `keepsWooContent(row)` tells whether a WooCommerce page's content is WooCommerce's own block, kept and never rebuilt from a tree (the cart and checkout pages). `isContentOnlySurface(entry)` tells whether a surface is a content-only post surface, which `site-run.mjs` skips without `--with-content` and `lib/walker-check.mjs` lists instead of faulting when its walker files are absent. `isUnwalkedWooPage(target, row)` tells whether a surface is one of those pages (its target is not a template and its row keeps WooCommerce's content): the one decision `site-run.mjs` uses to skip its Fill, build and walk and `lib/manifest-build.mjs` uses to name no `walker`/`walkerFull` for it. |

The checkout is the page holding payment fields (`autocomplete="cc-…"`, or two or more `inputmode="numeric"` fields) and order lines (a loop of currency amounts); the cart any other page with order lines and a link to the checkout and no password field; the shop the filtered product listing (a loop of a mounted product card narrowed by the page's own controls; of two or more, the one the single-product page links to); an embedded manifest's `wp.type` or `kind` naming a product archive, cart or checkout is taken as written; a route whose words alone name one stays a page with a gap row. The page holding a surface is its file when that file is a route, else the route the `sc-if` gate above its `<main>` names; a header or footer repeated in two or more routed files is the site's; only a site-wide header, footer and nav drawer (one holding a `<nav>` or two or more links to routes) are layout rows; a page's own header or footer, a drawer holding no nav, the cart block's panel (a drawer showing order lines and leading to checkout, on the cart row), and a form or modal on a WooCommerce page (shop, product, cart, checkout, my account) produce no row and are recorded in the owning row's `parts`; a chrome part with no header row is a gap row. A section or overlay with two or more pick lists (`onClick="{{ o.pick }}"` in an `sc-for`, the loop variable escaped; a loop of tabs or disclosures is none) and no `<form>` is an `sgs_choice_flow`, also on a WooCommerce page; a mega panel that gates several `sc-if` panels is one row per panel, named by the opener whose handler sets the state value the gate tests. WooCommerce's pages keep its slugs (`shop`, `cart`, `checkout`; every page holding a password field is the one `my-account` row), the shop row takes the label of the first menu item linking to it, a route reached only from inside the checkout page with order-confirmation evidence (the checkout's submit leads to it, or it shows an order reference) is its endpoint (no row; a part of the checkout row), and a shop client always gets cart, checkout and my-account rows. `surfaces.json` is a cross-check: a record pairs with the code surface of its kind where it sits (a hint modal or form with a code choice flow), a surface on one side only is a gap row, and a record the code does not show is a `draft-notes` part of its page. A drawer menu whose top-level items are each a header mega item adds no menu entries (the drawer binds the header menu); an embedded `data-sgs-manifest` is the proposal when present. Every draft link maps to a row, an external link, an in-page control or a gap row. `ACCOUNT_TEMPLATE` (`page`) is the template the account page names (the site's own page copy); `orderConfirmations(rows, route, pageOfTag, home, routeSlug, used, facts)` finds the checkout's order confirmation (a route every link to which comes from inside the checkout page and that shows the order is confirmed) and makes it its own row (FR-48-56). |

| `lib/tokens.mjs` | FR-48-31: parses an export's `tokens/*.css` into declared custom properties with no render (comments, strings, nested parentheses, `@import`, nested `@media`/`@supports`, element rules, CSS nesting), resolves each `var()` chain with fallbacks (a cycle or missing reference is a problem with a null value, R-48-3), types each value and normalises colours to `#RRGGBB[AA]`. |
| `lib/intake-palette.mjs`, `lib/intake-palette-usage.mjs`, `lib/intake-palette-grounds.mjs`, `lib/intake-palette-run.mjs` | FR-48-31 / FR-33-2 / R-47-7: the palette role proposal. Colour tokens matched to the draft's colours by exact value (alpha-aware), deduped at CIEDE2000 ΔE ≤ 1, and given slugs first from the draft's own declarations (`intake-palette-declared.mjs`: a var() alias whose name is a theme.json slug or that `data/palette-role-words.json` names, the comment beside a token in tokens/*.css, a README line naming a hex right after a role phrase; the strongest declaration per slug wins and a tie between colours cancels; a declared token nothing paints still takes its slug), then from rendered and code facts for what the declarations leave, which also check them (each slug where usage gives another value is listed in `disagreements`, a reader defect to trace) (`intake-palette-usage.mjs::usageRoles`: the page ground and page text read from the body (else the colour of the most page text, by characters), the footer's own or inherited ground, heading and link colours on light grounds, divider lines by the pixels they draw (a link underline is none), the ground of links that open WhatsApp (`whatsapp`, never a brand ground), button grounds, the next grounds, the text on each ground (by characters on the section and accent fills; the accent's own ink, a text colour of its hue, when its fill holds none), the accent's light tint of its hue that carries that ink, muted text as the most text at least 10 L* lighter than the text, and `success` as the green the draft's code gives a `role="status"` message); one value fills every role its facts give, and every token of that value carries them (`roles`, the shown `role` first). A token name is a last-resort hint: it fills only a slug no usage fact gave, never one a usage fact gave another value or a one-off colour does most, and its row says `by: 'name'` at lower confidence. Unused tokens get no role; colours outside the tokens stay one-off hex; slugs read from `theme/sgs-theme/theme.json` at run time. `intake-palette-grounds.mjs::readGrounds` is the in-page probe beside each 1440 rest read (each element's ground, the elements holding text, the page ground and text, the footers); `intake-palette-run.mjs::runPaletteOnly` is `intake.mjs --palette-only`; with `--into-intake`, `intake-palette-into.mjs::intoIntake` puts the proposal on the marking path: it replaces intake.json's palette block (creating a minimal intake.json, `paletteOnly` in its header, when the client has none) and regenerates review.md's palette section (creating review.md with that section alone when absent), keeping a mark only when the slugs it names (`accept`: the old proposal's roles; `role:a,b`; `one-off`: none) equal the new proposal's, or when that token's proposal is unchanged (so an override Bean made stands); `--apply-review` marks a palette-only record without a page map or skeleton choices. A row of a merged (ΔE ≤ 1) group carries `value`, the member hex the draft paints most, which the extractor's confirm step keeps for the merged slug. |

Not route code. |

| `data/runtime-probe.dc.html` | FR-48A-5: the fixture draft the runtime probe renders: nesting, `sc-if` both ways, `sc-for`, camelCase attributes (`viewBox`, `tabIndex`, `onClick`), a helmet `<style>`, `style-hover`, a table and a select, so the encoder's renames are numbered. No external scripts. |
| `data/runtime-probe.expected.json` | FR-48A-5: the fixture's recorded numbering from both known builds (identical): `live`, the rendered page's `[tag, data-dc-tpl]` list in document order, and `annotated`, the same from `__dcAnnotatedTemplate`. |
| `data/runtimes/c60c49083997.support.js`, `data/runtimes/8fe7df74405f.support.js` | Byte copies of the known builds (`data/runtimes/<first 12 hex>.support.js`), so the probe can print the module difference of a new build; `--add-runtime` adds one per new build. Not route code. |
| `prompts/skeleton-finaliser.md` | The brief for the finaliser subagent that settles a skeleton's low-confidence rows from `<s>.finalise-request.json`; the session runs it, the scripts never call a model. |
| `calibration-targets.json` | The calibration page per site (`envFile`, `envKey`, `postId`), `image` (a media object on that site written into background-image markers and overlay preconditions) and `refs` (the site's object for each fixture token, see `lib/calibration-refs.mjs` below; a mirror inherits its `mirrorOf` test site's refs and may override any). |
| `fixture-refs.mjs` | The fixture-reference detector: `--survey [--json]` lists every reference-shaped value and its category, `--fix [--apply] [--home <target>]` turns literal ids into tokens by the home target's refs (default `eye-care-test`), `--check` exits 1 while a bare reference remains (lint.mjs runs the same check), `--self-test` runs its own cases. |
| `provision-calibration-refs.mjs` | `--site <test site> [--image <file>] [--logo <file>] [--dry-run]`: creates on a test site, over SSH as user Claude, what the fixture tokens need (the "CR calibration" menu with a submenu, a mega-panel item and the menu-item role fourth; a calibration mega panel and `sgs_drawer`; image and logo attachments from the given repo files, else an existing image role is kept and the logo reuses it; a product when WooCommerce is active) and writes the IDs as that target's `refs`. Finds by name or slug before creating, so a rerun creates nothing. Refuses a mirror (provision its test site, then refresh the mirror). `--dry-run` reads and prints the plan only. |
| `calibration-fixtures.json` | Minimum content, inner blocks, parent chain, optional variants, optional `preconditions` (`{ <setting>: { <attr>: <value> } }`: attributes a setting paints only under, put on that setting's instance) optional `before` blocks (placed ahead of the instance, for a block that reads the page, such as a table of contents) and optional `paintStates` (`[ { name, when: { inner, has, lacks }, attributes, inner, props } ]`: a way the block renders whose resting paint differs from the default instance's, such as an empty Floating layer, measured once each and matched to a node by `lib/paint-state.mjs`; `props` names the only properties the render shape changes, for a state drawn with settings it needs only to render) per calibrated block. |
| `ledger.mjs` | Divergence ledger command: `accept <report.json> <row id>` adds an entry dated today; `stale <report.json>` exits 1 while any entry is stale. |
| `answer-sheet.mjs` | Answer-sheet scorer (route-accuracy R1): `--client <slug> --surface <s>[,<s>...] [--report f] [--triage f] [--solve f] [--sheet f] [--baseline f] [--write-baseline] [--detail]`; scores the frozen hand-labelled rows of `<build>/qa/answer-sheet.json` against each surface's walk, triage and Solve report (by default the files its baseline entry was written from, else its triage and the walk and Solve report that names; `none` leaves one out), prints the pass counts per label (false alarms per pattern) with the outcomes behind them, exits 1 when a row that passed in `<build>/qa/answer-sheet-baseline.json` fails now, and exits 2 when a file it names is missing (walks under `qa/solve/` are not in git). |
| `lib/db.mjs` | Read-only `block_attributes` queries. |
| `lib/normalise.mjs` | Value parsing and token snapping from `theme-snapshot.json`, with the snap log. |
| `lib/snapshot-freshness.mjs` | FR-33-12 freshness gate (called by `lib/normalise.mjs::loadSnapshot`): refuses a `theme-snapshot.json` whose recorded MANIFEST hashes are absent or differ from the export's current `handoff/MANIFEST.json`. |
| `lib/resolve.mjs` | The one property-to-setting engine. |
| `lib/box-tier.mjs` | Spec 48 FR-48-52: the one builder of a box setting's per-tier value, used by `lib/resolve.mjs::resolve` and `lib/fill-width-ladder.mjs::ladder`: a padding, margin or border-width setting holds `{ top, right, bottom, left }` per tier, so a per-side row writes its own side and a shorthand row all four, and a flat string never lands in a box. |
| `lib/resolve-side-effects.mjs` | The tie rule for settings that all paint one property on one element, used by `lib/resolve.mjs::resolve`: `sideEffectFree(rows, { effectsOf, slot, same, siblings, paintOf, equal, isLength })` takes the tied rows (`attr_name`), calibration's effects of each (`effectsOf(row)`), the read element's path (`slot`), `same(path, slot)`, the draft's reads of the element's other properties (`siblings`) and its calibrated default paint (`paintOf(property)`), and returns the tied rows no side effect rules out, or `rows` unchanged when none would remain. A setting whose marker also moves a property the draft shows at the element's default paint at every width both state cannot give the draft (a text gradient clips the background to the text, so a draft whose `background-clip` is the default is painted by the background gradient beside it); a length never decides, and no block, setting or property name is read. |
| `lib/resolve-declared.mjs` | FR-48-36: the declared value a confirmed row writes, in the form the setting holds (a unit setting switches unit; a setting with no unit partner holds a relative length only where calibration proved a string form). |
| `lib/tree.mjs` | Trees: read, write, refs, setting writes, live-site safety. |
| `lib/cache.mjs` | The calibration cache's two outputs: the library-wide slot map and each site's own default paint; reading, writing, the split of one into the other and the per-output skip rules (a file read under another `READ_KEY` is due on every site). |
| `lib/calibrate.mjs` | Calibration library: calibration trees, slot detection, default paint; re-exports the calibration modules below. |
| `lib/calibrate-props.mjs` | What calibration reads: the read and pseudo-layer properties, `READ_KEY` (the key of those lists, carried by every cache file) and how a css_property maps onto them. |
| `lib/calibrate-markers.mjs` | Marker values per setting shape (Spec 48 §3.2 marker table). |
| `lib/calibrate-instances.mjs` | Which instances a block's calibration page holds: markers, their preconditions (variant, gating toggle, border partners, overlay image, layout mode) and state targets. |
| `lib/calibrate-read.mjs` | The in-page reader and the per-width read under each state trigger. |
| `lib/calibrate-open.mjs` | The Open state of a calibration instance: an already-open toggle or `details[open]` is read as it is; otherwise the instance's first closed toggle whose panel sits inside it, or its first closed `<details>`, is opened, forced open when its handler cannot, and closed again after the read. |
| `lib/calibrate-chunk.mjs` | How calibration builds a block's page in pieces: the child process's heap, the fixture's own chunk size, and halving a chunk whose build timed out. |
| `lib/calibration-lock.mjs` | One calibration per calibration page: a per-site lock in the machine's temp folder that `calibrate.mjs` takes before building; and one measurement of a block's library-wide slot map at a time: a per-block lock beside the slot map (`cache/<block>.slotmap.lock`) that `calibrate.mjs` takes for each block whose slot map is due. |
| `lib/stage0.mjs` | Stage 0 library: the fixed block set, per-block slot-map state against the deployed hash and the current `READ_KEY`, the baseline and its validation, the orchestrator `runStage0` with injected effects. |
| `lib/calibrate-container.mjs` | What a block's source says before any browser opens: whether its tiers follow its container's width (an `@container` rule in the built CSS, or a render passing `container_queries`/`container` true), and whether its render returns early on this site. |
| `lib/calibrate-content.mjs` | The content side of calibration: which elements a setting makes appear or disappear, whose text it prints and which it makes a link — the settings that paint no CSS property, queried by framework-database `role` because `attrsFor` never returns them. |
| `lib/deploy-hash.mjs` | The deploy key: md5 of a block's front-end build files, locally and on the site. |
| `lib/calibration-refs.mjs` | Site references in calibration fixtures: the token vocabulary, resolving a fixture from a target's refs, the existence check on the site (`wp eval-file -` in a WSL mirror or over SSH) and the gate that leaves a block with an unsupplied or missing reference uncalibrated. `evalScript(php)` takes a PHP body and returns the file text `wp eval-file -` reads: one opening tag, whether or not the body carries its own. |
| `lib/fixture-refs.mjs` | The fixture-reference detector's survey, transform and check. |
| `lib/provision-product.mjs` | FR-48-3: the generic calibration product (colour and size axes, a tag attribute, two brands, `_sgs_rrp`, a sale price, WooCommerce's placeholder image) completed in place by title, with a key over its spec so a site already at the spec plans nothing; no client attribute names. |
| `lib/calibrate-basket.mjs` | FR-48-3: a fixture's `basket` key fills and empties the WooCommerce basket through the Store API from inside the page; the block is read a second time with the line in the basket and each instance keeps the reading that shows its marker (`pickPass`). |
| `lib/calibrate-wishlist.mjs` | FR-48-3: a fixture's `wishlist` key reads the block a second time with one saved product in the logged-in user's `_sgs_wishlist` list (`setWishlist`, the user's own list kept under a backup key and put back), as a basket is; a fixture's `settleGone` holds every pass until no element matches it, and `wishlist.settle` until its row appears (`lib/calibrate-read.mjs::readAll`). `wishlistPhp({ customer, on })` takes the customer login and a flag and returns the PHP that puts the wishlist into the states that print every element a setting paints (`on`), keeping what the user and the site had, or puts that back exactly (`on` false). |
| `lib/calibrate-order.mjs` | FR-48-3: a fixture's `order` key reads the block holding one pending test order for the logged-in user (`withTestOrder`, through `lib/site-build-apply.mjs::testOrderPhp`'s `customer`), deleted afterwards whether the read passed or threw; `sgs/account` prints its latest-order card only for a user with an order. `CALIBRATION_ORDER_META` (`_sgs_calibration_test_order`) is the meta key that marks an order calibration made, so the site run's test-order cleanup never deletes it. |
| `lib/provision-refs.mjs` | What `provision-calibration-refs.mjs` creates, its plan from a site's state, and the read and apply PHP. |
| `lib/draft.mjs` | Serves a local draft folder on 127.0.0.1 at an ephemeral port: directory index, an explicit index file, content types, Range, no path escape, clean close. |
| `lib/handoff.mjs` | Spec 48A's check: the required list, the folder walk (symlinks fail), draft and script reachability, Ajv 8 for the master schemas, the manifest builder, and `checkHandoff`, which returns every failure and warning naming the file and rule. |
| `lib/svg-screen.mjs` | FR-48A-10's SVG safety screen: parses each SVG as namespace-aware XML (saxes from the route's own pinned install) and refuses malformed XML, a DOCTYPE, elements outside the SVG namespace (editor and provenance metadata excepted), script and foreignObject by local name whatever the prefix, on* attributes, animations that rewrite a link, `href`/`src`/`data`/`action` and CSS `url()` targets other than `#…` or a raster `data:image`, `javascript:` and CSS `@import`. |
| `lib/runtime-probe.mjs` | FR-48A-5's probe (Playwright by path): renders the fixture with a build through `serveDraft`, reads both numberings, compares them with the record, splits builds into their `// src/<name>.ts` modules and records a passing build. |
| `lib/fill-skeleton.mjs` | The skeleton contract: `draftRef` and `draftSlots` are walker finders, slot finders resolve inside their node's element; node indices, targets, problems, and the clean tree. |
| `lib/skeleton-inventory.mjs` | Skeleton step 1: every `data-dc-tpl` element of a draft surface in a real browser at 1440, 375 and 768 (key, parent, tag, words, attributes, the draft's own code, loop membership, boxes, visibility, screenshots), through the page bridges `__dcAnnotatedTemplate` and `__dcRootName`. The page it opens is set to the state route its url carries (`lib/state-route.mjs::applyStateRoute`). |
| `lib/skeleton-model.mjs` | Skeleton step 2, reading an inventory: the element tree, parts of another element, words, placeholders, stacked runs, the fingerprint. Pure functions.  `ICON_PX` is the most a draft icon measures on either side (48) before an image beside a label is a picture. The icon beside the words of a link or button, and the cite a blockquote ends with, are parts of that element, never blocks of their own. |
| `lib/skeleton-facts.mjs` | The data the skeleton writer reasons from: the framework database (read-only), the brand registry, the client's Site Info placeholder map, `data/skeleton-decisions.json` and the list block's `block.json` items; and the check that the decisions data is sound. |
| `lib/skeleton-classify.mjs` | Skeleton step 2a: which block each element becomes, as scored candidates with their evidence (the four generator rules: footer and header wrappers and rows, link runs as one list, typed brand names and logo images as the logo block, brief-only lines as remove). Precedence: a client decision, then the standing rules (`lib/skeleton-rules.mjs`), then a finaliser pick or a standing element decision, then the scoring. |
| `lib/skeleton-rules.mjs` | Skeleton step 2a, the standing rules (Spec 48 FR-48-6): framework-wide verdicts that settle an element before any scoring (remove, unwrap, absorbed, or a block, with `absorb` when the block draws its children); each rule is switched on by its entry in `data/skeleton-decisions.json` `rules`, and the row names the rule that settled it. |
| `lib/skeleton-rerun.mjs` | The skeleton step offline: every surface proposed again from the inventories the last intake run saved (`sites/<client>/build/intake/inventories/`), with today's rules and database and the context `skeleton.mjs propose` passes (the surface's handoff states, the draft's width thresholds, the calibration keys and settings), so a rule change is measured on a whole client in seconds and an intake-generated proposal carries the same child slots and collapse points. |
| `lib/draft-link.mjs` | Spec 48 FR-48-6 "Links": a draft link's destination as a site address. `resolveDraftLink` reads a hash route (`#/contact`, `#contact` in a hash export), a page file (`Help.dc.html?x#part`, resolved from the linking draft's folder) or a `#` link whose click handler calls the page setter with one page (intake's routing links), finds the page-map row (with the routes the page map folded into another row, `lib/intake-pagemap.mjs::proposePageMap` `routeSlug`) and answers its root-relative address (`/` for the front page, the parent chain for a page, `/shop/`, a post's `/<slug>/` from `lib/site-build-plan.mjs::PERMALINKS`), the fragment kept and the query dropped; a product id the draft catalogue holds (FR-48-92, `catalogue.json`, not dropped) is that product's `/<product base>/<slug>/`, a query key naming one of its choices becoming `attribute_pa_<slug>=<term>`; a product id the catalogue lacks, a row that makes no page or a route no row holds is unresolved, never written as it stands; an external link, a root-relative address and an in-page anchor stay as they are. `draftLinksFor` reads a client's context once per process; `linkWrite` turns an answer into the value and handover row every skeleton writer of a link setting uses (`lib/skeleton-write-content.mjs`, `lib/skeleton-write-units.mjs`'s lists, `lib/skeleton-rules.mjs`'s whole-card link and card action). `handlerOf(value)` returns the `{{ x }}` expression of a click handler value, or null; `elementHandler(e)` returns the click handler an inventory element's own markup carries (`sc-camel-on-click`), or null; `elementFile(e)` returns the draft file an inventory element's template lives in. |
| `lib/skeleton-context.mjs` | The proposal context of a surface (`proposeSkeleton`'s `calKeys`, `calSettings`, `states` and `thresholds`), shared by `skeleton.mjs::intakeContext` and `lib/skeleton-rerun.mjs::rerunSkeletons`. |
| `lib/skeleton-part-slots.mjs` | The draftSlots of a draft element a drawing block absorbs that is one of its own parts (Spec 48 FR-48-6): the drawer's opener is the navigation block's burger, given at the part's calibrated slot (`lib/fill-part-pairing.mjs::partHome`) with its children at the part's sub-part slots (the bars) where calibration measured them; an icon box the draft does not draw is named in the row's slotNotes, never invented. The absorbing rule names the part (`rules.<rule>.part`). |
| `lib/skeleton-child-slots.mjs` | The slots and collapse width a block that draws the draft's children still needs (Spec 48 FR-48-6): `draftSlots` for its resting and current child from its calibration, and a navigation block's collapse point from the handoff states and the draft's width thresholds. |
| `lib/skeleton-record.mjs` | The skeleton writer's reading of the fact record (FR-48-7): the client's `facts.json` loaded once per process (a stale or invalid record throws, a missing one is none), a view of each inventory element's template attributes (`aria-current`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-pressed`, `required`, `tabindex`), real tag, declared display, flex and grid track sizing, position, width, minimum width, aspect ratio, own paint and template parent chain, a loop-of-links test, the disagreements between record and render, and the surface the proposal is for (kind, parent, opener, `surfaceParts`). |
| `lib/skeleton-narrowing.mjs` | Which lists a group of choice chips narrows (the `optionSetFilter` rule), read from the draft's logic syntax tree and never run: the chips' loop list is a `renderVals` key whose handlers `this.setState` a key, and another `renderVals` key is narrowed when its value is produced by a `.filter(callback)` (through a local variable or a `.map`/`.sort` chain) whose callback reads that key (`this.state.k`, an alias of `this.state`, or a destructured name). `renderVals` returns an object literal or `Object.assign(…)` of literals and names it declares as literals. A computed key writes nothing the reader knows; a draft whose hash is not the record's is not read. |
| `lib/skeleton-handler-opens.mjs` | Which surface a control's click handler opens (the `blockPartOpener` and `drawerOpener` rules), read from the draft's logic syntax tree and never run: the handler is a `renderVals` key whose `this.setState` calls (an object, or a function returning one) write keys; a key written with a value that is not a falsy literal opens, and a surface opens on it when one of its handoff states holds the key at true. |
| `lib/skeleton-panel-rules.mjs` | The standing rules that read the record: the children of a header's disclosure panel (`megaGroup`, `megaAside`, `megaContainer`, and `megaGridContainer` for a panel root that lays its own grid out with its own padding or paint) and the icon-only Site Info run in a drawer's top row (`drawerIconRun`). |
| `lib/skeleton-propose.mjs` | Skeleton step 2b: the inventory to a Fill skeleton and the proposal rows (units of siblings that are one block, content settings, `tpl` draft links, fingerprints, handover). |
| `lib/skeleton-form-link.mjs` | FR-48-57, Spec 48 §3.3: `formLinksFor` reads a client's `surfaces.json` and intake record (none without them) and passes them to `formLinksOf`, which lists the saved forms a manifest provides (`provides` `<block>:<slug>` of a linked reference block, the record id from the intake skeleton the entry names) and `linkedFormOf` makes the `formLink` rule's verdict for an inventory element: the draft element directly under the innermost root of a form record whose `parent` is this page is the reference block with its `<x>IsLinked` and `<x>Id` set (names read from render.php by `lib/references.mjs::detectReferences`), any other element under that root goes, and a form surface links nothing of its own; called once by `skeleton-propose.mjs` (the links come through `lib/skeleton-context.mjs::proposalContext` and `skeleton.mjs`), whose linked node carries no `draftRef` so Fill reads nothing for it. |
| `lib/skeleton-saved-inventory.mjs` | `savedInventoryOf` reads the inventory intake saved for a manifest surface's intake skeleton (`build/intake/inventories/<intakeSurface>.json`) in the served key space (`servedInventoryOf`; the saved top element's parent, which is not in it, is cleared); `skeleton.mjs inventory --saved` writes it, for a form surface that is read at its page's route and has no root selector of its own. |
| `lib/skeleton-field-groups.mjs` | FR-48-57: `makeFieldGroups` counts a form's fields for `lib/skeleton-rules.mjs::fieldContent` and `fieldText`: a field is one control or one field group, a tiles field (rule `optionSetForm`) or, inside a form, two or more radios or ticks of one name (of one parent or grandparent when unnamed) held by their lowest common element when it holds no other control and is not the form or the surface root (`groupAt` gives its block, members and options, each option the words of the label round its control). `holdsField` is a field's own wrapper: an element holding exactly one field, that field a child of it or the element itself; a wrapper of two fields gives neither its text. |
| `lib/skeleton-form-host.mjs` | FR-48-57: `formHostNodes` holds the form fields that no `sgs/form` holds in one `sgs/form` node (no draftRef) at the lowest node holding them all, for a draft whose logic holds a form schema but draws its fields without a `<form>` element (a wizard component); a `<form>` element is the form block by the `formHost` rule; called once by `skeleton-propose.mjs`. |
| `lib/skeleton-form-slots.mjs` | FR-48-6: `fieldInputSlot` gives a form field node's draft control as its `draftSlots` entry at the field block's input part, the longest tail of the form block's calibrated home slot for the `rules.formControl.inputPart` css_element that is also an element of the field block's own calibration (no slot where the calibration holds none, as for the file zone and the consent tick); called by `skeleton-propose.mjs`. |
| `lib/skeleton-surface-role.mjs` | FR-48-6, FR-48-30: `surfaceRole` reads the page map's role for a skeleton surface: a choice flow (an `sgs_choice_flow` row whose draftId is the surface's `surfaces.json` id) or a part another row's block draws (a part with owner `block` of the surface's own kind, named by its surfaces.json id or by the page map's tag id matched on component, tag and aria-label); `blockPartSurfaces` lists the fact record's surfaces of a kind that the page map hands to another row's block, seen from any surface (their elements are the record entries at or under their roots); `parseTagId` reads a page-map tag id |
| `lib/intake-openers.mjs` | §3.4 step 1: the ways intake opens a state-driven surface. `openers(s, census, widthKeys)` lists the `state` and `reach` ways `lib/state-openers.mjs::stateWays` builds from every `surfaces.json` state (`openStates`), then the template handlers `opened_by` names; a state bounding a width key (`widthBounds` over the component's `widthKeys`) carries `bounds` and never sets that key. `wayAt(list, first, width)` → `first` where its bounds hold, else the first way held at the width, else null. |
| `lib/state-openers.mjs`, `tests/state-openers.test.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/state-openers-mutations.json` | §3.4 step 1 and §3.6 item 28: the one opener builder intake's `openers`, Fill's `stateOpener` and the walker's `walkerStates` call. `openStates(states)` (closed and default dropped, a state named open first), `isDescription(v)` (the value rule: a hole, a JS literal sketch such as `[]` or `{open:true}`, an ellipsis or a run of `#`, a comparison, prose of three words or more and, at the top level, a range are descriptions, never set), `plainState(st)` (every value a value: the plain keys before the calls), `judgeKeys(st, { own, live })` (settable keys and each dropped key's reason; width bounds over the component's width keys `own`; `live`, Fill's fact record entry), `stateWays(states, { own, live, extra })` (each state's `state` and `reach` ways and the refused states), `noWayReason(dropped)` (the one refusal text for a state with no way, Fill's and the walker's), `describeWay(w)` and `boundsText(bounds)`. Tests: MUST FAIL an accordion range key is no width bound in Fill, a prose value is decided the same way by both, the value rule's cases from the three clients' handoffs, negative controls (a width key's comparison still bounds, a plain state still sets first); 15 of 15 mutations killed with `tools/mutate.mjs`. `CLOSED` is the pattern (`closed` or `default`) naming a state that is not an open one. |
| `lib/skeleton-choice-flow.mjs` | FR-48-6 `rules.choiceFlow` (Spec 43): `choiceFlowModel` reads a choice flow from the fact record: one question per loop whose item holds a button its handler binds to the item (words from the loop's container or the text just before it; options from the loop's recorded values, only when one value per item), the conditional panels after a question's options as the steps it opens underneath, and the summary aside, close, skip, add-to-bag, continue and back controls as the flow's own settings; then `lib/skeleton-choice-flow-canvas.mjs`'s readers: a group of pressable buttons as a question whose revealing option opens what it reveals, a live line naming two questions' picks as the summary, the one text after the last control as the stage note, and every other worded element with no control as a canvas block beside the flow (a lone heading the title); prices, pictures, badges and the header logo are hand-overs, anything unreadable a note. Each step records the record keys of what draws it (a question's `parts`: heading, intro, first option description and badge, help toggle; a panel's `wrapper`; a result part's `resultAt`; each field's `key`) and the model's `pieces` the flow's own (`lib/skeleton-choice-flow-refs.mjs::flowPieces`) |
| `lib/skeleton-choice-flow-refs.mjs` | FR-48-6 `rules.choiceFlow.slots`: `flowPieces(x, o)` → the record keys of the flow root's pieces (the logo and eyebrow in the bar holding Close, Back, the box holding Continue and Add to bag, the stage note and its copy outside the summary); `pieceSlots({ roles, map, settings, at, finder, block })` → a node's `draftSlots`: each rendered piece at the calibrated slot of the setting `rules.choiceFlow.slots` names for it, a note for a setting the calibration lacks |
| `lib/skeleton-choice-flow-canvas.mjs` | FR-48-6 `rules.choiceFlow`, R-47-12: what a choice flow holds outside its option loops, read from the fact record before anything is a gap row: `toggleGroups` (two or more pressable buttons under one parent, the condition one of them reveals), `summaryOf` (with no aside, the live line naming two questions' picks and the panel holding it), `stageNoteOf` (the one text after the last control) and `canvasOf` (every other worded element with no control, the highest such unless a loose control sits beside it, before or after the flow by document order; a lone heading the title) |
| `lib/skeleton-choice-flow-tree.mjs` | FR-48-6 `rules.choiceFlow`: `flowNodes` composes the model into `sgs/form-step` > `sgs/choice-flow-question` / fields + `sgs/choice-flow-result` under the `sgs/choice-flow` root and returns `{ nodes, slots, notes }`: each node made from a rendered draft element carries it (`draftRef` and fingerprint: a question its container, a panel's step the panel's element, a result part's result its root, a field its control, also its input part slot), a question's own pieces are its `draftSlots` and the flow root's are `slots` (`lib/skeleton-choice-flow-refs.mjs`); one draft element is one node's (a question's step and a panel's result have none of their own), and an element the opened page never rendered gives nothing; `canvasSiblings` builds each canvas group with the ordinary rules as a top-level block before or after the flow, in place of the rows the flow absorbed for it; `settleFlowRows` names the covered piece on each absorbed row and makes every other element with words or a control a gap row (`unresolved`, FR-48-34) |
| `lib/skeleton-form-steps.mjs` | FR-48-57: `formStepNodes` makes a form surface's skeleton `sgs/form` > one `sgs/form-step` per draft schema step with fields (label from the draft; the rendered field nodes go into the first) and an `sgs/form-review` where the schema has a step with no fields; called once by `skeleton-propose.mjs`. |
| `lib/form-submit-page.mjs` | The in-page half of a live form submission, shared by `form-submit-check.mjs` and `site-check.mjs`: filling a rendered form's reachable required controls (a tile or chip through its label; never the honeypot) and, with a marker, its empty free-text fields, then walking its steps with the form's own Next to the submit button, stopping with a named error on a stuck step, scoped to one form's selector. `MAX_STEPS` (14) is the most steps the in-page walk takes before it throws. |
| `lib/skeleton-form-step-parts.mjs` | FR-48-57: the parts `formStepNodes` reads by: `stepChrome` (the step list, count, Next and Back by `rules.formSteps`; a node holding a field is never one), `navRole` (a button is Next or Back by its words or click handler, the one test the skeleton and `lib/fill-form-buttons.mjs` share), `pairFields`, `schemaFieldNode` (a schema field's block by its type through `rules.formControl`, else `rules.formSteps.schemaTypes`; its label, required mark, help text and `fieldName` where the block holds them; a type no rule names is a note, never a guess), `boundKey` and `boundCopy`. `isField(n)` tells whether a node is an `sgs/form-field-*` block. |
| `lib/site-check-report.mjs` | `site-check.mjs`'s eight checks, each check's verdict from its items (an unknown item status is a fail), `--only` parsing, the exit code and `result.md`. |
| `lib/site-check-site.mjs` | Where `site-check.mjs` points: the site's calibration target or secrets file (only `WP_URL_*` is read, from the repo or a worktree's main checkout), the page map's ids for that site (a mirror shares its test site's), the rows it loads and the client the scripted flows belong to. |
| `lib/site-check-nav.mjs` | `site-check.mjs`'s navigation checks: the header links against the page map, draft-file links, mega menus and the drawer, each opened and closed by the block's own behaviour. |
| `lib/site-check-coming-soon.mjs` | `site-check.mjs`'s coming-soon gate: the in-page test for WooCommerce's holding page replacing the site, and the check records it gives (one failure naming `woocommerce_coming_soon=yes` and the fix command, the other checks n-a). `COMING_SOON_ROOT` is the selector for WooCommerce's holding page; `comingSoonInPage(selector)` runs in the page and tells whether the holding page replaces the site; `storeVisibleCommand(client, site)` returns the `site-build.mjs --store-visible --apply` command that fixes it; `comingSoonChecks({ ids, url, client, site, screenshot })` returns every check's record for a site in coming-soon mode (the failure on `pages`, else on the first check run; the rest n-a or skipped). |
| `lib/site-build-store-visible.mjs` | FR-48-55 part 6: WooCommerce's coming-soon switch for a shop client (`woocommerce_coming_soon` `yes` -> `no`), planned by the whole build and run alone by `site-build.mjs --store-visible`. `COMING_SOON_OPTION` (`woocommerce_coming_soon`) and `STORE_PAGES_ONLY_OPTION` (`woocommerce_store_pages_only`) are the two options read; `isShopClient(rows)` tells whether any row is a shop post type; `comingSoonAction(value)` returns the one action a site in coming-soon mode needs, else null; `storeVisiblePhp()` returns the read's PHP; `readStoreVisible(runPhp)` runs it and returns both options; `planStoreVisible(rows, read)` returns `{ actions, options, notes }`; `runStoreVisible({ rows, runPhp, apply, log })` prints the plan and, with `apply`, writes the option and fails unless it reads `no` (exit 0 done or nothing to do, 1 the read-back disagrees). |
| `lib/site-check-parts.mjs` | `site-check.mjs`'s part checks: finding the modals, choice flows and forms a page renders and proving each works, and the WooCommerce cart, checkout and account pages. |
| `lib/form-submit-run.mjs` | The pure parts of `form-submit-check.mjs`: the form post's settings and field copy, test values by control type, the success verdict, the original recipient of a redirected test mail, a target's site folder and SSH endpoint from `deploy_common.py` (`targetSite`, `sshEndpoint`), the checked `--form` slug (`formSlug`) and a LIKE-escaped value (`likeLiteral`). |
| `lib/skeleton-write-record.mjs` | The proposal writer's row book and the subtrees a block draws itself: a row per element, removed and absorbed subtrees, and the width variations of a drawn block (split out of `lib/skeleton-propose.mjs`). |
| `lib/skeleton-write-units.mjs` | The proposal writer's units of siblings that are one block (a typed brand name, a drawer's icon run, a stacked list of text links) and the nodes built from them. |
| `lib/skeleton-write-content.mjs` | The proposal writer's content, identity and structure settings for one element's block, and the ids the build carries for it (a logo's `logoId`, a menu's `ref`).  `ownWordsNode(e, block)` is the text child (the tag map's `p` block) that carries a container's own words in DOM order (before its children when the markup opens with the text); a blockquote's body is that child and its cite the attribution. A button with no href resolves through its click handler like a `#` link. `levelSetting(facts, block)` is a text block's heading-level setting (its tag-identity setting whose values are heading tags, from the framework DB); `contentAttrs` writes the draft heading tag into it. |
| `lib/skeleton-write-slots.mjs` | The proposal writer's header rows (`rules.headerSlots`), footer rows (`rules.footerSlots`) and the host slot of an unwrapped element. |
| `lib/skeleton-review.mjs` | Skeleton step 3: the review table (key, tag, words, code, screenshot, proposed block, confidence, decision). |
| `lib/skeleton-finalise.mjs` | Client decisions and finaliser picks on disk (with the element signature that makes a stale decision visible), the finalise request for the low rows and the validated merge of the picks. |
| `lib/fill-read.mjs` | Reads the draft in Chromium through the walker's `collectPair` and `resolveFinder` at 375, 768, 1440 and the fluid widths, plus the 16px sweep; with a state opener (`lib/fill-open.mjs`) every page is read with the surface's state open, a width the surface does not render at is not read, and a state that rendered nowhere leaves every read missing; blocks or mirrors requests beyond the draft's origin. Its helpers' `goto` sets a `#@state/page=<route>` url's route on the draft (`scripts/parity/lib/helpers.mjs`: `routed` and `route()`, which the walker's load-entrance reload also calls, `scripts/parity/lib/entrances.mjs::sampleEntrances`). `TIE_PROPS` (`background-clip`) lists the properties read only so the resolver can tell apart settings that all paint one property by what else each paints (`lib/resolve-side-effects.mjs`); they are never resolved or written from the computed read. |
| `lib/fill-read-collect.mjs` | The in-page half of Fill's draft read (`lib/fill-read.mjs::readDraft`): `collectMany` runs inside the draft page through `page.evaluate` and collects every job's pair snapshot with the walker's `collectPair`, plus the exact border box, whether the element takes part in flow, its own words and link, and a block's own text styles. |
| `lib/fill-declared.mjs` | FR-48-8: what the draft's code declares on each target beyond Fill's list, read rendered too, and the declared value a used-value property (height, offsets, grid tracks) takes. |
| `lib/calibrate-probe.mjs` | FR-48-81: calibration's probe trigger. A setting state with no dedicated trigger (disabled, closed and the rest) is entered by the first state-vocabulary mechanism (an attribute set, a class or BEM modifier added, a pseudo-class forced) that changes the setting's property, on the target or an ancestor up to the instance root; every mechanism is undone. |
| `lib/state-vocab.mjs` | FR-48-81, FP-1: the state vocabulary as intake and calibration read it, from the framework DB's `states` table (an error when the table is missing): the forceable pseudo-classes and the motion inventory's trigger type for a pseudo-class. |
| `lib/state-check.mjs` | FR-48-81, FR-48-46: whether the live page shows each state look the draft shows. Both pages are read by `readStates`; every paired element's draft state values are compared with the live values in the same state (or the live resting value where the live state changes nothing) by property type with the confirm tolerances; a row whose look was read by code (`read: "code"`) compares declared values and carries `confirmed: false`. |
| `lib/state-live.mjs` | FR-48-81, FR-48-46: the live side of the state check. Solve runs it after its last walk: the draft and the built page are opened as the pairing command opens them, every pair of the walker config is read by `readStates` in each walker state it names (a pair naming none in every state, as the walker walks it: rest and each later state the config opens on both sides; a state with `widths` only at those) at 375, 768 and 1440 on both sides, with the state map's `set-state` rows' logic looks (`readLogicStates`) joined to them (the live resting value of every property the draft states change too), and `checkStates` compares them; `timingCheck` adds a row per transition timing the draft declares; the report lists every row that differs or could not be read, and marks the unconfirmed ones. |
| `lib/state-map.mjs` | FR-48-13: the state map generated from the draft. For one surface, every `sc-if` of the draft template is mapped to a way of reaching it (a walker state naming its draft state, the logic's state set directly, or the route, width, props or content that shows it) or listed as a gap row naming the condition; each walker state is listed with its setting state; `diffReasons` compares the map with `scripts/parity/flows/state-map-reasons.json`. Pure functions. |
| `lib/state-map-io.mjs` | FR-48-13: the files the state map reads: the committed reasons (refused when a conflict entry names no client), a client's `surfaces.json` and intake record, and the conflict entries one client's surface carries (the groups Solve refuses). |
| `lib/walker-states.mjs` | Spec 48 §3.6, FR-48-13: the walker states a generated config walks: every state the surface's `surfaces.json` entry maps, opened by the record state it names (`lib/state-openers.mjs::stateWays`, over the width keys Fill's opener reads), at the widths it holds at; the live side scrolls for a scroll-reached setting state, opens the live root's `aria-controls` control for another mapped one, and does nothing for a rest state; a state with no record state, or no way and no width bound, is refused as a gap row. |
| `lib/walker-check.mjs` | Spec 48 §3.6, FR-48-13: the walker check `state-map.mjs --check-walkers` runs: every walker file a surface names exists and walks every state the surface maps. A content-only post surface (`lib/site-build-plan.mjs::isContentOnlySurface`) whose walker files are all absent is printed as a `listed` line ("content-only: walked only with --with-content"), never a fault; one whose files exist is checked like any other. |
| `lib/fill-state.mjs` | FR-48-81: Fill's state pass. Each target's state looks are written through the setting that holds that state (`block_attributes.css_state`), a first pass from the code beside the resting pass; focus that paints like hover is left to the hover setting; a look read from the stylesheet (`read: "code"`) is written with its declared values and tagged `read: "code"`; an open look of a modal, nav drawer or mega panel hidden at rest is that surface's resting look; a focus, press or link-state look the theme holds goes to the snapshot patch (`lib/fill-state-tokens.mjs`); every other look (a different focus property, a press on another block, a combination, a state no setting holds) is a named gap. |
| `lib/fill-state-tokens.mjs` | FR-48-81, Spec 32 FR-32-19: Fill's theme-token writes. A focus look unlike hover (outline and box-shadow), a press look on a block that reads `buttonPresets`, and a `:link`, `:any-link` or `:visited` look on a plain content link become keys of one patch in `theme-snapshot.json`'s own shape (`settings.custom.focus` with source "draft", `settings.custom.buttonPresets.<variant>.active-*`, `styles.elements.link[':<state>']`), written by `fill.mjs` as `theme-snapshot.patch.json` beside its other outputs. A key two targets set differently keeps the first value and the later one is an UNMAPPED conflict; the mappers' gaps are UNMAPPED rows. |
| `lib/fill-state-focus.mjs` | FR-48-81: keyboard focus beside a written hover look. Every SGS hover setting also paints `:focus-visible`; where the draft keeps an element's resting look on focus, the block's `sgsFocusRests` switch is written once per node, and where the draft's focus look equals its hover look nothing is written. |
| `lib/fill-state-script.mjs` | FR-48-81: the looks a draft sets from its script. A pointer or focus handler (`onMouseEnter`, `onFocus`) changes state that the element's `{{ }}` style holes read, so no stylesheet rule names the look; this reader fires the handler's DOM event the way the draft's React runtime listens for it, reads each copy of a repeated element on its own, then fires the leave event and restores the draft's state. |
| `lib/fill-shop-parts.mjs` | FR-48-60 to FR-48-62: Fill's reader of the shop parts record (`sites/<client>/build/shop-parts.json`, written by `shop-parts.mjs`). A part whose home is a block's settings and that carries the draft's own value (the literal label of a checkout, remove or empty-basket part) is written onto every node of that block in the surface as a content write (`how: 'shop-part'`); a setting the block's schema lacks, and a value another writer already put on the node that differs from the draft's, are rows, never written silently; a part whose block is not in this surface is a note. |
| `lib/fill-timing.mjs` | FR-48-81: the transition timings a draft declares (`transition-duration`, `transition-timing-function`) written into the block's role-motion settings with CSS direction semantics: the resting rule times both directions, a state rule times entering that state, closing reverses opening; and the check that the live page times each change as the draft does. |
| `lib/fill-state-apply.mjs` | FR-48-81: the state reader's in-page helpers: entering and leaving one state token on its host (an attribute, a class, a dialog or popover opened, the page scrolled), restoring what was there (a popover open at rest stays open; one another call light-dismissed is reopened), and reading a target's rendered values. |
| `lib/snapshot-tokens.mjs` | FR-48-81, FR-32-19: the pure mappers from a draft's theme-level looks to the snapshot keys that carry them. A keyboard-focus look becomes `settings.custom.focus` keys (`width`, `ring`, `offset`, `near`, `far`, `bloom`, and `source: "draft"`, which `check-snapshot-focus-tokens.py` records instead of failing on 3:1); a button's `:active` look becomes `buttonPresets.{variant}.active-transform` and `active-shadow`, the only press roles `includes/helpers-button-press.php` paints. Whatever has no token (a dashed outline, a hard shadow ring, a pressed colour) is a named gap. A plain content link's `:link`, `:any-link` and `:visited` look becomes `styles.elements.link[':visited']` (`color.text`, `color.background`, `typography`), written only for an `<a href>` that is not button-styled and not owned by a block setting. Not yet wired into `fill.mjs`. |
| `lib/fill-state-read.mjs` | FR-48-81: each target's state looks. Every draft rule whose selector carries a state the framework vocabulary names (pseudo-class, ARIA or native attribute, state class, data attribute) is entered on the element that carries it, then the target's declared values and their render are read and the page restored; a state the page cannot enter (`:visited`, a state inside `:has()`, a sibling host) is read from its stylesheet rules by `lib/fill-state-code.mjs` and marked `read: "code"`; only a combination whose rules declare nothing is listed as unreached, with the reason. |
| `lib/fill-state-combos.mjs` | FR-48-81: the state combinations a draft's stylesheets hold. `tokenSource` turns a vocabulary row's token into the regex source that finds it in a selector, `readableRows` keeps the rows the reader can enter, and `stateCombos` (run in the page) lists each target's combinations with the host of every token and, for one the page cannot enter, the declarations of the rules behind it. |
| `lib/fill-shop-targets.mjs` | FR-48-60 to FR-48-62: the draft elements of the shop parts, found by their `locators`.
