- `imageRecords`: The image rows of the confirm record (FR-48-36): one per image Fill mapped, read at the desktop width, for the live check to settle with paintedImageCheck. images: Fill's `images` list ({ node, index, block, attr, idAttr, id, url, asset, ratio, alt }). Draft side and declared side are both the draft's own file (the media map entry), so D–R agrees by construction; the live half decides.

### `lib/brand-registry.mjs`

- `REGISTRY_FILE` → the registry's path. `brandRegistry(file?)` → its brands in file order. `brandBySlug(slug, brands?)` → one brand or null.
- `brandDomains(brand)` → the domain names a social brand's addresses live on (its slug and the name inside its `socials.<name>` Site Info key).
- `brandNames(brand)` → the names a brand goes by in a token name: its slug and Site Info name (three letters or more), its label's words and the registry's `aliases`.
- `brandOfToken(name, brands?)` → the brand a colour token is named for (one of its names is a whole word of the token name; a brand carrying a colour or a review block), or null.
- `reviewPlatformBlocks(db?)` → the `sgs/<brand>-reviews` block slugs in the framework DB (opened read-only when no connection is passed).
- `paletteBrands(brands?, blocks?)` → the registry's brands plus one per review-platform block whose brand the registry lacks, carrying `reviewBlock` (Spec 48 FR-48-31).
- `brandOwnsAddress(brand, address)` → whether the address is that brand's own: `tel:` for phone, `mailto:` for email, a host `<domain>.<tld>` (or a subdomain of it) for a social; a brand with no Site Info key, address, short links (`wa.me`) and country second-level domains are not claimed.

### `lib/fill-handover.mjs`
- `HANDOVER_OWNERS`: re-exported from `lib/issue-classes.mjs`, the one definition Solve and Fill share. `HANDOVER_KINDS`: `text`, `presence`, `link`, `behaviour`.
- `handoverEntry(entry)` → a validated entry; throws on an unknown owner or kind, because a handover nobody owns is the failure the list exists to prevent.
- `handoverProblems(list, label)` → problems in a skeleton's declared handover list.
- `handoverCounts(entries)` → the count per owner, every owner present.

### `lib/fill-page.mjs`
- `INITIAL`: the CSS initial values of the inherited properties a theme rarely sets.
- `lineHeightPx(lh, fontPx)` → px for a ratio or a px value.
- `pageBaseline(raw, tag)` → `{ value(prop, width, ownFontPx), own(prop) }`, the inherited baseline from the theme snapshot.

### `lib/fill-entrance.mjs` (Playwright)
- `STEP_MS`, `JITTER_MS`, `FRAME_MS`: 50, 40 and 16, the probe's rounding step, jitter tolerance and one frame.
- `SWEEP`: the page sweep (first scroll after 1.5s, 0.7 of the viewport every 700ms, 2.5s settle).
- `sampleEntrances(options)` → the raw probe results by job id (`sweep: null` keeps the page still; `opener` (`lib/fill-open.mjs::stateOpener`) with a state is applied through `openWith`, default `openState`, once the page has loaded). `sampleWidths(options)` → `{ id: { 375, 768, 1440: shaped entrance or null } }`. `sampleMotion(options)` → `{ entrances, loops: { id: { width: { loops, box, marquee, smil } } }, opened: { width: the opener's verdict } }` from the same page loads; a width whose opener verdict is `ok: false` samples nothing (`sampleEntrances` hands the verdict to `onOpen`).
- `translationOf(pose)` → `{ x, y }` in px. `scaleOf(pose)` → the uniform scale. `rotateOf(pose)` → the rotation in degrees (matrix angle plus the `rotate` property). `shapeEntrance(raw)` → `{ trigger, delayMs, durationMs, delayRawMs, durationRawMs, distancePx, scale, … }` or null.

### `lib/fill-motion.mjs`
- `writeNode(n, doc, pending, { flat, ref, schema, out, reads })`: validates one node's sgsMotion document and writes it, or raises one UNMAPPED `motion` row `sgsMotion refused: ` followed by each schema error and each refusal's text (a refusal is `{ id, text, layers }`), never the object.
- `COVERAGE_WRITES`: the coverage IDs whose register entry names this module as its writer, listed one per line and sorted; `coverageProblems` refuses a declared ID whose entry names another module and an entry naming this module that is not listed.
- `motionWrites(options)` → `{ writes, unmapped, notes }`: every node's `sgsMotion`, validated by `lib/motion-validate.mjs`.
- `targetName(node, target, targets)` → the motion target a skeleton target is: the block's root for its own element, the declared part a slot names, or null. `motionWrites` also takes `reads`: a hover transform only the state look finds (no inventory record reaches the element) is written as the same hover layer (`lib/fill-state-motion.mjs`).
- `elementMotion(records, options)` → one target's entrance and hover layers; with `options.offers` and `options.target` an animation the draft starts on a state change becomes a `state` or `open` entrance (`lib/motion-states.mjs`), a transition on a published state a state layer, and an SVG `<set>` (`lib/motion-set.mjs`) a held load entrance; none of these is render-checked at load. `elementEffects(records, options)` → one target's effects from its loop records, the run-time loop records its measured animation is, its `<marquee>` and its SVG animation elements. `renderRefutes(sampled)` is true when the element was found and played no entrance at any width, so the render wins and nothing is written.
- `elementKey(finder, rootComponent)`, `recordsByElement(records)`, `partForSlot(block, slot, targets)`.
- `adoptDrawnBehaviours`: A recognised behaviour whose element has no target of its own (an element a block draws, such as a nav item's label inside the nav block) is keyed under its nearest template ancestor that is a target, so the block that draws the element writes it. `targetKeys`: the element keys of every target; `template`: lib/fill-behaviour.mjs::templateOf; `isBehaviour( r )`.
- `sameTriggerResolved`: A record whose trigger detail only says "Same as A-12" takes that record's trigger (its type, detail and state keys). A chain of "Same as" is followed to its end (a cycle stops), and a field the record states itself (not null, not empty) is laid over the referenced trigger.
- `elementMotion`, `elementEffects` and `renderRefutes` are re-exported from `lib/fill-motion-element.mjs`.

### `lib/fill-motion-element.mjs`
- `elementMotion(records, options)` → one target's entrance and hover layers; with `options.offers` and `options.target` an animation the draft starts on a state change becomes a `state` or `open` entrance (`lib/motion-states.mjs`), a transition on a published state a state layer, and an SVG `<set>` (`lib/motion-set.mjs`) a held load entrance; none of these is render-checked at load.
- `elementEffects(records, options)` → one target's effects from its loop records, the run-time loop records its measured animation is, its `<marquee>` and its SVG animation elements. `renderRefutes(sampled)` is true when the element was found and played no entrance at any width, so the render wins and nothing is written.
- `snapLayer(layer, fieldOf, ctx, prefer)` → every duration, easing and distance of a layer's values (and its device overrides) as a token reference where one equals it. `paintLayer(paint, ctx)` → the hover layer's `paintDuration` and `paintEasing` from `lib/fill-timing.mjs::paintTimings` (unset, the layer's paint runs on the client's `transition.fast`), and `shadowDuration` and `shadowEasing` where the draft times its box-shadow other than its paint at any width (both halves, a narrower tier's duration as a device override); `unmapped` rows are `{ field, reason }`.

### `lib/fill-motion-stagger.mjs`
- `staggerOf(record)` → the stagger a record's delay states (`{ offset, step, cap }`), `{ refused }` for an index formula the helper cannot hold, or null when it states no per-item delay.
- `staggerPlans({ records, template, nodes, keyOf })` → one plan per list entrance with a per-item delay; `applyStagger({ plans, doc, own, where, out, pending, emArgs })` → writes the plans held at one root target: the stagger and the items' entrance on the root, or a named unmapped row.

### `lib/motion-layers.mjs`
- `asPlayed(record)` → a one-shot record as it plays (a reverse run swaps from and to, mirrors its keyframe offsets and its easing; a curve with no mirror is kept as `easingUnmirrored` and named by `entranceLayer`). `mirrorEasing(easing)` → E'(x) = 1 - E(1 - x) as a CSS easing, or null.
- `classify(record)` → `entrance`, `hover`, `effect` (an endless or repeating animation) or `skip` with the owning wave. `entranceLayer(record, mdb, measured, named)`, `hoverLayer` → layers; `named` (`lib/motion-states.mjs::stateTrigger`) makes the entrance a `state` (with its state name) or `open` one. `stateTransitionLayer(record, mdb, named)` → the `entrance.base` of a transition on a published state: its poses (none for a stylesheet transition) over its duration, delay and easing.
- `poseDistance`, `matchByPose`, `delaySteps` (a per-item delay's offset and step), `delayOf`. `entranceLayer` writes `values.keyframes` (one entry per distinct offset, offsets in percent, at most 8) when a property carries more than two keyframes, and refuses a record whose stop value the code computes.

### `lib/motion-states.mjs`
- `stateTrigger(record, offers, target)` → `{ trigger: "open" }` for a state change whose words are open words on a part the block opens, else `{ trigger: "state", state }` for a state the block publishes (the longest name the words spell, else the one published state ending in an open word), else null. `offers`: `{ names, opens, openWords }` from `lib/motion-db.mjs::statesOf` and `::opensOf`.
- `openWords(rows)` → the words that mean a disclosure is open: `open` and the last word of each attribute or class token the `states` table lists for it, less any word that names another state.

### `lib/motion-set.mjs`
- `COVERAGE_WRITES`: the coverage IDs whose register entry names this module as its writer, listed one per line and sorted; `coverageProblems` refuses a declared ID whose entry names another module and an entry naming this module that is not listed.
- `setRecord(sampledSet, index)` → `{ record }` (an entrance record: opacity from the value before to the value set, duration 0, the begin time as its delay) or `{ reason }` for a `<set>` that begins on an event, reverts, switches another attribute or changes nothing. `lib/fill-entrance.mjs::sampleMotion` samples a `<set>` with its begin, end, fill and the attribute it overrides (`base`).

### `lib/motion-check.mjs`

- `entranceCheck(layer, record, sampled)` → `{ devices, differences }`: per width the duration and delay (overrides) and the from-pose (translate, scale, rotate, opacity) and easing (reported only), and the pose sampled at 0, half and all of the run (`lib/motion-sample.mjs`, reported only); `tolerance(ms)` (max of 100ms and 10%) is how far a measured time may sit before the render overrides it; `WIDTH_TIER`: 375 mobile, 768 tablet, 1440 desktop.

### `lib/motion-channels.mjs`
- `lengthOf`, `angleOf` (a length as px or its unit string, an angle in degrees); `round`; `transformChannels`, `filterChannels`, `propertyChannels`, `poseOf` (the from and to channels of a record's properties); `IDENTITY` (each channel's resting value).
- `keyframeStops(properties)` → null when no property has more than two keyframes, else `{ stops: [{ offset (percent), channels }], unsupported, other, computed }`.
- `transformChannels` also reads a flat `matrix()`/`matrix3d()` (translate, scale, rotate; a skew is reported), `rotate3d()` about one axis, and `perspective()` (returned as `perspective`). `shadowParts(value)` → `{ inset, x, y, blur, spread, colour }` of a single box-shadow; `colourHex(value)` → lowercase hex for a hex or `rgb()` colour.

### `lib/motion-effects.mjs`
- `COVERAGE_WRITES`: the coverage IDs whose register entry names this module as its writer, listed one per line and sorted; `coverageProblems` refuses a declared ID whose entry names another module and an entry naming this module that is not listed.
- `writeEffect(effect, { mdb, schema, offers })` → `{ target, list, layer, dropped }` or `{ refused }`. `placeEffect(effect, { schema, offers })` → `{ target }` (a block-level effect found on a part goes on the root) or `{ refused }`.
- `loadEffectOffers()` → `{ qualifying, elementsOf(block) }` from `generated-fx-qualifying-blocks.json` and each block.json; `levelsForTarget(target, elements)`, `kindsFit(meta, target, elements)` (the editor's `refusal-match.js` rules); `PANEL_LISTS`: each panel's list, DB layer and schema layer.

### `lib/motion-loop-css.mjs`
- `loopKeyframesCss(record, name)` → `{ css, stops, error }` or `{ unmapped }`; `loopName(record)` (`sgs-loop-<record id>`), `isLoopRecord(record)` (an endless loop with literal keyframes); `bezierOf(easing)` (`[]` for linear, `null` for a curve no cubic-bezier states), `easedProgress(bezier, t)`.
- `lib/fill-motion-element.mjs::elementEffects` returns such a loop as `css` when `chooseEffect` reports it unmapped; `motionWrites` writes it to `sgsCustomCss`, `sgsCustomCssId` (`loop` plus the record id's letters and digits, at most 16, the key `includes/custom-css.php::sgs_custom_css_uid` accepts) and `sgsCustomCssNote` of the block root, adding nothing the box already holds, refuses a hex colour loop, and reports it unmapped on a declared part.

### `lib/motion-loops.mjs`
- `COVERAGE_WRITES`: the coverage IDs whose register entry names this module as its writer, listed one per line and sorted; `coverageProblems` refuses a declared ID whose entry names another module and an entry naming this module that is not listed.
- `loopEffect(record, measuredByWidth, { mdb })`, `chooseEffect(loop, narrow, { mdb })` → `{ effect, values, devices, trigger, notes }` or `{ unmapped }`; `LOOP_BUILDERS`: marquee, ambient-float, svg-loop, ken-burns.
- `loopCheck(record, measuredByWidth)` → one line per width where the measured loop differs from the code's duration or keyframes, `[]` when every width agrees.
- `resolvedLoop(record, measured)` (values from the code, else the measured animation; random values refused), `measuredLoopOf(record, measured)`, `loopFamilies(loop)`, `runtimeLoopsFor(candidates, measured)`; `smilLoop(animate, box)`, `marqueeTag(attrs)`.

### `lib/motion-sample.mjs`
- `sampledPoseDifferences(width, record, samples)` → one line per sampled fraction and channel (opacity, the composed transform) off the code's pose, a line when an animated entrance had nothing to sample, `[]` for an older probe; `SAMPLE_FRACTIONS`.
- `codePoseAt(record, f)` → `{ opacity, transform, animates }` the code states at fraction f (a Web Animation eased over the run, a CSS animation per keyframe stretch, a reverse run backwards); `easingAt(easing, x)` (linear, the keywords, `cubic-bezier()`; null otherwise); `lerpTransform(from, to, p)` (function by function, null where CSS interpolates matrices); `measuredTransform(pose, animates)`.

### `lib/css-matrix.mjs`
- `transformMatrix(value)` → the 16 numbers (column-major) of a transform list, or null; `sameMatrix(a, b, { tol, tolPx })` → whether two matrices draw the same.

### `lib/motion-clip.mjs`
- `clipChannels(value)` → a clip-path `inset()` as the entrance clip channel (`clip`, and `clipEdge` for one inset edge), any other shape unsupported.

### `lib/motion-shadow.mjs`
- `shadowParts(value)` → `{ inset, x, y, blur, spread, colour }` of a single box-shadow, or null (its colour is turned to hex by `lib/tokens.mjs::colourHex`).

### `lib/motion-db.mjs`
- `motionDb(db)` → `{ targetsOf(block), statesOf(block), opensOf(block), field(layer, path, effect), presets(panel), effect(slug), effects() }`; `statesOf`/`opensOf` read `motion_targets.motion_support` (the named motion states a block publishes, the parts it opens). `fieldKey(layer, fieldPath)`.

### `lib/motion-tokens.mjs`
- `COVERAGE_WRITES`: the coverage IDs whose register entry names this module as its writer, listed one per line and sorted; `coverageProblems` refuses a declared ID whose entry names another module and an entry naming this module that is not listed.
- `tokenTypes(schema)`, `clientTokens(rawSnapshot)`, `clientPalette(rawSnapshot)` (palette hex to slug, so a palette colour is written by its slug and a one-off colour stays hex), `tokenises(field, fieldType)` (durations, curves and travel only), `snapToken(value, fieldType, ctx)`, `presetFor(layer, presets)` (a pose channel at identity counts as absent), `intensityPresetFor(layer, presets)` (an open or state layer that holds the travel an intensity preset stamps), `effectPresetFor(layer, presets)` (an intensity preset whose stamped values the effect layer holds); `msOf`, `pxOf`, `easingOf`.

### `lib/fill-config.mjs`
- `walkerPairs(nodes, tree)` → `{ ref, draft }` per node carrying a `draftRef`.
- `baseConfigText(options)` → the base config file's text.
- `walkerConfigs(options)` → `{ baseFile, baseText, fullFile, fullText }`.

### `lib/fill-report.mjs`
- `unmappedList(unmapped)` → property, value, node and reason (plus block and element, and a `no-setting` row's `code`, `checks` and `unchecked`, which `lib/gap-classes.mjs::mapResolverGap` classes it by) — the framework work for the surface, known before the first build.
- `fillReport(r)` → `{ markdown, json }`; `r.alreadyHeld` rows are listed under `## Already held` by reason with counts (`counts.alreadyHeld` in the JSON) and are not in UNMAPPED.
- `SETTLED`: why a value Fill read ends with nothing written and nothing unwritten (`equal`, `already-set`, `used-size`). `READ_FAILED`: the property an UNMAPPED row names for a failed draft read; `readFailureRows({ reads, nodes, flat, refOf })` → each failed read as an UNMAPPED row with its reason.
- `supersededWrites(writes)` → `Map( index -> index of the write that replaced it )`: a write whose every place is changed again by a later write on the same node and setting stays in `writes` marked `supersededBy` and is not counted.
- `passSettled(before, after, reason, answered)`: the UNMAPPED rows a later pass (`fill-logo-size.mjs`, `fill-width-ladder.mjs`, `fill-dimensions.mjs`) answered, as settled rows naming the pass, one per entry of its `answered` list with the read widths it answers; a row taken out without an answer stays a dropped read.

### `lib/css-families.mjs`
- `familyOf(prop, { dir })` → `{ short, kind: 'side' | 'corner' | 'axis' | 'mixed', part, members }` or null, from mdn-data; `settingKey(prop)` → the shorthand a setting stores the longhand under (edge and corner families and the row/column gap pair), else the property; `reachedBy(prop)` → every name a stored `css_property` can match it by; `storedKeys(stored)` → a stored name and its physical spelling; `isCssProperty(name)`.
- `physicalOf(prop, dir)` → a logical longhand as its physical one (`ltr` default, `rtl` flips the inline edges); `expand(prop, dir)` → the physical longhands a property sets (a logical shorthand too); `membersOf(short)` → a family's members in CSS order.
- `foldParts(short, parts)` → `{ equal, value, css, distinct, missing }`, the shorthand serialisation of the parts; `unfoldValue(short, css)` → the parts of a plain 1 to 4 value shorthand, null for an elliptical one; `completePaint(paint)` → a default paint with each family completed both ways; `partKey(token)` → the key a box setting stores a part under.
- `canonicalName(prop)` → the standard property a name stands for (a legacy alias or vendor-prefixed name as its standard property); `shorthandsOf(prop, dir?)` → every shorthand whose longhands include a longhand, nearest first; `initialOf(prop)` → the initial value as CSS text, or null; `isInherited(prop)`; `keywordsOf(prop)` → the keywords the property's own grammar lists at its top level.

### `lib/css-equivalence/index.mjs`
- `EQUIVALENCE_HELD`: the held reasons the engine gives (`lib/fill-held.mjs` lists held rows under their reason).
- `consult(input, ctx)` → `{ verdict: 'write', writes, layer, detail }`, a held verdict (`default` or `inert`) or a named home, for `input` = `{ slot, prop, state, perWidth, siblings, parent, rest, anyIndex }` and `ctx` = `{ calibration, db }`. `timingHome` and `TIMING_LONGHANDS` are re-exported from `effect.mjs`.

### `lib/css-equivalence/keywords.mjs`
- `alignmentCanon(prop, value, ctx)` → `start` or `end` for a `flex-*` value of align-items, align-content or justify-content where the two agree in `ctx` = `{ display, 'flex-wrap', 'flex-direction' }`, else the value; `sameAlignment(prop, a, b, ctx)` → whether two values align to the same edge.

### `lib/css-equivalence/values.mjs`
- `listItems(text)` → the items of a comma list, commas inside a function kept; `easingCurve(v)` → one easing as its curve's four numbers rounded to 4 places.
- `canonical(prop, value)` → a value in its canonical text (a time list in ms, an easing list as curves); `sameCanonical(prop, a, b)` and `sameAtWidths(prop, a, b)` → whether two values, or two per-width maps at every shared width, paint the same.
- `projectLonghand(short, value, longhand)` → the value a shorthand's value gives one of its longhands, or undefined when it cannot be read.

### `lib/css-equivalence/tolerance.mjs`
- `PX_TOL`: half a pixel; `EXACT`: the properties whose lengths compare exactly (letter and word spacing); `NORMAL_ZERO`: the properties whose `normal` paints 0px.
- `paintCanon(prop, v, ctx)` → a value as the browser paints it (`normal` spacing is 0px; an `auto` minimum height is 0px only off a flex or grid item); `toleranceFor(prop, a, b, ctx)` → the px tolerance two values compare within; `samePaint(prop, a, b, ctx)` → whether two computed values paint the same.

### `lib/css-equivalence/inert.mjs`
- `appliesIn(prop, c)` → `{ applies: true | false | null, code }`: whether a property applies to an element in context `c`, from mdn-data's `appliesto` corrected by the specification's own "Applies to" line; `contextAt(own, parent, w)` → the element's context at a width.
- `inertVerdict({ prop, perWidth, own, parent, paint, state })` → `{ inert: true, detail }` when every width paints nothing, else `{ inert: false, detail }` naming why.

### `lib/css-equivalence/effect.mjs`
- `spellingsOf(prop)` → every name the paint of a property can be recorded under; `paintAt(calibration, slot, prop, w, anyIndex)` → the block's calibrated default paint of a property on a part at a width, read through the spelling layer, as `{ value, via }`.
- `measuredHomes(calibration, { slot, prop, state, anyIndex })` → every setting whose measured effect reaches the property on the slot (`via` `property`, `reaches`, `effect` or `discovered`), strongest evidence first; `discoveredWrite(calibration, { slot, prop, perWidth, state, anyIndex })` → a discovered setting value that paints the draft's value at every width the draft states.
- `TIMING_LONGHANDS`: the timing longhands `timingHome` answers for; `timingHome({ block, slot, longhand, state, anyIndex }, { calibration, db })` → which setting holds a timing longhand on a part: calibrated evidence first, then a framework DB row whose `css_property` names the longhand.

### `lib/fill-census.mjs`

- `loadRules(file?)` → `{ unmapped, held }`, the rules of `data/census-classes.json` with each pattern compiled; `censusRun(dir, rules?)` → `{ total, classes, unclassified }` for a Fill run folder (its `unmapped.json` and the `alreadyHeld` rows of `fill-report.json`); each class carries its count, blocks, properties and Spec 48 owner, and a row no rule matches lands in `unclassified`.

### `tools/fixture-completeness.mjs`

- `cssRules(css)` → `[{ selector, body }]` of a stylesheet, at-rules descended into; `varsOf(php, attr)` → the custom properties render.php ties to a setting; `consumers(rulesByBlock, vars)` → the rules that read them; `renderedClasses(cacheDir, block)` → the classes the fixture rendered (the `elements` keys of every site paint); `emitCondition(php, cls)` → the `if (` above the line that emits a class and its attributes; `judge(...)` → one dead setting verdict (`fixture-gap`, `variant-gated` or `unexplained`); `proposals(rows)` → the fixture additions as data; `DEFAULT_CACHE` and `DEFAULT_SRC` are the main checkout cache and the blocks source folder.
- `nameSuffixes(attr)` → the suffixes of a camel-case setting name a helper may build it from, longest first; only those long enough to be specific.
- `statements(text)` → the statements of a PHP source: runs of lines up to one ending in `;`, `{` or `}`, comments dropped.
- `selectorsOf(text, name)` → what a setting's emission statements name (the statements holding its name, those reading a variable its value flows into, and the other variables' definitions) as `{ classes, anchor }`.
- `emission(attr, own, shared, others, contextKey)` → the emitters of a setting: each PHP source that names it or a suffix a helper builds it from, with the classes and anchor its selectors name; the block's own sources first, then `includes/`, then another block's read through block context.

### `lib/fill-held.mjs`
- `makeSettle(st)` → `settle({ unmapped, writes })` → `{ unmapped, alreadyHeld }`: a row a resolver could not place by name (`no-setting`, `shape`) that the element already renders at every width it states is held as `covered` (a written setting whose property includes it, calibration's recorded side effect, or an sgsMotion state carrying the transform, duration or easing), `inherited` (an inherited property on a node with child nodes, equal to its parent) or `default` (the calibrated default paint of the element, a flex or grid item's inline display blockified); `HELD_REASONS`; `heldCounts(held)` → the count per reason.
- `RESOLVER_GAP`: the reasons a resolver gave for a property it could not place; any other reason is a real gap and is never judged here. `elementContext(st, rec, target, w)` → the draft element's context at a width (`parentDisplay`, `position`, `direction`) for the `canon` comparison.
- `slugColour(value, palette)` → a written palette slug as the colour it paints (`primary` → `rgb(10, 126, 168)`), from the site's tokens; any other value unchanged.
- `coveredBySetting(row, values, writes, cal, contextAt, palette)` → whether, at every width the row states, what a written setting paints equals the draft's, compared in the element's context there; a palette slug is compared as its colour.
- `coveredByMotion(row, values, writes, tokens)` → why a root-element row is covered by a written sgsMotion layer, or null: a timing longhand (`transition-duration`, `transition-timing-function`, `transition-delay`) when every entry is a value the root's layers give it (`lib/census-conservation.mjs::layerGives`: duration, paint and shadow durations, easings, delays, `token:` values through the client's tokens), a transform row when one layer's state values carry its channels; a part's row is never covered.

### `lib/pair-scope.mjs`

- `judgePairScope({ draftIn, liveIn, matches, dTexts, lTexts })` → `{ ok, checked, split, why }`: twin containment. A hand pair is a mispair when a matched word inside one element has its twin OUTSIDE the other, meaning the two finders landed on different parts of the page. A size difference alone never counts, so a link wider on one side passes. A word repeating on its own side is skipped, because the matcher may have chosen another occurrence. `pairs.mjs` computes the verdict once into `qa/pairs/<surface>.json` as `handScope` and `scripts/parity/lib/lint.mjs::lintConfig` enforces it, so a mispair is refused before any browser opens. **A surface with no `handScope` has not been judged, which is not the same as passing.** The obvious box-ratio gate was ruled out on evidence: it flags 40 pairs of which at least 12 are legitimate.
- `MIN_CHECKED` → the smallest evidence base a twin-containment verdict is allowed to rest on (3 matched, unrepeated words). Below it `judgePairScope` returns `{ ok: true, judged: false }` rather than a verdict, because one or two words cannot distinguish a mispair from a word the matcher placed elsewhere. The floor is on the evidence base, not on the share that splits: the real `about-step` mispair splits only 1 of 9, so a share threshold would have let it through.

### `lib/issue-classes.mjs`
- `HANDOVER_OWNERS`: who edits content living outside the layout tree (`site-info`, `product-data`, `content-page`, `behaviour`, `woocommerce-text`). Defined here because Solve discovers a handover from a measured row while Fill carries one its skeleton declared, and a list kept in both would drift.
- `VISUAL`: the row kinds counted as a distinct open issue: the painting kinds (`style`, `hover`, `box`) plus `CONTENT_KINDS`. Any other kind is reported, never counted.
- `CONTENT_KINDS`: the row kinds carrying page content (`text`, `presence`).
- `LINK_COVERAGE_PREFIXES`: the key prefixes that make a kind-`auto` row a link-coverage issue (`link-missing`, `link-extra`).
- `normalisePath(path)` → the path with positional selectors (`:nth-of-type(n)`, `:nth-child(n)`) removed.
- `normalisedIssueKey(row)` → `issueKey` over that normalised path. Sweep-over-sweep deltas compare on THIS, never the raw key: a cosmetic walker change that adds a `:nth-of-type(1)` re-keys rows wholesale with no verdict change (18 of `sgs/brand-strip`'s 51 rows did exactly that between two sweeps), which Gate TAIL would read as a wave of closes and opens. `issueKey` itself is deliberately unchanged, because re-keying it would re-key every existing ledger entry.
- `CONTENT`: the class for a content row no Solve class holds (`content`), walked after `UNMAPPED`, which never takes one.
- `isContentRow(x)` → whether a row is page content (text, presence, or link coverage).
- `isIssue(x)` → whether a row is a distinct open issue at all: the one test every reader applies.
- `SOLVE_CLASSES`: Solve's classes for a surviving row, in the order both readers walk them (`hardcode`, `missing`, `unresolved`, `derived`); the first class to hold a key keeps it.
- `UNMAPPED`: the class for a visual row from a walker state the surface maps to no setting state (`unmapped-state`), walked last.
- `issueKey(x)` → `solve-report.mjs::wholePage`'s key: one element and property whatever the width or state (`pair` stands in for a row with no ref).

### `pairs.mjs`
- `runPairing(opts)` → one pairing pass: collects both sides, chooses each block's partner, and returns the kept pairs and the left-out blocks with their reasons.
- `recheckWidths(kept, opts)` → re-reads each kept finder at `RECHECK_WIDTHS`, re-pairing at a width where it fails and keeping a joined finder only when `mergeWidthFinders` allows it.
- `identityPass({ browser, cfg, width, state, origin, kept, left, retarget?, covered?, handByRef?, handInner?, add? })` → `{ counts, mispaired, replaced, uncheckedHand, tagMismatch }`: each origin block paired by its tpl finder and the word matcher's element (generated, or a hand pair on the block root) checked against it on the draft; a hand pair inside a block (`handInner`, from `runPairing`) must measure a draft element inside the block's identity element; an identity element whose tag is not the skeleton fingerprint's is not trusted; `add` false checks without adding blocks. The CLI runs it once per pairing state (`lib/identity.mjs::identityStates`) and reports `unaligned` live blocks with no origin entry; mutates `kept` and `left`.

### `lib/sweep.mjs`
- `latestReport(solveDir, surface)` → the newest `solve-report.json` of a surface (the last timestamp folder holding one), or null.
- `issueRows(report, surface, reportPath)` → `{ rows: [{ key, row }], otherRows }`: one row per distinct style, hover or box issue of the hardcode, missing, unresolved and derived classes, then of Solve's `other` rows from unmapped walker states (class `unmapped-state`, unless a Solve class holds the key) (`wholePage`'s key and own-block filter); `otherRows` counts the non-visual rows.
- `reportStatus(solveDir, surface, sweepStart?)` → `{ file, stale }`: `file` as `latestReport`, and `stale` either null or `{ reason, report, newestRun }` — `incomplete-run` when the surface's newest run folder holds no `solve-report.json` (a Solve that failed part-way, so `file` is an OLDER run's report served as current), or `predates-sweep` when the report predates `sweepStart`. Observed live: `header` failed with a walker `TimeoutError` and its earlier measurement would have been reported as current. `sweepStart` is the epoch ms the SOLVE batch began, never the aggregation's own clock — Solve always writes before the sweep reads, so defaulting it to now would mark every surface stale; omitted, only `incomplete-run` is checked.
- `walkerCaveats(source)` → one caveat per state `findings` entry (`reveal-unfired`) and per `unsettled`: a statement about the instrument, never an issue row, and it changes no count.
- `readWalkerCaveats(reportPath)` → the caveats of a run, read from the `draft-cache-*.json` files beside its report, which is the only place the walker persists `states`.
- `sweepDelta(prev, next)` → the closed and opened rows between two sweeps, counted per `normalisedIssueKey` so siblings differing only by position stay separate findings.
- `aggregate(entries, unmeasured?, date?)` → `{ date, surfaces, unmeasured, total, byClass, rows, stale, caveated }` from `[{ surface, reportPath, report, config?, stale?, caveats? }]` in manifest order. Each surface counts its own rows; on the site a row with a ref, or a block-less row of the same hand config, counts once under the first surface, later ones in its `alsoIn`.

### `sweep.mjs`
- `sweep(surfacesFile, date, out?)` → `{ result, file }`: reads each surface's newest report and writes the sweep file.

### `lib/register-sweep.mjs`
- `STATUSES`, `STATUS_RULES`: the five A4 statuses and their decided rules (the plan's, plus partly measured).
- `GROUPS`, `SITE_WIDE`, `SECTION_SURFACES`: the eight A4 groups by register section, the site-wide section's name, and the sweep surfaces each section is measured by (an empty list: none).
- `registerItems(markdown)` → `[{ ids, section, cells, covers, line, header }]`: every register table row whose first cell holds ids.
- `item.aliasOf` → the id a row's Fix cell points at when the WHOLE cell is nothing but the pointer (`See 17`, `Same as N2B`), else null. Such a row is an alias of another, not independent work, and its `covers` is set to that id so the roll-up stops double-counting it — only the site-wide tables have a `Covers` column, so three rows carried their pointer solely in the Fix cell and were modelled nowhere. An explicit `Covers` entry always wins. The match is deliberately narrow: a Fix cell that merely mentions another row inside prose is its own work.
- `itemKey(item)` → the item's ids joined with ", ".
- `groupItems(items)` → `{ groups: [{ name, items }], ungrouped }`.
- `itemSurfaces(item, items)` → the sweep surfaces an item is measured by (`null`: a section no surface covers); a site-wide item's are those of the items it covers.
- `verdictsFor(item, items, verdicts)` → the verdicts that answer one item.
- `measuredRefs(pairing)` → the refs one surface's pairing report measured (generated pairs and hand-covered blocks).
- `checkStatuses(items, verdicts, sweep, measured?, walks?)` → problems: one valid status per item; a still-open claim cites one exact row (report, ref or pair, path, property) and quotes one of its `values`, or an open diff of a walk report in `walks` (`evidence.walk`, `pair`, `property`, `draft`, `live`), with `evidence.element` tying the item to that element; not walker-measurable never rests on hover alone (a reason naming a behaviour, focus, content or motion kind may mention it); a clean claim with `evidence.path` is judged on that element of the block only; a clean claim cites a report the sweep holds and a ref no row touches (and, with `measured`, one its surfaces measured); a site-wide clean needs every covered item clean and measured.
- `addSweepColumn(markdown, items, verdicts)` → register text with a Sweep column, or that column rewritten when the table already has one (a re-run never stacks a second); every other cell unchanged.
- `bundleGroup(group, items, sweep, measured?)` → one agent's input: items, surfaces (with measured refs), rows and the status rules.

### `register-sweep.mjs`
- `measuredFrom(pairsDir, sweep)` → `{ surface: [refs] }` from the pairing reports present.
- `bundle(registerFile, sweepFile, pairsDir, outDir)` → `{ files, ungrouped }`.
- `merge(registerFile, sweepFile, pairsDir, verdictFiles, out)` → `{ problems, items }`; writes `out` only with no problems.

### `lib/triage.mjs`
- `TRIAGE_CLASSES`: W, F, T, U (a box row nothing explains), L (a row that follows a difference the divergence ledger accepts, citing the entry).
- `issuesOf(report, surface)` → `[{ key, solveClass, rows }]`: distinct hardcode, missing, unresolved and derived issues, then the unmapped-state rows of `other` (a visual row from a walker state the surface maps to no setting state) under `solveClass` `unmapped-state`, in the sweep's class order so a key a Solve class already holds stays under it (`lib/sweep.mjs::issueRows`).
- `emissionOf(cite, props, ctx)` → the classes and pseudo-elements a cited control actually emits to, or null when that cannot be read. Either `ctx.emissionFor(block, setting, property)`, or the PHP index `ctx.helpers`: every `sgs_*` function emitting the property whose file names the setting contributes the `.sgs-*` classes and `::` pseudos in its string literals; a function emitting to the root is skipped.
- `reachesElement(cite, issue, ctx)` → whether that emission can match the row's element. `canvasSettable` and `resolveIssue`'s ancestor hop both credit a control only when it does. An UNKNOWN emission keeps the old credit, because refusing unknowns would turn every such claim into a false F. This stops a container-only control being classed `W/canvas-settable` on form inputs, tab buttons and filter inputs that the control's emitting helper can never paint.
- `reachabilityVerified(cite, issue, ctx)` → whether `reachesElement` actually TESTED this citation or fell through its fail-open branch, stamped onto the citation evidence as `reachabilityVerified`. The fail-open above is deliberate (refusing unknowns would manufacture false F rows) but it means an untested claim and a tested one are indistinguishable in the evidence, and R-47-12 requires the citation be tested before the row is called resolved. **Measured 2026-10-06: `emissionOf` returned null for 17 of 17 citations that a live read then refuted** — so for those rows the gate asserted reachability rather than testing it, which is why every one read `W`. The real repair is to supply `ctx.emissionFor`, which is read here but set by no caller; until then this flag is what separates evidence from assertion. Findings: `.claude/reports/2026-10-06-session-c2/CANVAS-SETTABLE-CONFIRMATION.md`.
- `measuredReachFrom(results)` → the `ctx.measuredReach( block, setting, property )` lookup built from `confirm-canvas.mjs`'s results: `false` for a REFUTED or NO-RULE family read with no skipped stylesheet, otherwise `undefined` (CONFIRMED and VAR-CHANNEL are not proof; ABSENT and NO-URL were never read). `reachesElement` consults it before the source gate, so a measured refutation is final and an unmeasured citation still falls to the fail-open. `triage.mjs::runTriage` reads it from `sites/<client>/build/qa/canvas-confirm.json` when that file exists.
- `nameFits(prop, attr, vocabulary)` → whether a setting's name fits a property (size families, hover effects, background, shorthand words; never a name spelling a longer property ending in it, read by `lib/name-fit.mjs::compoundSpelled` from the CSS property list and `vocabulary`).
- `settingFits(name, setting, prop, values, vocabulary)` → css_property or name fit; visibility toggles only for `none`, unit companions never.
- `rosterApplies(ext, block, supports)` → whether a roster extension reaches a block.
- `delta(row)` → live minus draft in px, or null.
- `anchorOf(walk, row)` → the ref a `y-from-`/`y-after-`/`x-`/`right-` row is placed against, or null.
- `explainRow(row, open, { ancestorsOf, walk })` → the open row it follows, or null. A row measuring this row's own element from an enclosing block (its owner frame: one element is read by every pair it carries text or layout for) is never its parent; a row that IS such a reading follows the element's own row with the same values (`match: 'same-element'`).
- `ledgerParent(issue, ctx)` → `{ check: 'ledger-consequence', entry, parent, match, widths }` for an issue with no open parent whose every row follows a ledger decision: a ledger-accepted row (`ctx.ledgerRows`, stamped `decided.id` by the walker) explaining it by the same amount or as the same element, or a position row placed after a node an entry accepts in full (property `*`); else null. A walker accept (an equivalence rule) or a bare layout row never attributes a row.
- `mispairOf(issue, walk)` → `{ check: 'mispaired', widths, sizes }` when, at every width the issue was read, its pair's draft and live boxes differ more than twice in width or height (the walker compared two different elements), else null; `triageIssue` labels such an issue W `mispaired` before any setting lookup.
- `transientOf(rows)` → transient evidence (a motion property at an entrance start value) or null.
- `usedValueOf(issue, walk)` → used-value evidence (a width or height with no declared plain length) or null.
- `resolveIssue(issue, ctx)` → the resolver read-only as `writeRound` builds it: `{ gap, detail }` or `{ writes, on, holds }`.
- `holdsValue(current, value, merge)` → whether an attribute already holds a write.
- `fittingSettings(issue, ctx)` → attribute, discovered, extension and enclosing evidence (`reaches: false` where calibration shows a setting not reaching the element).
- `triageIssue(issue, ctx)` → `{ key, class, decidedBy, evidence, source? }`. Before any setting lookup: `no-live-element` (live empty at every width), `unmeasured-side` (a side the walker never read at any width, as Solve's classify), `mispaired`, `same-element` (an enclosing block's reading of an open element row), then `L`/`ledger-consequence`. A fitting attribute calibration never measured decides W as `uncalibrated-fit` (its reach is unknown: a calibration gap), never `attribute`.
- `canvasSettable(issue, ctx)` → FR-47-8 (c): the citation for the nearest block already in the canvas that declares the row's property **in the row's state** — enclosing blocks first, then `ctx.canvasBlocks()` — or null. A declaration is a `css_property` match or a modifier of it, never a name that merely reads like the property. A classification only: the route cannot decide which sibling should own the value, so it refuses F and hands the row on with its citation.
- `issueContext(report, walk, ctx)` → the per-issue ctx `triageIssue` and `canvasSettable` read (the walk, its open rows, its ledger-accepted rows (`ledgerRows`) and writable groups, Solve's gaps and writes); `triage` and `confirm-canvas.mjs::candidatesFrom` both build it here.
- `triage(report, walk, surface, ctx)` → `{ verdicts, counts }`.

### `lib/triage-source.mjs`
- `phpSymbols(text, file)` → `{ functions, classes }` one PHP file defines (an `sgs_*` function's body; a class's whole file).
- `helperIndex(files)` → `{ functions, classes, files }` over many files (the first definition wins).
- `callsIn(text)` → `{ functions, classes, requires }` a PHP text calls, uses or requires.
- `helperSources(entries, index, depth?)` → `[{ file, symbol, text }]` the helpers PHP texts reach, nearest first (depth 2 by default).
- `sourcePass(issue, names, ctx)` → `{ render, rules, helpers }`: setting names `render.php` mentions, `style.css` rules declaring the property for the element, and the helpers it reaches that read a setting (quoted key) or emit the property.

### `triage.mjs`
- `phpFiles(dir)` → every PHP file under a folder as `{ file, text }` (plugin-relative paths).
- `finalWalk(reportFile)` → the highest `round-N/report.json` beside a Solve report, or null.
- `treeIndex(buildDir, manifest)` → `{ nodes, ancestors }` for every ref in every surface tree.
- `surfaceContext({ client, surface, report?, measuredReach? })` → `{ buildDir, reportFile, walkFile, report, walkReport, ctx, close }`: one surface's Solve report, final walk and triage ctx; `measuredReach` replaces the canvas-confirm.json lookup.
- `runTriage({ client, surface, report?, out? })` → `{ verdicts, counts, file }`.
- `surfaceBlocks(buildDir, entry)` → every block in one surface's own tree as `[ { ref, name } ]`: the sibling roster R-47-12 (c) checks for a block that can hold the row.

### `lib/skeleton-inventory.mjs` (Playwright by path; imports `scripts/parity/lib/collect.mjs`)
- `INVENTORY_WIDTHS`: 1440, 375, 768.
- `readInventory({ url, root, shotsDir, log, page, settle, strips })` → `{ generated, source, root, keyFormat, checks, elements, beside? }`; with `strips` (`{ exclude: [ [ component, tpl ] ] }`, a header surface's: the roots of the other surfaces that are not global, which a sibling may not be or hold at any depth) it also reads the root's siblings: `beside` lists each stamped sibling (`key`, `side`, `distance`, computed `position`, `renders`, `holdsOtherRoot`) and the elements of the in-flow, rendering siblings next to the root, up to the first holding another surface's root, are in `elements` under a root with a `strip` (`{ side, distance }`), no `parentKey` and depth 1 (`lib/skeleton-header-slots.mjs` decides which are rows); with `page` (a prepared, navigated page) and `settle(page, width)` it reads that page at each width without launching a browser or navigating (intake runs it inside the sandbox and harness), and without them it behaves as before; an element is `{ key, parentKey, depth, tag, template, tpl, attrs, words, allText, membership, srcTag, srcOwnText, snippet, box, visible, display, position (per width), flexDirection, layoutChanged375, screenshots }`.
- `withWidthOnly`: The 1440 records plus the elements that render only at another width (an sc-if on a width, a branch shown only on a phone or a tablet): each is inserted in document order after the record that precedes it in its own width's read, so a width alternative sits beside its sibling branch. An element read at 1440 keeps its place and its record unchanged.

### `lib/skeleton-model.mjs`
- `inventoryModel(inventory)` → `{ els, byKey, kids, isPart, blockKids, words, textWithBreaks, placeholders, box, stacked, isTextLink, isIconLink, childIndex, fingerprint, root, tplFinder }`.

### `lib/skeleton-facts.mjs` (reads the framework database read-only, `brand-registry.json`, `block.json`)
- `REPO`, `DECISIONS_FILE`: the repository root and `data/skeleton-decisions.json`.
- `rxList(spec)` → the regular expressions of a `{ patterns, flags }` spec; `specHit(spec, text)` → the spec when any pattern matches, else null.
- `loadFacts({ db, client, repo?, siteName?, decisionsFile? })` → the facts object (tag map, composition, capabilities, attributes, row and logo blocks, the list block and its item properties, display types, the placeholder map, the brand registry, and `replacedBy`: each core block's SGS replacement from `blocks.replaces`).
- `decisionsProblems(facts)` → readable problems in the decisions data (an unknown block, a core block with its replacement, a pattern that does not compile, no list block).

### `lib/skeleton-classify.mjs`
- `W`: the evidence weights. `finish(candidates)` → ranked candidates with shares and `confidence = min(1, top) * top / (top + second)`.
- `overrideFor(element, { clientDecisions, picks, facts, surface, words })` → the decision that settles an element (a client decision kept only while its signature holds, a finaliser pick, a standing element decision), `{ stale }` for a decision that no longer fits, else null.
- `sgsOnly(result, replacedBy, exceptions?)` → the result with every core candidate rewritten to its SGS replacement (the rewrite as evidence) or dropped into `rejectedCore` when none exists, unless `exceptions` (`rules.coreExceptions`, each `{ block, reason }`) names it; SGS bans core blocks.
- `makeClassifier({ m, facts, overrides, surface?, blockOfKey? })` → `{ classify(element, parentBlock, ctx), brandFor, siteInfoKinds, isColumnLabel, briefOnly, drawsChildren }`; `classify` runs the standing rules after a client decision and before a pick or the scoring, and passes every result through `sgsOnly`.

### `lib/skeleton-rules.mjs`
- `declared(element)` → its inline style as `{ property: value }`.
- `isSiteInfoValue(text, kind)` → whether words are the Site Info value of a kind itself (a typed phone number or email address); a rule's verdict names the attributes it set from that test as `wordBound` (`{ attr: kind }`), and `lib/skeleton-propose.mjs` keeps each on a width variant only when the variant's own words pass it too.
- `isMarker(element, m, D, view?)` → whether the element is a small marker that holds nothing, in the flow of its text: a dot or a short bar within the sizes of `D` (`rules.dotMarker`); `lib/skeleton-propose.mjs` reads it to take stacked rows of a marker and one piece of text as one list.
- `makeRules({ m, facts, surface: { slug, kind, name }, hooks: { siteInfoKinds, isColumnLabel, topBlock, blockOfKey }, motion?, view?, scope? })` → `{ ruleFor(element, parentBlock), drawsChildren(block), declared, iconRun, siteInfoBrand }` (`view` is `lib/skeleton-record.mjs::recordView`, `scope` its `surfaceContext`). `ruleFor` returns the first verdict in precedence order (`{ rule, reason, action?: 'remove'|'unwrap'|'absorbed', into?, block?, attributes?, absorb?, handover? }`) or null; a rule with no entry in `rules` never fires. `drawsChildren` gives why a block draws an element's children (a leaf, an `array_item_schema` block, or `rules.drawsChildren`), else null.

### `lib/skeleton-header-slots.mjs`
- `OUT_OF_FLOW`: the declared positions (fixed, absolute) that take an element out of the flow, so it is never a strip.
- `besideStrips(inventory, { declared?, sameParent? })` → `{ before: [ element ], after: [ element ], skipped: [ { key, side, why } ] }`: the strips beside the header root, nearest first on each side, from `inventory.beside` (an out-of-flow sibling, by computed or declared position, and one that does not render are passed over; one that is or holds another surface's root ends the walk; one that does not share the root's template parent, or is not stacked above or below the header root, is skipped).
- `stackedOn(el, side, refAt)` → whether the element lies wholly above (`top`) or below (`bottom`) the reference box at every width where both render, and at one width at least.
- `insideStrips(kids, { holdsMain, declared? })` → `{ top, bottom, main }`: the direct children of the header root; every child holding navigation or logo is the main bar, a child before the first (after the last) is a top (bottom) strip when it renders at some width, is in flow (declared or computed position, at the widths it renders) and is stacked above (below) the bar; a child inline with the bar is part of it; nearest the main bar first.
- `slotPlan({ beside, inside })` → `{ top, bottom, extras: [ { el, side, taken } ] }`: the nearest strip on each side is the row; every other strip on that side is an extra, named in a proposal problem and never built.

### `lib/skeleton-footer-slots.mjs`
- `isFloating(el, declared?)` → whether the element is declared or computed fixed or absolute at a width it renders at.
- `sideBySide(list)` → the elements of the list that sit beside another at desktop (disjoint horizontal extents, shared vertical extent).
- `footerPlan(kids, { subtree, kidsOf, declared? })` → `{ mode: 'grid' | 'band' | 'none', top, columns, bottom, floating }`: the footer root's children split into the three slots; with no grid (`none`) every in-flow child is in `columns`.
- `buildFooterRows(e, block, ctx)` → the three row nodes for the footer root, built with the writer's helpers `skeleton-propose.mjs` passes in.

### `lib/skeleton-rerun.mjs`
- `loopingKeys(animations)` → the Set of template keys (`<component>/<tpl>`) whose own animation in `intake.json`'s `inventory.animations.records` loops a translate for ever (a marquee track, by its mechanism): a record names its element by `template_tpl_id`; only a record with no `template_tpl_id` (a strip built in code and bound through a hole) is named by the `nearest_tpl_id` of the element holding that hole, and a record counts only when the record is a translate transform with an infinite iteration; `rerunSkeletons` and `refreshIntakeSkeletons` pass it to the rules as `motion`, and `runIntake` ends with the same refresh so a fresh run and `--rerun-skeletons` agree.
- `refreshIntakeSkeletons({ intake, client, db, repo? })` → `{ intake, missing, before, after }`: `intake.json` with its skeletons proposed again and collapsed as the intake run collapses them (a surface with no saved inventory keeps its rows).
- `rerunSkeletons({ client, db, repo?, skeletons, motion?, picks?, useBuilt?, breakpoints?, propose? })` → `{ surfaces: { <slug>: { kind, name, built, rows, counts, problems, skeleton, entry, fingerprints } }, missing, absorbed }` (a header surface is proposed first; a global surface whose root is a strip the header read beside itself is `absorbed: { <slug>: { into, root } }` and has no surface of its own) from the saved inventories, each checked by `assertCurrent` against `sites/<client>/handoff`; `skeletons` is `intake.json`'s, for each surface's id, kind and name; `useBuilt: false` proposes even a surface the client built; `breakpoints` is `intake.json`'s (the saved file's by default) and gives the thresholds; `propose` replaces `proposeSkeleton`. Throws when the decisions data is not sound.

### `check-approved.mjs`
- `tallyApprovals(approved, { rows, fingerprints, review })` → every approval of `approved-decisions.json` category3 measured against the rows proposed now; an approval whose row ID a review row names in its `realignedFrom` (`lib/intake-review-carry.mjs::earlierIds`) is measured against the row that element has now.

### `lib/skeleton-context.mjs`
- `calKeysFor(site)` and `calSettingsFor(site)` → functions of a block slug returning the calibration cache's element keys and settings; `statesOfRecord({ repo, client, id })` → the handoff record's states; `thresholdsOf(breakpoints)` → the distinct numeric thresholds; `siteOf({ repo, client, slug })` → the site `build/surfaces.json` records for the manifest entry naming that intake skeleton (null when none); `proposalContext({ repo, client, slug, id, breakpoints, site? })` → `{ calKeys, calSettings, states, thresholds, formLinks, refs, draftLinks }` (`draftLinks`: `lib/draft-link.mjs::draftLinksFor`).

### `lib/skeleton-record.mjs`
- `recordFor(facts)` → the client's fact record (`lib/fact-record.mjs::loadFacts`, cached per file state) or null when it has none; a stale or invalid record throws. `openersFor(facts)` → surface id to its `surfaces.json` `opened_by` words.
- `recordView(record, { openers? })` → `{ has, entry, attr, realTag, declared, holeProps, ownPaint, describe, childrenOf, repeated, linkRun, parentsOf, checkParent, disagreements, ... }`; an element with no entry reads its rendered attributes. `describe(e)` returns every field the rules read.
- `surfaceContext(view, { kind, name })` → the surface's id, parent, opener, `headerDisclosure` and `surfaceParts` (the records whose parent is the surface), or null.
- `gridTracks(value)`, `fractionalTrack(track)`, `fixedTrack(track)` → a `grid-template-columns` value as tracks, and whether a track shares the free space or has a fixed length.

### `lib/skeleton-narrowing.mjs`
- `narrowedLists({ folder, record, component, listKey })` → the `renderVals` keys (other than `listKey`) whose lists the chips looped from `listKey` narrow, for the component's `<component>.dc.html` in `folder` (the draft folder, `sites/<client>/handoff`); `record.sources.files` holds the draft hashes. An empty list when the record hashes no such file; a hashed file that is missing or differs throws the freshness module's `StaleRecordError`.
- `draftLogicOf({ folder, record, component })` → the component's draft logic as the record hashed it (`{ idx, method, values }`, `values` each `renderVals` key to its value node), or null when the record names no such file or `renderVals` returns no object.
- `narrowedListsOfDraft(draft, listKey)` → `narrowedLists` for a draft `loadDraft` read (no fact record), its logic read once per draft object.
- `narrowsRenderedList(narrowed, rendered)` → the standing filter rule in one place: whether any narrowed `renderVals` key is a list the same component renders (`rendered` holds the `list` attribute of each of its `sc-for` elements). The skeleton stage (`optionSetFilter`) and the page map's choice-flow detection (`isChoiceFlow`) both ask it.

### `lib/skeleton-handler-opens.mjs`
- `handlerOpens({ folder, record, component, handler })` → the Set of state keys the `renderVals` handler writes with an opening value (empty when the record names no draft file or the handler is not a `renderVals` key).
- `opensOn(surface, keys)` → whether one of the fact-record surface's states holds one of `keys` at true.

### `lib/skeleton-panel-rules.mjs`
- `makePanelRules({ m, view, scope, R, facts, surface, declared })` → `{ panelChild(element), iconRun(list, i, parent), siteInfoBrand(element) }`: the verdict for a direct child of a header disclosure panel, the run of icon-only Site Info links at `list[i]` in a drawer's top row, and the brand a link stands for.

### `lib/skeleton-propose.mjs`
- `COVERAGE_WRITES`: the coverage IDs whose register entry names this module as its writer, listed one per line and sorted; `coverageProblems` refuses a declared ID whose entry names another module and an entry naming this module that is not listed.
- `proposeSkeleton({ inventory, facts, surface, surfaceKind?, surfaceName?, built?, clientDecisions?, carried?, picks?, calKeys?, calSettings?, states?, thresholds?, db?, factRecord?, openers?, pageMap?, surfaceId? })` → `{ skeleton, proposal, problems }` (the proposal also carries `surfaceParts` and `recordDisagreements`); the skeleton passes `skeletonProblems` and `lintSkeleton`. Each row carries `rule` (the standing rule that settled it, or null) and, for an absorbed row, `into`; stacked text links, or stacked rows of a marker (`isMarker`) and one piece of text, are one list; a bare wrapper holding exactly one list is unwrapped into it (`listWrapper`); with `built` (`sites/<client>/build/skeleton/built-surfaces.json`) every row is settled by that post (`clientBuiltSurface`). `proposal.counts.byRule` counts rows per rule.

### `lib/skeleton-write-record.mjs`
- `makeRecorder( ctx )` → `{ record, removeSubtree, absorbSubtree, absorbWidthKids, takeVariants }`: `record( element, verdict, extra? )` adds the element's proposal row; `removeSubtree` removes the subtree inside a removed element; `absorbSubtree` and `absorbWidthKids` absorb what a block draws, turning an element shown only at some widths into one block instance per variation (FR-48-50) or listing it as a width alternative; `takeVariants( node )` hands over a node's variant instances. `ctx` is the shared context `proposeSkeleton` builds.

### `lib/skeleton-write-units.mjs`
- `LOGO_HANDOVER`: the handover entry of a logo block, whose image setting a skeleton cannot hold.
- `makeUnits( ctx )` → `{ unitsOf, removedBy, buildBrand, buildIcons, buildList }`: `unitsOf( element, list? )` splits the children into `brand`, `icons`, `list` and `single` units; the builders make the node and rows of one unit.

### `lib/skeleton-write-content.mjs`
- `skeletonSettings(facts, block)` → `{ text, link, others }`: the settings the skeleton writer can give a block (its first text-content and link-href settings, a wrapper shell's tag and layout, Site Info's display type, a link target, a logo's `logoId`, a menu `ref`); `contentAttrs` writes through it.
- `makeContent( ctx )` → `{ contentAttrs, carriedAttrs }`: `contentAttrs( block, element, top )` → `{ a, handover }`, the block's settings from the draft element and the notes for what no setting holds; `carriedAttrs( block, element )` → `{ attrs, said, missing }`, the ids the build holds for the block.
- `PREFIX_WORDS`: the most words a copyright prefix may hold. `copyrightPrefixParts(text, siteName)` (`{ prefix, leftover }`) and `copyrightPrefixOf(text, siteName)` (its prefix) → the words a draft's copyright line prints before its mark (`Copyright` for "Copyright (c) 2026 Acme", empty for "(c) 2026 Acme"), a short run of words that never holds the site name.

### `lib/skeleton-write-slots.mjs`
- `makeSlotRows( ctx )` → `{ buildHeaderRows, buildFooterRows, hostSlot }`: the header's top, middle and bottom rows, the footer's three rows, and the draft element a wrapper's hostSetting names as the slot of the block it unwraps into; the strips beside the header land in `ctx.stripRoots`.

### `lib/skeleton-part-slots.mjs`
- `partSlots({ db, block, cal, part, opener, m, finder })` → `{ slots, notes }`: `slots` maps the part's calibrated slot to the opener's finder and each calibrated sub-part path under it to the draft child it is (a `:nth-of-type(k)` step is the k-th child, a lone step the only child); `notes` names a part with no calibrated slot and a sub-part the draft has no element for.
- `placePart({ e, node, tree, nodeOfKey, m })` → where the absorbing block stands: `{ placed: <wrapper key> }`, `{ dropped: <wrapper key> }` or `{ note }` when it stays where it is, so the block stands in the part's draft wrapper when that changes nothing at its own widths.

### `lib/skeleton-child-slots.mjs`
- `childSlots({ cfg, items, m, finder, settings?, states? })` → `{ slots, notes }`: the `draftSlots` of a block that draws its children (`rules.drawsChildren` entry `childSlots`: the calibration settings measured on a resting and on the current child, and `glyph`: the setting measured on the glyph a child draws beside its label, read from the draft child's own small square wordless element). The slot path is the setting's own `slot` in the block's calibration; the resting child is the first child no current-state `sc-if` renders in, the current child the one a current-state `sc-if` (a handoff state whose name says the calibrated state) renders in. `collapsePoint({ e, states?, thresholds? })` → `{ value }` or `{ note }`: the one `>= N` width threshold, N a threshold the draft's code uses, of the handoff states whose `sc-if` shows the navigation element (`rules.navigation.collapseAttribute` names the block's attribute).

### `lib/skeleton-review.mjs`
- `decisionCell(key, surface, clientDecisions, picks)` → the recorded decision for a key, or null. `reviewHtml({ inventory, proposal, surface, clientDecisions, picks })` → the review page.

### `lib/skeleton-finalise.mjs`
- `skeletonDir(repo, client)`; `signatureOf(element)` → `tag|words`.
- `readDecisions(file)`, `recordDecision({ file, surface, element, choice, reason? })`, `recordSiteName({ file, siteName })`, `readPicks(file)`.
- `finaliseRequest({ inventory, proposal, surface, promptFile })` → the low-confidence rows with their elements and evidence; `applyPicks({ picks, request, knownBlock, file })` → the merged picks, refusing a key outside the request or an unknown block.

### `skeleton.mjs` (Playwright by path)
- `inventoryStrips({ client, surface, repo? })` → the `strips` option of `readInventory` for a surface whose intake skeleton is a header (the handoff `surfaces.json` records that are not global, and whether it has page surfaces), else null; `inventory` passes it, so a header's `<s>.inventory.json` holds the strips beside its root.
- `skeletonPaths({ client, surface, repo? })` → the run's files and draft url. `proposeSurface({ client, surface, repo?, siteName?, db? })` → propose from the files on disk and write the skeleton and proposal, with the surface kind, name, built post and looping templates of its intake skeleton and, for a served inventory (`Root/…` keys), the intake-space decision and motion keys rewritten to the served root. `intakeContext({ client, surface, repo? })` → `{ slug, kind, name, built, rootComponent, motion }` from the `intakeSurface` the surfaces.json entry names; it throws naming the missing link when there is none. `surfaceDecisions({ client, surface, repo?, inventory, file })` → `{ ctx, saved, served, clientDecisions }`, the intake context and the decisions as propose and review read them (under the intake slug, root-host keys in the served space for a served inventory). `reviewSurface({ client, surface, repo? })` → the review table's html. `decide` refuses an entry without an `intakeSurface`. `main(argv)` → the command line.

### `check-refs.mjs` (Playwright by path)
- `refStatus(entries)` → each entry with `status` (`ok`, `missing`, `ambiguous`, `outside`) and a reason. `skeletonTargets(skeleton)` → every `draftRef` and `draftSlots` target, Site Info row icons included. `checkRefs({ skeleton, draftUrl, opener? })` → `{ targets, summary, opened }` (`opened`: `lib/fill-open.mjs::openState`'s verdict at 1440 when an opener is given, else null).

### `lib/handoff.mjs` (Ajv 8 from `plugins/sgs-blocks/node_modules/ajv-formats`)
- `REPO`, `MASTER_SCHEMAS`, `KNOWN_RUNTIMES`: the repository root, `.claude/claude-design-handoff/handoff-schema/` and `data/known-runtimes.json`.
- `manifestSchemaVersion(schemas?)` → the `schema_version` const `manifest.schema.json` demands (throws when it names none); the gate chain's G0 expects it.
- `REQUIRED_FILES`: FR-48A-2's list (a root `.dc.html` is checked separately). `MAX_BYTES`: 20 MB (FR-48A-10).
- `sha256(buf)` → hex digest. `readJson(file)` → `{ data }` or `{ error }`. `readKnown(file?)` → `{ runtimes }` (empty when the file is missing).
- `walkFolder(root)` → `{ files, problems }`: every file relative with `/`, sorted; a symlink is a problem and is not followed.
- `resolveRef(value, fromDraft?)` → a folder-relative path (query and fragment removed, URL-decoded, leading `./` removed), or null for a templated value, an absolute URL, a bare fragment or a path leaving the folder.
- `draftRefs(text, fromDraft?)` → `{ drafts, scripts }` one draft names: `dc-import` names as `X.dc.html`, `href`/`src` values ending in `.dc.html`, `<script src>` and `x-import from=` values.
- `reachable(root, entries, files)` → `{ drafts, scripts }` reachable from the entry drafts (scripts: `.js` files on disk).
- `loadAjv()` → an Ajv 2020 instance (`strict: false`, `allErrors: true`, with ajv-formats), from the route's own pinned install.
- `schemaProblems(ajv, schemaFile, data)` → readable problems, at most 20 and a remainder line.
- `buildManifest(root, { project, entryDrafts, generated? })` → a MANIFEST.json object for the files now in `root`.
- `blockMappingHeadings(markdown)` → level 1 to 3 headings naming SGS or block mapping.
- `checkHandoff(root, { known?, schemas?, ajv? })` → `{ errors, warnings, sha }`, each line `FR-48A-<n> <file>: <reason>`.

### `lib/svg-screen.mjs` (saxes from the route's own pinned install)
- `saxesParser()` → the saxes `SaxesParser` class, from the route's own pinned install.
- `svgProblem(text)` → why an SVG is unsafe to run, or null.

### `lib/runtime-probe.mjs` (Playwright by path, serves through `lib/draft.mjs`)
- `PROBE_FIXTURE`, `PROBE_EXPECTED`, `RUNTIMES_DIR`: `data/runtime-probe.dc.html`, `data/runtime-probe.expected.json`, `data/runtimes/`. `runtimeCopyName(sha)` → `<first 12 hex>.support.js`.
- `probeRuntime(supportFile, { fixture?, timeout? })` → `{ rootName, live, annotated }`; copies the fixture and build into a temp folder and removes it after.
- `compareProbe(result, expected)` → one line per differing list (first differing index, both lengths); empty on a pass.
- `splitModules(src)` → `Map(module → text)` on the `// src/<name>.ts` markers. `moduleDiff(a, b)` → `[{ module, status, from, to }]`.
- `nearestKnown(src, known, { exclude? })` → `{ runtime, diff }` for the known build with a local copy that differs least, or null.
- `addRuntime(supportFile, knownFile, { source, added? })` → the entry, after copying the build into `data/runtimes/` and appending it with `probe: "pass"` (an already listed build is returned unchanged).

### `handoff-check.mjs`
- `parseArgs(argv)` → `{ folder, known, schemas, source, json, probe, add }` or `{ error }`. `main(argv, log?)` → the exit code.

### `intake.mjs`
- Runs `lib/intake-run.mjs` with the process arguments; no exports.

### `lib/intake-run.mjs`
- `REPO`, `INTAKE_SCHEMA`, `USAGE`; `parseArgs(argv)` → the options or `{ error }`; `pathsFor(repo, client)` → every input and output path.
- `buildRecord(...)` → the intake record from the stage results; `sectionCounts(record)`; `recordProblems(record, ajv)` (intake schema and page-map checks).
- `runIntake(options)` → the exit code and summary of a run; `confirmedPageMap(intake)` → the page map with `drop` and `edit:` marks applied; `applyIntakeReview(options)` → the exit code of `--apply-review`.

### `lib/intake-run-worker.mjs`
- No exports: the worker module `runIsolated` starts, which checks it holds no secret and runs the browser stages (side by side by `STAGE_NEEDS` when `INTAKE_POOL` is above 1, applied in `STAGES` order; one after another at 1).

### `lib/intake-run-stages.mjs`
- `routeVisits(routing, entry)` → a visit per route; `STAGES`: the browser stages in FR-48-17's order, each `{ name, run(ctx) }`; `STAGE_NEEDS`: per stage, the earlier stages whose context fields it reads (`'*'`: every earlier stage applied); `SLOT_STAGES`: the stages that open a browser outside the pool and hold one slot when stages run side by side; the `inventory` stage (after the draft defects, whose `notDefects` it reads from the context) returns `{ inventory: { animations, surfaces, diff, occurrences, propVariants, samples } }` and leaves the widened census's sources in `ctx.widenedSources`; the `facts` stage (after the skeletons, which save the inventories it takes instance keys from) writes `sites/<client>/build/intake/facts.json` from those sources (`lib/fact-record.mjs`) and returns `{ facts: counts }`.

### `lib/intake-run-defects.mjs`
- `twoPassDefects(ctx)` → the draft-defect result from a three-visit pass and a pass over every route for its remaining candidates; `defectReviewRows(result)` → one review row per defect group.

### `lib/intake-run-finalise.mjs`
- `FINALISER_PROMPT`, `LOW_BELOW` (0.7, the confidence below which a row goes to the finaliser); `needsFinaliser(row)`; `intakeFinaliseRequest(intake, { promptFile })` → the request per surface; each row carries `parent`, `siblings`, `siblingCount` and `boxes` copied from the skeleton row (null on a record written before they existed).
- `picksProblems(picks, request, intake, knownBlock)` and `dbKnownBlock()` → a checker for block slugs in the framework DB (read-only); `applyIntakePicks(intake, picks)` → the intake with picks applied.
- `reviewDecisions(intake, today)` → the confirmed choices to save; `decisionsFile(repo, client)`; `recordReviewDecisions(decisions, file)`.
- `runFinalise(at, options, { log })`, `runApplyPicks(at, options, { log, recordProblems, knownBlock })`, `runRender(at, options, { log })`, `runRerunSkeletons(at, options, { log, recordProblems })` → exit codes for the four flags. `runRerunSkeletons` keeps every other part of `intake.json`, rewrites `finalise-request.json`, carries the picks `picksProblems` still accepts and keeps the previous record as `intake.prev.json`.

### `lib/intake-run-collapse.mjs`
- `templateOf(row, census)`, `collapseSkeletonRows(rows, census)`, `collapseSkeletons(skeletons, census)`, `collapseFlags(rows)`, `collapseMissing(rows)`: one row per repeated construct, every original key kept inside.
- `paletteUsage(records)` → the palette's colour usage from rest-mode readings; `slimCensus(census)` → the census rows the record keeps; `mergeBlocked(lists)` → one blocked-request row per URL and reason from every stage's sandboxes, with every draft ID seen.

### `stage0.mjs`
- `parseArgs(argv)` → `{ client, site, plan, recalibrate, allowLoss }` or `{ error }` (`--allow-loss` is passed to `calibrate.mjs`). Runs `lib/stage0.mjs::runStage0` with the real handoff check, framework DB, deploy hashes and `calibrate.mjs`.

### `lib/stage0.mjs`
- `BASELINE_SCHEMA`: path of `data/calibration-baseline.schema.json`. `FIXED_BLOCKS`: the layout and content blocks every surface uses (the spec's fixed rule).
- `surfaceKinds(surfaces)` → the sorted distinct `kind` values of a `surfaces.json`.
- `blocksForKinds(kinds, rows)` → `{ byKind, unmapped }`: `block_composition` holds no kind column, so a kind maps to the `sgs/` blocks whose slug is the kind or ends in `-<kind>`; a kind with none is `unmapped`, never guessed. `blockSetFor(kinds, rows)` → `{ blocks, byKind, unmapped }` with `FIXED_BLOCKS` first.
- `blockState({ cacheDir, block, hashes, fixtures, refsWhy })` → `{ block, state, deployHash, cachedKey, why }`; `state` is `current`, `stale`, `missing`, `undeployed`, `no-fixture`, `needs-refs` (the fixture names a reference the target does not supply or the site does not hold; `refsWhy` says which) or `no-build`.
- `paintSnapshot(cacheDir)` → every `.paint.json` with size and mtime; `paintChanges(before, after)` → the paint files added or changed.
- `buildBaseline({ client, site, takenOn, playwright, cacheDir, blocks, hashes })` → the Appendix C object; `baselineProblems(baseline, ajv?)` → readable problems (empty when valid and a browser is named).
- `calibrateArgs({ site, client, blocks, recalibrate, allowLoss? })` → the `calibrate.mjs` argument list, always with `--slot-map-only`, plus `--allow-loss` when `allowLoss` is non-empty. `installedPlaywright()` → the installed Playwright version.
- `runStage0(o, deps)` → `{ code, lines, baseline }`; `o` is `{ repo, client, site, cacheDir, plan, recalibrate }`, `deps` the injected effects (`refs` and `refsExist(roles)` among them).

### `lib/intake-sandbox.mjs`
- `PINNED_SCRIPTS` (the three unpkg runtime scripts with their SRI hashes), `FONT_HOSTS`, `STOCK_HOSTS`, `DROP_HEADERS` (response headers never replayed). `hostOf(url)` → the hostname or null.
- `fetchOrigin(url, init, { attempts = 5, delayMs = 100, fetchImpl })` → `{ status, headers, body }` (body a Buffer), the draft-origin response the route handler fulfils from, retried with a growing delay when the socket fails with `ENOBUFS` in the request or the body read (Windows runs out of socket buffer space with many browsers at once); any other failure, and the last `ENOBUFS`, is thrown unchanged.
- `classifyUrl(url, { origin, stockHosts })` → `origin`, `inline`, `pinned`, `recordable` or `unlisted`. `replayFilter(stockHosts)` → the RegExp of URLs the HAR serves. `replayPath(client, manifestHash)` → the replay file path.
- `recheckManifest(folder)` → `{ manifestHash, schemaVersion, files }`; throws naming a version other than "1.0", a size or hash mismatch, a missing or unlisted file, or a wrong `runtime_sha256`.
- `runtimePins(supportSrc)` → `{ url: sri }` from `support.js`; throws unless they are `PINNED_SCRIPTS`. `startTrapProxy()` → `{ server, attempts, close }`, a local proxy that refuses and logs every connection.
- `openSandbox({ folder, index, harPath, mode, widths, stockHosts, pinnedBodies, headless, timeout, resolveTimeout, args })` (`args`: extra Chromium flags; the forced draft-defect pages pass `--disable-gpu-vsync --disable-frame-rate-limit`, so a frame does not wait for the 60 Hz display tick) → `{ browser, context, origin, manifest, newPage, visit, requests, blocked, proxyAttempts, close }`; `mode` is `replay` (HAR only) or `discover`; `blocked` rows are `{ url, host, reason: unlisted-host|replay-miss|websocket, draftId, draftIdError? }`; each draftId is resolved on any page the context opens (newPage and visit alike) once the context is request-free, when a newPage page closes, in visit and in close; a row the request-free lookup finds no ID for is retried and settled only at a page close, visit or close; an unresolvable ID, or a lookup the page does not answer within `resolveTimeout` ms (default 5000), sets draftIdError.

### `lib/browser-close.mjs`
- `CLOSE_GRACE_MS` (3000); `browserPidOf(browser)` → the browser process id over its DevTools session, or null; `closeBounded({ close, kill, graceMs? })` → whether the browser had to be killed (`close` runs; when it has not finished within `graceMs`, `kill` runs and the close is awaited once more, bounded by `graceMs`); `closeBrowser(browser, pid)` → `closeBounded` over `browser.close()` and `process.kill(pid)`. `openSandbox`'s `close()` uses it, so a draft page stuck in a busy script never holds a close.

### `lib/intake-record.mjs`
- `fetchPinned({ integrity })` → `{ url: Buffer }`, the pinned scripts fetched in Node and SRI-checked. `assertIntegrity(url, body, sri)` throws on a mismatch.
- `discoverRequests({ folder, index, widths, visits, stockHosts, harPath })` → `{ urls, blocked, userAgent, manifestHash }`, pass 1 with the network closed.
- `recordReplay({ urls, harPath, userAgent, stockHosts, integrity })` → `{ harPath, added, entries, bytes }`; refuses a URL that is not recordable. `recordExport({ folder, index, harPath, widths, visits, stockHosts, rounds })` → `{ harPath, urls, blocked, rounds }`, discovery and recording repeated until no new URL appears.

### `tools/compare-intake.mjs`
- `EXCLUDED`: the fields left out. `normaliseIntake(record)` → the record without them, serialised as intake.mjs writes it; `normaliseReview(md, takenOn)`; `firstDifference(a, b)` → the first differing JSON path or null; `compareIntake([ a, b, reviewA?, reviewB? ])` → `{ same, lines }`.

### `lib/intake-pool.mjs`
- `poolSize(env?)` → `INTAKE_POOL` as a positive integer (default 4; anything else throws).
- Test 2's three widths run side by side by default (`INTAKE_PARALLEL_WIDTHS=0` runs them one at a time, as the reference run `INTAKE_POOL=1 INTAKE_SKIP_QUIET=0 INTAKE_PARALLEL_WIDTHS=0` does) and `INTAKE_PARALLEL_VISITS=<n>` runs each page group's visits in n slices side by side (off by default; `lib/intake-defects.mjs::unreachedDefects`, which returns `units`, the number it ran). Each unit has its own copy of the work list, merged in the order a one-at-a-time run visits; if the copies that read a row's state disagree, everything runs one at a time. The earlier difference on Indus (six mega-menu constructs reached only side by side) was the chain's leaked drawer timer (`lib/intake-defects-chain.mjs`), now fixed. Use them with `INTAKE_POOL=4` (more browsers at once hit Windows `ENOBUFS` socket errors when other sessions are busy). On Indus (2026-10-19) side-by-side widths gave `draftDefects`, `notDefects`, census and inventory identical to the one-at-a-time run and pass B 2.2 times faster with both runs sharing the machine (Spec 48 FR-48-28). Until 2026-10-20 `INTAKE_PARALLEL_VISITS` never reached the defect pass: the intake worker runs with `lib/intake-process.mjs::ENV_ALLOW`, which did not list it, so every run that set it ran the visits one at a time (`tests/intake-sandbox.test.mjs` now refuses an `INTAKE_*` variable the code reads that the list drops). Measured with the switch reaching the worker (Indus, 2026-10-20, widths side by side in both runs): `INTAKE_PARALLEL_VISITS=3` took pass A 621 s against 401 s one at a time, and pass B had not finished after about 105 minutes against 41 (stopped), so side-by-side visits stay off by default; a pass whose copies disagree runs everything again one at a time, and its log line now says so (`lib/intake-run-defects.mjs`).
